The page's `<title>` and `lang` are placeholders in `client/index.html` filled from the catalogue by a `transformIndexHtml` plugin in `client/vite.config.ts`, which the development server also runs. `client/src/ui/messages.test.tsx` enforces the rule twice: a static scan with the TypeScript compiler API of every shipped client source (`client/src/ui/testing/uiTextScan.ts`) fails on JSX text, a literal child expression (including either branch of a conditional, `&&`, `||`, `??`, `+` and templates), a literal in a text-bearing attribute or prop (`aria-label`, `aria-description`, `aria-roledescription`, `aria-valuetext`, `aria-placeholder`, `alt`, `title`, `placeholder`, `label`, `error`), a literal assigned to `title`, `textContent`, `innerText`, `innerHTML` or `outerHTML`, and a literal given to `alert`, `confirm` or `prompt`, and on a title or body text written into `index.html`; and a runtime check renders both views and both fallbacks in jsdom and requires every text node and text-bearing attribute to match a catalogue text, placeholders matching any value. The scanner is tested against a planted literal of each kind. The same test file forbids "D&D" and "Dungeons & Dragons" in the catalogue (`01` §8). Whitespace-only literals, error messages thrown to the console and tests are not UI text.
Why: `08` §6 asks for every UI string in one catalogue so that a translation needs no code change; a JSON file is data, and a new language is another file with the same keys. A static scan catches literals in states no test renders; the runtime check catches text that reaches the screen by a path the scan does not model, such as a literal held in a variable. Together they make the acceptance of FND-04 ("a test fails on a string literal rendered as UI text outside it") executable.
Alternatives: An i18n library such as i18next or FormatJS (rejected: plurals and locale negotiation have no MVP consumer and the dependency would outweigh the code; revisit when a second language arrives); the catalogue as a TypeScript object (rejected: a translation would be a code change); an ESLint rule such as `react/jsx-no-literals` (rejected: a new plugin, and it covers JSX text but not DOM assignments, dialogs or the HTML page; the acceptance names a Vitest test); only the runtime check (rejected: misses states that are not rendered, such as error fallbacks added later); a hard-coded `<title>` compared with the catalogue by a test (rejected: still a second place the text is written).
Affected specs: `08` §6, `01` §8.

## D-071 (2026-09-24) — Bundled font and icons
Type: implementation
Decision: Both views render with Inter Variable, from the npm package `@fontsource-variable/inter` (SIL Open Font License 1.1), imported by `client/src/ui/tokens.css` so that Vite copies its woff2 files into `client/dist/assets` and the server serves them from its own origin; the font stack falls back to the system UI font. Icons are SVG files or inline SVG kept in the repository and bundled by Vite; the only one today is the favicon `client/public/favicon.svg`, an original mark, declared in `client/index.html` so the browser has no reason to ask for `/favicon.ico`. No icon library is added until a view needs an icon. `e2e/tests/views.spec.ts` shows that both views request only the local origin, that the font files are among those requests and answer 200, that the page renders with the bundled face, and that the declared icon is local and served as SVG.
Why: `02` §6 requires fonts and icons bundled so both views work offline. A bundled font makes the text look the same on the owner's TV browser, whose fonts are unknown (G-002), as on the DM's laptop. The OFL permits bundling and redistribution with AGPL software. An icon library with no icon to draw would be a dependency without a consumer (`14` §3).
Alternatives: The system font stack only (rejected: nothing to bundle, but the TV's fonts are unknown and text would look different on each screen); a font from a CDN such as Google Fonts (rejected: forbidden by `02` §6); copying font files into the repository (rejected: the npm package carries the licence and updates with the lockfile); an icon set such as Lucide now (rejected: no consumer yet; when one arrives it must be bundled the same way, which is tree-shaken SVG components).
Affected specs: `02` §6, `10` §6.

## D-072 (2026-09-24) — Keyboard, focus and contrast smoke tests, and jsdom for component tests
Type: implementation
Decision: `e2e/tests/keyboard.spec.ts` is the keyboard-operability gate of FND-04 and runs in `make e2e` against the production build. It is generic: it collects every element of `/dm` that sequential navigation can reach (links, enabled buttons and form fields, non-negative tabindex, contenteditable), fails if there is none or if any positive tabindex exists, and for each element reloads the page, presses Tab until the element is reached and asserts that it is focused, that its focused outline or shadow is visible and differs from its unfocused state, that it is in the viewport, and that Enter (and Space for a button) activates it; an in-page link must also move focus to its target. A second test computes the WCAG contrast of every element with its own text against its effective background in both views and requires 4.5:1; `client/src/ui/tokens.test.ts` checks the palette's text pairs (4.5:1) and the focus colour (3:1). Component tests in Vitest render into jsdom, selected per file with `// @vitest-environment jsdom`, through the small helper `client/src/ui/testing/render.ts` built on React's `act`; the client project's default environment stays `node`. The e2e workspace's TypeScript configuration gains the DOM library for code that runs inside the page.
Why: `08` §8 asks that core DM actions are keyboard-operable and text keeps readable contrast, and `13` §3 asks for the keyboard test to be a real gate. A test that walks whatever is on the page covers each control a later package adds without being edited, so the gate grows with the DM view instead of staying a check of today's single skip link. Deliberate mutations turned each test red: removing the focus outline, adding an external font import, and a muted text colour of 2:1.
Alternatives: A fixed list of elements to tab through (rejected: it would pass while new controls stay unreachable); an accessibility engine such as axe-core in Playwright (rejected for now: a dependency whose rules go beyond the no-formal-target decision of `08` §8, Q-030; the checks the spec asks for are written directly); Testing Library for component tests (rejected: `act` and the DOM are enough for the handful of tests, one dependency fewer); happy-dom instead of jsdom (rejected: jsdom is the more complete DOM for focus behaviour). Known limit: an element made clickable without being focusable, such as a `div` with a click handler, is invisible to the walk; native elements from the base components avoid it, and the UX review of PRP-01 checks for it.
Affected specs: `08` §8, `13` §3, `10` §1.

## D-073 (2026-09-24) — Message catalogue and the check that it is the only UI text (after review)
Type: implementation
Decision: Every UI string lives in one flat JSON catalogue per language, `client/src/ui/messages/en.json`, with dotted keys and `{name}` placeholders. `client/src/ui/messages.ts` exports `t(key, params)`, typed so that an unknown key fails `make typecheck`; the locale is fixed to `en`. The page's `<title>` and `lang` are placeholders in `client/index.html` filled from the catalogue by a `transformIndexHtml` plugin in `client/vite.config.ts`, which the development server also runs, and HTML-escapes what it inserts. `client/src/ui/messages.test.tsx` enforces the rule twice: a static scan with the TypeScript compiler API of every shipped client source (`client/src/ui/testing/uiTextScan.ts`) fails on JSX text, a literal child expression (including either branch of a conditional, `&&`, `||`, `??`, `+` and templates), a literal in a text-bearing attribute or prop (`aria-label`, `aria-description`, `aria-roledescription`, `aria-valuetext`, `aria-placeholder`, `alt`, `title`, `placeholder`, `label`, `error`, `children`), the `value` of an `input` of type submit, button or reset, the text props and literal children given to `createElement`, `jsx`, `jsxs` or `jsxDEV`, a literal assigned to `title`, `textContent`, `innerText`, `innerHTML` or `outerHTML`, and a literal given to `alert`, `confirm` or `prompt`, and on a title or body text written into `index.html`; and a runtime check renders both views and both fallbacks in jsdom and requires every text node and text-bearing attribute to match a catalogue text, placeholders matching any value. The scanner is tested against a planted literal of each kind. The same test file forbids "D&D" and "Dungeons & Dragons" in the catalogue (`01` §8). Whitespace-only literals, error messages thrown to the console and tests are not UI text. The same test requires every catalogue text to hold literal text besides its placeholders, because a text made only of placeholders would let the rendered-view check match anything. A literal held in a variable is seen only by the rendered-view check, so each package that adds a screen or state adds it to that check.
Why: `08` §6 asks for every UI string in one catalogue so that a translation needs no code change; a JSON file is data, and a new language is another file with the same keys. A static scan catches literals in states no test renders; the runtime check catches text that reaches the screen by a path the scan does not model, such as a literal held in a variable. Together they make the acceptance of FND-04 ("a test fails on a string literal rendered as UI text outside it") executable. The FND-04 review (2026-09-24) found that the scan missed a `children` prop, `createElement` arguments (already used by `client/src/views.ts`) and the label of a submit input, each of which reached the screen unflagged in a probe.
Alternatives: An i18n library such as i18next or FormatJS (rejected: plurals and locale negotiation have no MVP consumer and the dependency would outweigh the code; revisit when a second language arrives); the catalogue as a TypeScript object (rejected: a translation would be a code change); an ESLint rule such as `react/jsx-no-literals` (rejected: a new plugin, and it covers JSX text but not DOM assignments, dialogs or the HTML page; the acceptance names a Vitest test); only the runtime check (rejected: misses states that are not rendered, such as error fallbacks added later); a hard-coded `<title>` compared with the catalogue by a test (rejected: still a second place the text is written).; resolving variables to their literal values in the scan (rejected: needs the type checker's data flow for little gain over the rendered-view check).
Affected specs: `08` §6, `01` §8.

## D-074 (2026-09-24) — SQLite schema of the eight entities, its constraints and the fixture database
Status: superseded by D-075
Type: implementation
Decision: Migration `server/migrations/0001_initial_schema.sql` creates one table per entity of `03` §1, named in singular snake case (`image`, `asset`, `asset_tag`, `campaign`, `session`, `scene`, `token`, `settings`), with the specification's field names as columns, including a quoted `"order"`. A scene's grid is stored as `grid_type`, `grid_size`, `grid_offset_x`, `grid_offset_y`, `grid_visible`, `grid_feet_per_square`, `grid_columns` and `grid_rows`, and an image's preset as the same eight fields prefixed `grid_preset_`, wholly absent or wholly present and calibrated: the preset is the template a new scene copies (Q-001, answer A). Every table is `STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID`, keyed by a text `id`: a lowercase UUID checked by a GLOB pattern, and for Image the lowercase hex sha256. AssetTag and Settings carry a UUID `id` too, because `03` §3 names every entity but Image; AssetTag is also unique on (`asset_id`, `tag`). Foreign keys follow `03` §2 and the deletion rules of `03` §7: session, scene, token and asset tag cascade from their parent; an asset used by a token, and an image used by an asset or a scene, are restricted; `settings.live_scene_id` is set to NULL when the live scene or an ancestor is deleted. Every foreign key is indexed. Sessions and scenes are unique on (parent, `order`), so SRV-03 reorders in two steps (SQLite checks uniqueness row by row). `grid_size` is NULL until a square size is calibrated, which a map-less scene never has; the extent defaults to 30 x 20, feet per square to 5, offsets to 0 and player visibility to on. `session.date` is an optional `YYYY-MM-DD`. `image.variants` is a JSON object, empty until SRV-04 writes it. `asset.default_hidden` has no default, because D-020 derives it from the category. `token.character_id` must be NULL; the empty string is refused too, so that empty has one representation. Settings is one row, inserted by the migration under a version 4 UUID built with `randomblob`, and held at one row by triggers. Its defaults are PHB, 50 MiB (52,428,800 bytes) and 4,096 px, and `pin_hash` is NULL until SRV-02 sets it. The three fields prepared for later phases (`03` §8: `character_id` empty, `grid.type` and the preset type `square`, `rules_version` `5e-2014`) are held by BEFORE INSERT and BEFORE UPDATE triggers rather than CHECK constraints. Permanent invariants are CHECK constraints, each written so that a NULL cannot pass it. The runner (D-054) now turns foreign keys off while migrations run and runs `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` inside each migration's transaction, rolling back one that leaves a dangling reference; `openDatabase` turns them on for every other connection. The contract types are TypeBox schemas in `shared/src/entities.ts`, with the same field names, booleans for 0/1, the grid and the preset nested, and the PIN hash left out. The generated fixture database is `server/src/db/testing/fixture.ts`, excluded from the build: made-up rows in every table, written against schema version 1 (`FIXTURE_VERSION`, never edited afterwards). Every later migration is tested by migrating it from that version, keeping every row, reference and contract shape.
Why: Exact field names let a test read `03` §1 and compare it with the tables, so the schema cannot drift from the specification unnoticed. A rowid is an internal sequential key, which `14` §8 forbids (Q-084), and STRICT is the only way SQLite refuses text in a REAL column such as a token's `x` (`03` §4). Database constraints for key invariants are what `14` §8 asks for. The foreign-key actions make the cascades, the refusals and the blanking of a deleted live scene (Q-031) hold even if a later route forgets them. The input says `character_id` exists so that Phase 2 can link characters "without a painful migration". Removing a trigger takes one statement, while removing a CHECK in SQLite means rebuilding the table, so the prepared fields get triggers. SQLite's documented procedure for changing a table's schema requires foreign keys off. With them on, rebuilding `scene` would cascade-delete every token, and the check before commit replaces the enforcement that is switched off. A preset shaped like the grid needs no rule about which fields a copy takes. 50 MiB is the size Windows Explorer labels "50 MB". Keeping the PIN hash out of the shared contract means no type a client compiles against can carry it (`07` §1).
Alternatives: Plural or `Pascal` table names (rejected: SQLite convention is lower case, and singular matches the entity names); a `sort_order` or `position` column (rejected: renames a field the specification names); the grid and preset as JSON columns (rejected: no constraint could check a value inside them); rowid tables with a UUID column (rejected: a rowid is a sequential key); plain type affinity (rejected: SQLite would store `'2.5px'` in a REAL column); a composite key for AssetTag and an integer check for the one Settings row (rejected: `03` §3 gives every entity but Image a UUID); CHECK constraints for the three prepared fields (rejected: each Phase 2 or 3 change would rebuild a table); enforcing everything in application code only (rejected: `14` §8 prefers database constraints, and one forgotten route would break an invariant); no uniqueness on `order` (rejected: two scenes at one position would have no defined order); a non-null default square size such as 70 px (rejected: invents a calibration no one made); 50,000,000 bytes (rejected: shown to the DM as 47.7 MB by Windows); foreign keys left on during migrations (rejected: a table rebuild would cascade-delete children, shown by a test); an empty fixture or one generated at the latest version (rejected: it would not exercise the path a DM's existing data takes); the fixture as a committed `.db` file (rejected: generated data is reviewable as code, and Q-082 asks for a generated fixture).
Affected specs: `03` §1, `03` §2, `03` §3, `03` §4, `03` §6, `03` §7, `03` §8, `05` §6, `05` §7, `06` §2, `06` §5, `07` §1, `09` §2, `14` §8.

## D-075 (2026-09-24) — SQLite schema of the eight entities, its constraints and the fixture database (after review)
Type: implementation
Decision: Migration `server/migrations/0001_initial_schema.sql` creates one table per entity of `03` §1, named in singular snake case (`image`, `asset`, `asset_tag`, `campaign`, `session`, `scene`, `token`, `settings`), with the specification's field names as columns, including a quoted `"order"`. A scene's grid is stored as `grid_type`, `grid_size`, `grid_offset_x`, `grid_offset_y`, `grid_visible`, `grid_feet_per_square`, `grid_columns` and `grid_rows`, and an image's preset as the same eight fields prefixed `grid_preset_`, wholly absent or wholly present and calibrated: the preset is the template a new scene copies (Q-001, answer A). Every table is `STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID`, keyed by a text `id`: a lowercase UUID checked by a GLOB pattern, and for Image the lowercase hex sha256. AssetTag and Settings carry a UUID `id` too, because `03` §3 names every entity but Image; AssetTag is also unique on (`asset_id`, `tag`). Foreign keys follow `03` §2 and the deletion rules of `03` §7: session, scene, token and asset tag cascade from their parent; an asset used by a token, and an image used by an asset or a scene, are restricted; `settings.live_scene_id` is set to NULL when the live scene or an ancestor is deleted. Every foreign key is indexed. Sessions and scenes are unique on (parent, `order`), so SRV-03 reorders in two steps (SQLite checks uniqueness row by row). `grid_size` is NULL until a square size is calibrated, and a CHECK keeps it NULL on a scene without a map; the extent defaults to 30 x 20, feet per square to 5, offsets to 0 and player visibility to on. `session.date` is an optional `YYYY-MM-DD`. `image.variants` is a JSON object, empty until SRV-04 writes it. `asset.default_hidden` has no default, because D-020 derives it from the category. `token.character_id` must be NULL; the empty string is refused too, so that empty has one representation. Settings is one row, inserted by the migration under a version 4 UUID built with `randomblob`, and held at one row by triggers. Its defaults are PHB, 50 MiB (52,428,800 bytes) and 4,096 px, and `pin_hash` is NULL until SRV-02 sets it. The three fields prepared for later phases (`03` §8: `character_id` empty, `grid.type` and the preset type `square`, `rules_version` `5e-2014`) are held by BEFORE INSERT and BEFORE UPDATE triggers rather than CHECK constraints. Permanent invariants are CHECK constraints, each written so that a NULL cannot pass it; every REAL column refuses infinity (`abs(v) < 9e999`), which JSON cannot carry, and a NaN bound by a driver arrives as NULL. A trigger keeps `settings.id` from changing. The settings UUID's variant digit uses `random() & 3`, which cannot overflow. The runner (D-054) now turns foreign keys off while migrations run and runs `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` inside each migration's transaction, rolling back one that leaves a dangling reference. Each migration's transaction is `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` and re-reads `user_version` under that write lock, skipping a migration another process has applied meanwhile; the backup (`VACUUM INTO`, which cannot run inside a transaction) is taken before, so two processes may each write one. A `target` below the database's version is refused with its own message. `INSERT OR REPLACE` is never used on an entity table, because its implicit delete fires the cascades; `openDatabase` turns them on for every other connection. The contract types are TypeBox schemas in `shared/src/entities.ts`, with the same field names, booleans for 0/1, the grid and the preset nested, and the PIN hash left out. The generated fixture database is `server/src/db/testing/fixture.ts`, excluded from the build: made-up rows in every table, written against schema version 1 (`FIXTURE_VERSION`, never edited afterwards). Every later migration is tested by migrating it from that version, keeping every row, reference and contract shape; until a second migration exists, a test migrates it through an additive probe migration so the path is exercised now.
Why: Exact field names let a test read `03` §1 and compare it with the tables, so the schema cannot drift from the specification unnoticed. A rowid is an internal sequential key, which `14` §8 forbids (Q-084), and STRICT is the only way SQLite refuses text in a REAL column such as a token's `x` (`03` §4). Database constraints for key invariants are what `14` §8 asks for. The foreign-key actions make the cascades, the refusals and the blanking of a deleted live scene (Q-031) hold even if a later route forgets them. The input says `character_id` exists so that Phase 2 can link characters "without a painful migration". Removing a trigger takes one statement, while removing a CHECK in SQLite means rebuilding the table, so the prepared fields get triggers. SQLite's documented procedure for changing a table's schema requires foreign keys off. With them on, rebuilding `scene` would cascade-delete every token, and the check before commit replaces the enforcement that is switched off. A preset shaped like the grid needs no rule about which fields a copy takes. 50 MiB is the size Windows Explorer labels "50 MB". Keeping the PIN hash out of the shared contract means no type a client compiles against can carry it (`07` §1). The SRV-01 review (2026-09-24) found: two processes migrating one file (`make migrate` while the server starts) both applied the same migration, because the version was read before a deferred transaction, which a data migration would turn into silent double application (high, reproduced by a test with a second process); an infinite REAL was stored though the contract refuses it; the settings id could change; a map-less scene could hold a square size; the forward-migration test applied nothing at version 1; several CHECK constraints, column types and defaults had no test.
Alternatives: Plural or `Pascal` table names (rejected: SQLite convention is lower case, and singular matches the entity names); a `sort_order` or `position` column (rejected: renames a field the specification names); the grid and preset as JSON columns (rejected: no constraint could check a value inside them); rowid tables with a UUID column (rejected: a rowid is a sequential key); plain type affinity (rejected: SQLite would store `'2.5px'` in a REAL column); a composite key for AssetTag and an integer check for the one Settings row (rejected: `03` §3 gives every entity but Image a UUID); CHECK constraints for the three prepared fields (rejected: each Phase 2 or 3 change would rebuild a table); enforcing everything in application code only (rejected: `14` §8 prefers database constraints, and one forgotten route would break an invariant); no uniqueness on `order` (rejected: two scenes at one position would have no defined order); a non-null default square size such as 70 px (rejected: invents a calibration no one made); 50,000,000 bytes (rejected: shown to the DM as 47.7 MB by Windows); foreign keys left on during migrations (rejected: a table rebuild would cascade-delete children, shown by a test); an empty fixture or one generated at the latest version (rejected: it would not exercise the path a DM's existing data takes); the fixture as a committed `.db` file (rejected: generated data is reviewable as code, and Q-082 asks for a generated fixture). For the concurrent run: an exclusive lock for the whole run (rejected: `VACUUM INTO` cannot run in a transaction, and a per-migration lock with a re-read gives the same guarantee); a lock file in the data directory (rejected: a stale file after a crash would stop the server, and `09` §5 lists what the directory holds). Moving the PIN hash to its own table so that `SELECT *` on settings cannot return it (rejected: `03` §1 places it in Settings and the register fixes eight entities; explicit column lists in SRV-02 cover it, G-008).
Affected specs: `03` §1, `03` §2, `03` §3, `03` §4, `03` §6, `03` §7, `03` §8, `05` §6, `05` §7, `06` §2, `06` §5, `07` §1, `09` §2, `14` §8.

## D-076 (2026-09-24) — PIN, DM session, guessing protection and the /api session guard
Type: implementation
Decision: Routes (`shared/src/auth.ts`): `POST /api/auth` enters the PIN, `DELETE /api/auth` signs the calling browser out, `GET /api/auth` answers `{ dm }` for that browser only (D-048, Q-085), `GET /api/setup` answers `{ pin_set, local }` so that a DM view opened from the LAN can say setup happens on the server PC, `POST /api/setup` sets the first PIN, `GET /api/settings` returns the settings, and `PUT /api/settings/pin` changes the PIN given the current one. These five auth and setup operations are the only `/api` routes reachable without a DM session (`PUBLIC_API_ROUTES`). New error codes: `unauthorized` (401), `forbidden` (403: a foreign Origin, or setup from a LAN address), `pin_incorrect` (401), `locked_out` (429 with `Retry-After` in seconds), `pin_not_set` and `pin_already_set` (409). Guard: one root `onRequest` hook, which Fastify runs before body parsing and also for paths no route matches, treats a request as an API request when its matched route or, failing one, its raw path is `/api` or under `/api/`. It sets `Cache-Control: no-store`, refuses a POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE whose Origin is present and is not `http://` plus the request's own Host (so `Origin: null`, another port or `https` are refused, and a request without an Origin is accepted), lets the public routes through by matched route and method, and otherwise answers 401 `unauthorized` unless a valid session cookie is present. A protected route and an unknown `/api` path therefore get byte-identical answers whatever the body (G-005). Setup refuses a non-loopback client (127.0.0.0/8, ::1, IPv4-mapped forms normalised) in a route-level `onRequest` hook, before the body is read. PIN: scrypt with N = 2^15, r = 8, p = 1, a 16-byte random salt and a 32-byte key, stored as `scrypt$N$r$p$salt$key` in base64url so a later cost change still verifies old hashes; hashed asynchronously on libuv's thread pool; compared with `timingSafeEqual`. `settings` is read and written in `server/src/db/settings.ts` through explicit column lists, the hash by its own functions only; setup is `UPDATE ... WHERE pin_hash IS NULL` and a change is compare-and-swap on the old hash, so two concurrent writers cannot both win. Sessions: 32 random bytes as 64 hex characters in an in-memory set; cookie `emberglass_dm` with `Path=/; Max-Age=34560000; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict`, no `Secure` (plain HTTP, `07` §4); PIN entry always issues a fresh identifier and ends the one the browser brought; a successful setup signs its browser in and ends every existing session; a PIN change ends every session but the caller's. Guessing protection: per normalised client address, an attempt is counted as a failure when it starts, before hashing, and forgiven if the PIN is right; every fifth failure starts a lockout of 60 s times 2^(n-1) for the n-th lockout, the exponent capped at 30; attempts during a lockout are refused without counting; a correct PIN clears the address; at most 10,000 addresses are remembered, the oldest dropped first. A wrong current PIN in a PIN change counts towards the same lockout. `npm run reset-pin` (`server/src/auth/reset-pin-cli.ts`, run through tsx like `npm run migrate`) refuses a data directory without a database, applies pending migrations, clears the hash and logs `pin.reset`; the server reads the hash on every check, so it takes effect in a running server at once, and sessions that server holds last until the new PIN is set or the server restarts. Start-up logs `pin.unset` with the setup URL while no PIN is set. Logging: `pin.failed` (address, failure count), `pin.locked` (address, seconds), `pin.set`, `pin.changed` (sessions ended), `auth.signed_in`, `auth.signed_out`, `pin.reset`; a wrong PIN is logged once as `pin.failed`, not also as `http.rejected`. Rejected-request lines (`http.rejected`, which now carry the client address) are limited (`server/src/log/limiter.ts`) to 20 per address and 100 in total per 60 s window; the rest are counted, and one `http.rejected.dropped` line per window gives the number and the five addresses dropped most, written when the window ends. Failed-PIN and lockout lines are not limited, since the lockout bounds them to five per locked period per address. The code lives in `server/src/auth/` (hashing, sessions, lockout, reset), `server/src/http/auth.ts` (guard and routes) and `server/src/db/settings.ts`. `buildApp` takes the open database and records every declared route in `app.declaredRoutes`, which the tests walk so that a later route cannot escape the session and `pin_hash` checks.
Why: SRV-02 (`13` §4) implements the owner's PIN and session decisions (`07` §1, `07` §2, `07` §6, `07` §7, `07` §8; Q-007, Q-008, Q-009, Q-033, Q-042, Q-043, Q-044, Q-046, Q-058, Q-085) and closes G-005, G-006 and the SRV-02 half of G-008. Counting a failure before hashing is what makes the limit of five hold: with the count after the 0.1 s hash, twenty parallel guesses were all checked (shown by mutation). An onRequest hook matched by route and raw path covers unknown paths and runs before the parser, so a browser without the PIN cannot map routes or bodies. The loopback check comes before body parsing for the same reason. A persistent cookie lets the session last until the server restarts, as `07` §2 says, even when the browser is closed; the server-side store is what ends it. A request without an Origin cannot carry a SameSite=Strict cookie from another site, and non-browser clients send none, so refusing it would protect nothing. Setup is done at the server PC by the DM, so signing that browser in saves typing the new PIN twice; ending the sessions it finds covers the one case where some exist, after `reset-pin`. The per-window summary keeps a flood visible without letting it rotate away the lines `07` §8 wants kept. `GET /api/settings` is read-only here: G-008 asks for a test that no `/api` response carries the hash, which needs the route that serialises settings; changing settings belongs to REL-01.
Alternatives: @fastify/cookie and @fastify/rate-limit (rejected: two dependencies for one cookie and one counter, and a rate limit by request count does not express the doubling lockout); a browser-session cookie without Max-Age (rejected: closing the browser would end the session before the restart `07` §2 names); refusing writes without an Origin (rejected: protects against nothing a SameSite=Strict cookie does not, and breaks command-line use); a session check per route in a preHandler (rejected: runs after parsing and misses unknown paths, G-005); counting a failure only after the hash (rejected: parallel guesses bypass the limit); resetting the failure count on a timer (rejected: `07` §6 doubles on each further run, which a reset would undo); N = 2^14 or 2^17 (rejected: 2^14 halves the offline cost for no gain a DM would notice; 2^17 needs 128 MiB per concurrent hash); ending running sessions from `reset-pin` (rejected: a separate process cannot reach the server's memory, and the next setup ends them); setup that does not sign in (rejected: the DM would enter the new PIN twice at the same PC); the PIN change on `PATCH /api/settings` (rejected: settings changes are REL-01's, and a separate resource keeps the lockout on one route); rate-limiting failed-PIN lines as well (rejected: they are the lines G-006 exists to keep, and the lockout bounds them); a timer-free limiter reporting only on the next rejection (rejected: a flood that stops would never be summarised).
Affected specs: `02` §5, `07` §1, `07` §2, `07` §6, `07` §7, `07` §8, `09` §2, `09` §6.

## D-077 (2026-09-24) — SRV-02 review fixes: old-PIN sign-ins, setup Host, normalised /api paths, test hash cost
Type: implementation
Decision: Adds to D-076, which stays in force. (1) `POST /api/auth` re-reads the stored hash after the scrypt check and refuses with 401 `pin_incorrect` if it changed meanwhile; the check and the session's creation run in one synchronous step, as a PIN change's compare-and-swap and its ending of sessions do, so a session obtained with the old PIN is either ended by the change or never created. (2) Setup (`POST /api/setup`) and the `local` flag of `GET /api/setup` require the server machine by address and by name: a loopback client address and a Host whose name is `localhost`, `[::1]` or a 127.0.0.0/8 address; a missing or unparsable Host is refused. (3) The /api guard treats an unmatched request as an API request when its path is under `/api` as sent or after normalisation (`normalizedPath`: dot segments resolved by the URL parser, percent-escapes decoded, repeated slashes collapsed), so `/%61pi/...`, `//api/...` and `/x/../api/...` get the same 401 as any other /api path without a session. (4) `hashPin` and `buildApp` take the scrypt parameters; production always uses `SCRYPT_PARAMS`, and tests use N = 2^10 (`TEST_PIN_HASH_PARAMS` in `server/src/http/testing/app.ts`) except the tests of the stored format, of parallel guesses and of the old-PIN race, which use the production cost. (5) The e2e global teardown, on Windows, retries removing the temporary data directory as the Playwright process exits, after the server under test has stopped, and warns instead of failing if it still cannot. (6) The `npm run reset-pin` test runs the command as one shell command line.
Why: The SRV-02 review (2026-09-24) found: a sign-in with the old PIN still being hashed when a PIN change committed kept its session afterwards (high, reproduced: sign-ins started 130-160 ms into a change survived it), contrary to Q-033, whose point is to revoke whoever learnt the old PIN; a web page on the server PC whose host name was re-pointed at 127.0.0.1 passes both the loopback check and the Origin check, because its Origin equals its own Host, and could have set the first PIN and held a DM session (medium); `/%61pi/settings` answered 401 and `/%61pi/nope` 404 without a session, which is the route probing G-005 forbids (medium); on the two-core Windows runner, dozens of 32 MiB scrypt hashes in parallel starved other test files into 5 s timeouts (CI run 35990134838, `test · windows`); and the e2e teardown failed with EPERM because Playwright stops the web server only after the global teardown, while the server now holds the database open (`e2e · windows`). The hash cost is not what those tests check, and the tests that do check it keep the real cost.
Alternatives: A lock or generation counter around sign-in and change (rejected: the re-read in the same synchronous step gives the same guarantee without new state); refusing sign-in for the duration of a change (rejected: a DM's other device would see a spurious refusal); a Host allowlist for every /api route (deferred: it decides which names a DM may use to reach the server, which is the owner's; setup is loopback-only, so its Host can be checked now); rejecting every path that is not already normalised (rejected: browsers never send one, and a refusal would differ from the 401 an /api path gets); lowering the cost through an environment variable (rejected: a production process could be started with it); raising the test timeout on Windows (rejected: it hides the starvation, and the schema tests that timed out do not hash at all); stopping the server from the teardown (rejected: Playwright owns that process).
Affected specs: `07` §1, `07` §2, `07` §6, `02` §5, `10` §2.

## D-078 (2026-09-24) — Campaigns, sessions and scenes over REST: routes, order, duplication and confirmed deletion
Type: implementation
Decision: Routes (`shared/src/campaigns.ts`, `server/src/http/campaigns.ts`, `server/src/db/campaigns.ts`), nested one level as D-015 says: `GET`/`POST /api/campaigns`; `GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE /api/campaigns/:id`; `GET`/`POST /api/campaigns/:id/sessions` and `PUT /api/campaigns/:id/sessions/order`; `GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE /api/sessions/:id`; `GET`/`POST /api/sessions/:id/scenes` and `PUT /api/sessions/:id/scenes/order`; `GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE /api/scenes/:id`, whose PATCH takes only `name` until PRP-02 and PRP-03 add the map and grid; `POST /api/scenes/:id/duplicate`; and `GET …/:id/deletion` for each of the three. Every route needs a DM session through the guard of D-076 and is none of `PUBLIC_API_ROUTES`. Bodies are strict TypeBox schemas: an `id`, `order`, parent id, `rules_version`, grid or any other unknown field is refused as `validation_failed` before anything is stored, so identifiers come only from the server (`randomUUID`, lowercase version 4, D-038); an update needs at least one field; a session `date` must be a calendar date that exists (`isCalendarDate`), since the pattern alone admits 2026-02-30, which the CHECK of migration 0001 would turn into a server error; path ids must be lowercase UUIDs (400 otherwise) and an unknown one is 404. Creation answers 201 with the entity, reads and updates 200, deletions 204; responses are serialised through the entity schemas. Order: a new session or scene goes last; `PUT …/order` takes `{ ids }`, every child of the parent exactly once (a repeated id is `validation_failed`; a missing, extra or foreign one is 409 `order_mismatch`, changing nothing), and answers the reordered list. Every rewrite of the order (reorder, deletion, duplication) runs in one transaction in two steps: every sibling moves above the highest order in use, then each onto 0, 1, 2, …, so the order stays unique and gap-free under UNIQUE (parent, `order`) (D-075). Campaigns store no order (`03` §1) and are listed by name, case-insensitively, until Q-090 is answered. A new scene with a map copies that image's grid preset; with a map whose image has no preset yet, or without a map, it takes the stored defaults (size null, 30 × 20, 5 ft, offsets 0, visible); a `map_image_id` that does not exist is 400 `reference_not_found`. Duplicating takes `{ name }`, worded by the client from its message catalogue, and places the copy right after the original with the original's own grid and map and a copy of every token (asset, label, position, visibility, z-order) under new UUIDs. Deletion: `GET …/deletion` answers `{ sessions, scenes, tokens, live }`, counting everything the deletion removes, the entity itself included, and whether the live scene is among it; `DELETE` must carry `{ confirm: <that summary> }`, and the server recounts in the same transaction and deletes only if nothing differs, otherwise 409 `confirmation_mismatch` and nothing changes. The foreign keys of migration 0001 cascade the rows and set `settings.live_scene_id` to NULL; the siblings of a deleted session or scene close the gap. New error codes: `reference_not_found` (400), `order_mismatch` (409), `confirmation_mismatch` (409). `ApiFailure` can now carry envelope details.
Why: SRV-03 (`13` §4) delivers the CRUD, ordering, duplication and cascades of `02` §5 and `03` §2, §5, §6, §7. A confirmation that the server checks, rather than one the client merely shows, makes `03` §7's "after a confirmation that states what will be removed" hold even if a client forgets it, and recounting in the delete's own transaction means that what the DM was shown is exactly what goes, including the live warning Q-031 asks for. Scene-level routes at `/api/scenes/:id` keep D-015's one level of nesting and are where PRP-02 to PRP-04 add setup and tokens. A full list for a reorder is idempotent and cannot leave two children at one position; naming the parent's children exactly guards against a stale client. Keeping English out of stored names means the server writes no UI text (`08` §6). Deleting an image that becomes unreferenced (`03` §7, Q-002) needs the image files of SRV-04, so it is left to that package (G-013).
Alternatives: The confirmation as a client-side dialog only (rejected: the server would delete without it, and `03` §7 makes it part of the rule); a query flag `?confirm=true` (rejected: confirms nothing about what goes and would delete a tree that grew after the dialog); a one-time confirmation token from the summary (rejected: server state to expire for what an echo of the counts already proves); a relative move (`{ before: id }`) instead of the full list (rejected: two concurrent moves compose unpredictably, and the list is what the sidebar has); fractional or spaced orders to avoid the two-step rewrite (rejected: `03` §2 and D-075 store an integer order unique per parent, and gaps would need compaction anyway); scenes under `/api/sessions/:id/scenes/:sceneId` (rejected: two levels of nesting, D-015); PUT for renames (rejected: would require every field, including ones SRV-03 does not own such as the grid); a duplicate named by the server (`"<name> (copy)"`, rejected: English in stored data outside the catalogue); the copy at the end of the session (rejected: the DM duplicates a scene to vary it and expects it beside the original); 404 for a missing map image (rejected: the route exists; the reference in the body is what is wrong); adding an `order` to campaigns now (rejected: a schema field `03` §1 does not list, asked as Q-090).
Affected specs: `02` §5, `03` §2, `03` §3, `03` §5, `03` §6, `03` §7, `08` §1.

## D-079 (2026-09-24) — Campaigns are listed by name, not reordered (Q-090)
Type: spec-amendment
Decision: `08` §1 now reads: campaigns, sessions and scenes are creatable, renamable and deletable from the sidebar; sessions and scenes are also reorderable, by dragging with a keyboard alternative, while campaigns are listed by name. Tagged `[input, Q-023, Q-089, Q-090]`. No schema or code change: `GET /api/campaigns` already lists by name, case-insensitively, then by id (D-078).
Why: The owner answered Q-090 with A on 2026-09-24 (recommendation accepted). `03` §1 gives Campaign no `order`, and D-050 and Q-089 order only sessions and scenes; one game runs per server, so a DM has few campaigns and gains little from ordering them. The amendment removes the contradiction between `08` §1 and `03` §1 that SRV-03 raised.
Alternatives: B, an `order` on campaigns with a reorder route (rejected by the owner: a schema field and a route for little use; still possible later as an additive migration); ordering campaigns by last use (not chosen: needs a stored timestamp the model does not have); leaving `08` §1 unchanged (rejected: it would keep a MUST that the data model cannot satisfy).
Affected specs: `08` §1.

## D-080 (2026-09-24) — Image upload pipeline: raw-body upload, content sniffing, staged folder per image, variants and removal
Type: implementation
Decision: Routes (`shared/src/images.ts`, `server/src/http/images.ts`, `server/src/db/images.ts`, `server/src/images/`): `POST /api/images` takes the file's bytes as the whole body, whatever its Content-Type says (the route has its own Fastify scope whose one parser hands over the unread stream), and answers 201 with the Image record for new bytes or 200 with the stored image and its grid preset for bytes already stored; `GET /api/images/:id` answers the record; `PUT /api/images/:id/preset` takes a strict `GridPreset` and answers the record, leaving existing scenes' grids alone; the id is the lowercase hex sha256 (400 otherwise, 404 when unknown). All three need a DM session through the guard of D-076. Files are served at `GET /images/:id/:variant`, variants `original`, `display`, `thumbnail`; that route checks the DM session itself, answers every request without one with the same 404 `not_found` whether the image exists or not (players are LIV-02), validates the id and variant by hand so that a malformed one is also 404, and sends `Cache-Control: no-store` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, the original with its stored MIME type and the other two as `image/webp`. Receiving: `upload_limit_bytes` and `display_variant_size` are read from settings on every upload; a declared Content-Length over the limit is 413 before a byte is read; otherwise the body is streamed into `images/.incoming/<uuid>/original` while it is hashed, and reading stops as soon as the count passes the limit (413) or the first 12 bytes are not a PNG, JPEG or WebP signature (415); the refusal carries `Connection: close`, so the rest of the body never arrives. A 415 names what the bytes are in its one detail (`detected type: image/gif`, from a closed list of GIF, BMP, TIFF, AVIF, HEIC and PDF, else `unknown`; `detected type: image/png, incomplete or corrupt` when an accepted signature does not decode); a 413 names the limit (`limit: N bytes`). Processing: sharp 0.35 (`failOn: 'error'`, its cache off so that Windows can rename and remove files) must report the format the signature claims; the display version and the thumbnail are decoded from the whole file, which is what refuses a truncated one; both are WebP at sharp's default quality, auto-oriented, long edge at most `display_variant_size` (capped at WebP's 16,383) and at most 256 px, never upscaled. Stored width and height are the image as shown after its EXIF orientation. An image over sharp's default pixel limit is refused as 413. Storage: one folder per image, `images/<sha256>/` holding `original`, `display.webp` and `thumbnail.webp`; once processed, in one synchronous step, the server re-checks that the bytes were not stored meanwhile, renames the staged folder into place and inserts the row, removing the folder if the insert fails; the staged folder is removed in every other outcome. At start, `.incoming` is emptied and every sha256-named folder without a row is removed. `image.variants` is always `{ display, thumbnail }` with width and height, validating against `ImageVariantsSchema` (G-009). `regenerateDisplayVersions(db, imagesDir, size)` rewrites each display version in turn through a staged file and a rename, updating `variants.display`; an image deleted meanwhile is skipped; the settings screen (REL-01) calls it without waiting. Removal: `deleteEntity` (D-078) collects the map images of the scenes it deletes and, in the same transaction, deletes each image row that no asset and no scene references any more, preset included; the route then removes their folders, and a folder it cannot remove is logged (`images.remove_failed`) and removed at the next start. An upload nothing references yet is kept: `03` §7 removes an image when nothing references it any more, which is decided when a reference goes away. The dependency is `sharp` ^0.35.4, whose lockfile entries cover the Linux, Windows and macOS binaries CI and the DM need.
Why: SRV-04 (`13` §4) delivers `05` §6 and §7, the sha256 identity of `03` §3 and the removal of `03` §7 (Q-002, G-013). A raw body is what `fetch(url, { body: file })` sends, needs no multipart dependency, and cannot carry anything but the file; since the type is judged by content (`05` §6), a Content-Type is not needed either. Streaming with a running count, rather than buffering, is what D-044 asks for: an oversized upload is cut off at the limit, with a real-connection test that the client had not finished sending. Checking the signature first also cuts off a large non-image early; decoding the whole file then catches a forged or truncated one that merely starts right. One folder per image moved with one rename is the only way three files arrive together, which is what D-032 means by moving into place after success; doing the move and the insert synchronously, as better-sqlite3 allows, means no other request of the one server process can see a half-stored image or store the same bytes twice. Oriented dimensions keep the display version and the original aligned for calibration in browsers, which apply EXIF orientation by default. Naming the detected type lets the DM view say what to convert from, and the names come from a closed list, so no request data is echoed (`07` §8). Answering 404 for every image without a DM session is what `07` §5 asks of anything not entitled, and the entitlement to the live scene arrives with LIV-02.
Alternatives: multipart/form-data with @fastify/multipart (rejected: a dependency and a second parser for one file, and the field names would need a contract of their own); trusting the Content-Type or the file name (rejected by `05` §6); checking only the signature (rejected: a truncated PNG passes it); checking the size only after the body was received, or relying on Fastify's bodyLimit (rejected: D-044, and bodyLimit is fixed at start while the limit is a setting); flat files `images/<sha256>`, `<sha256>.display.webp` and `<sha256>.thumbnail.webp` (rejected: three renames are not one step, and a crash between them leaves a partial image); staging in the operating system's temporary folder (rejected: on another file system a rename becomes a copy, and `09` §5 keeps state in the data directory); inserting the row before moving the files (rejected: a crash between them leaves a row without files, which only a user-visible error would find; a folder without a row is swept at start); upscaling the thumbnail to exactly 256 px (rejected: adds bytes and blur for an image the library shows at 256 px at most); keeping the stored orientation (rejected: calibration on the display version would disagree with the original's width and height); `Cache-Control` with a long max-age, since the URL is content-addressed (rejected for now: `07` §5 makes an image unfetchable for players the moment it is hidden, and LIV-02 decides what a cache may keep); a 400 for a malformed image URL (rejected: describes the route to a browser without a session); removing every unreferenced image, new uploads included (rejected: a map uploaded before its scene is created would be deleted at once); a regeneration route now (rejected: the settings screen is REL-01's).
Affected specs: `02` §5, `02` §7, `03` §3, `03` §5, `03` §7, `05` §6, `05` §7, `07` §5, `09` §5.

## D-081 (2026-09-24) — Upload processing: only the decode check refuses a file (SRV-04 review)
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-080's processing step. An upload is checked by `metadata()` (the format must be the one its signature claims) and then a full decode through `stats()`, with `failOn: 'error'`; only a failure of these two refuses the file, as 415 (`incomplete or corrupt`) or, for sharp's pixel limit, 413. Writing the display version and the thumbnail comes after the check, and a failure there (a full disk, a write error) is the server's: it answers 500 `internal_error` and, like every other failure, leaves no file and no row. The image file route closes the file it opened when reading its size fails.
Why: The SRV-04 review (2026-09-24) found that D-080's one catch around decoding and writing turned every processing failure into a 415 saying the file was incomplete or corrupt, so a full disk would tell the DM that a good PNG is broken and send them to convert it; `server/src/http/images.test.ts` "answers 500, not a refusal of the file, when writing a version fails, and leaves no file and no row" is red against D-080's code. A decode to a handful of statistics costs one more pass over the image, which for the 10,000 × 7,000 px acceptance map (`10` §3) is well under the time of writing its versions.
Alternatives: Classifying sharp's error messages as input or output errors (rejected: libvips messages are not a stable contract and differ by codec); decoding once into memory and writing the versions from that buffer (rejected: a raw 10,000 × 7,000 RGBA image is 280 MB held for the whole request); keeping the single catch (rejected: misreports a server failure as the DM's file).
Affected specs: `05` §6, `05` §7.

## D-082 (2026-09-24) — A refused upload is answered at once and the rest of its body discarded, not cut off
Type: implementation
Decision: Replaces the `Connection: close` clause of D-080 and states how D-044's abort is carried out. When `POST /api/images` refuses an upload before its body has ended (a declared length or a running count over the limit, a signature that is not PNG, JPEG or WebP, or any later failure), the server answers at once with the refusal and keeps the connection, reading the rest of the body and throwing it away: nothing more is written to disk, held in memory, hashed or decoded. A body that has not ended 30 s after the refusal (`DISCARD_MS`) has its connection destroyed. The refusal no longer carries `Connection: close`.
Why: CI run 36007512379 (commit 3989675) failed on `test · windows`: `read ECONNRESET` in the real-connection test of the size limit. Closing a connection whose incoming body has not been read makes the receiving system reset it; the client then loses the 413 it had not yet read. Browsers send the whole body before reading an answer, so every DM uploading an oversized map would see a network error instead of the refusal, on Windows always and on Linux by timing. A browser-like test over a raw socket now sends the whole body and reads the answer, and fails against D-080's code on Linux too (the server ended the connection). D-044's purpose, that an oversized upload never consumes memory or disk and is not processed, still holds: what is read after the refusal is discarded as it arrives.
Alternatives: Keeping `Connection: close` (rejected: the reset above); a lingering close that half-closes and reads for a moment before destroying (rejected: needs the socket taken from Fastify and the envelope written by hand, for the same effect); discarding without a time bound (rejected: a client could hold a connection open indefinitely by trickling a body); a byte bound instead of a time bound (rejected: the useful bound is how long a DM waits, and on a LAN 30 s covers several hundred megabytes); relying on the client to check the file size first (kept as well: PRP-01 and PRP-02 check `upload_limit_bytes` before sending, G-017, but the server cannot depend on it).
Affected specs: `05` §6.

## D-083 (2026-09-24) — Asset library over REST: routes, lower-case tags, search in the server, usages and the in-use refusal
Type: implementation
Decision: Routes (`shared/src/assets.ts`, `server/src/http/assets.ts`, `server/src/db/assets.ts`): `GET`/`POST /api/assets`, `GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE /api/assets/:id` and `GET /api/assets/:id/usages`, all behind the DM-session guard of D-076 and none of `PUBLIC_API_ROUTES`. Responses are `LibraryAsset`: the Asset record of `entities.ts` plus `tags`, sorted. Bodies are strict TypeBox schemas: an `id` or any unknown field is `validation_failed` before anything is stored; create needs `name`, `image_id`, `category` and `size` and takes `default_hidden`, `tags` and `notes`; an update needs at least one field, and its `tags` replaces the whole set (rows of tags that stay keep their ids). An `image_id` that does not exist is 400 `reference_not_found` on create and update, storing nothing. Default visibility: left out on create, `default_hidden` is true for `monster` and false for `pc`, `npc` and `object` (D-020, Q-045); a later change of category leaves it alone, and no token's own `hidden` ever follows it. Tag case (G-009): `normalizeTag` in shared trims, collapses inner white space to one space, applies Unicode NFC and lower-cases; the route stores that one spelling, collapses duplicates, and refuses a tag empty once normalised as `validation_failed` at its own path; the database keeps UNIQUE (asset_id, tag) and no migration is added. List: `?q=` is a substring of the normalised name or of any tag, `?category=` one category, `?tag=` repeatable and every one must match as a whole tag (normalised the same way), unknown parameters refused; results by `name COLLATE NOCASE, name, id`, as campaigns are listed. The filtering runs in the server process over the sorted rows, not in SQL. Usages: every scene with tokens of the asset, as `{ scene_id, scene_name, session_id, session_title, campaign_id, campaign_name, tokens }`, ordered by campaign name, session order and scene order. Deletion takes no body: an asset any token uses is refused as 409 `asset_in_use`, a new error code, whose envelope carries those usages in a new optional `error.usages` field, changing nothing; an unused asset is deleted with its tags (cascade) and, in the same transaction, its image when no other asset and no scene references it; the image files go after the commit through the remover of D-080. Changing `image_id` deletes the previous image the same way. A token holds no image (`05` §3), so a new image is every token's image at once; telling a live player view is LIV-04. `TOKEN_FOOTPRINT` in shared carries the size table of `05` §2 for the canvas.
Why: SRV-05 (`13` §4) delivers `05` §1, §2, §4, §5 and the asset rules of `03` §7 over the `/api/assets` resource of `02` §5. Lower-casing is the only rule under which two tags that look the same cannot exist, which is the consequence G-009 names, and it needs no migration of existing rows; NFC makes a tag typed on two keyboards one tag. SQLite's `LIKE`, `lower` and NOCASE fold only ASCII letters, so an in-SQL search would miss `Ärger` for `ärger`; a personal library is small enough to filter in memory. Carrying the usages in the refusal means the scenes named are exactly those that blocked this deletion, with no second request that could disagree; the log line of a refusal keeps only its code (D-029), so no scene name reaches the log. A body-less DELETE suffices because nothing cascades beyond tags and an unused image, unlike the confirmed deletions of D-078.
Alternatives: Preserving the spelling typed and matching case-insensitively (rejected: which spelling the filter list shows is undefined, and `Cave` and `cave` would still be two rows); a `COLLATE NOCASE` unique index by migration (rejected: ASCII only, and a table rebuild or data migration for a rule the one writing route already enforces); refusing a tag with surrounding white space instead of trimming it (rejected: a DM typing `cave ` means `cave`); `LIKE` search in SQL (rejected: ASCII-only folding); several categories in one filter (rejected: an asset has one category, so it would be the only OR filter, which `05` §1 does not ask for); the refusal as a bare 409 with the client fetching `/usages` (rejected: two requests can disagree); scene names in `details[].message` (rejected: details describe the request, and their message is English diagnostics); a server-checked confirmation body on DELETE (rejected: nothing to confirm beyond the asset itself); following the category when `default_hidden` was never set explicitly (rejected: needs a stored flag `03` §1 does not list); PUT for updates (rejected: every field would be required, the image included).
Affected specs: `02` §5, `03` §2, `03` §7, `05` §1, `05` §2, `05` §3, `05` §4, `05` §5.

## D-084 (2026-09-24) — Images nothing references are removed when the server starts (G-016)
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines the removal of D-080. When the server starts, before it listens, `prepareImagesDir` deletes every image row that no asset and no scene references (`deleteAllUnreferencedImages`, preset included) in one transaction, then removes their folders with the other folders without a row, and logs `images.unreferenced_removed` with the count. While the server runs, a new upload nothing references yet is kept, as D-080 says.
Why: G-016: an upload the DM abandoned before creating the asset or the scene it was for stayed on disk indefinitely, because `03` §7 (Q-002) removes an image when a reference goes away and such an image never had one. At start nothing can be in flight between an upload and the create that references it, so the sweep cannot remove an image the DM is about to use; the data directory, the backup unit (`09` §5), holds only images in use after each start. `server/src/http/assets.test.ts` "removes, when the server starts, an upload abandoned before its asset or scene was created, with its files" is red without the sweep.
Alternatives: Uploading and creating in one request (rejected: multipart was rejected in D-080, and PRP-02's scene map would need the same); a periodic sweep of images older than some age (rejected: a timer and a threshold that no specification states, for no gain over a start that happens every game night); sweeping on every request that removes a reference (rejected: would delete an image uploaded moments ago for a create still to come); keeping them (rejected: G-016's consequence stays). A second server started on the same data directory runs the sweep against the first one's fresh uploads, the same residual as its `.incoming` (G-017).
Affected specs: `03` §7, `05` §7, `09` §5.

## D-085 (2026-09-24) — DM view: sign-in screens, the REST client and the scene tree (PRP-01 part 1)
Type: implementation
Decision: Client structure under `client/src/dm/`, in the DM chunk only. `api.ts` is the one REST client: same-origin `fetch` with JSON; an error answer is the shared envelope, thrown as `ApiError { status, code, retryAfter }` (`Retry-After` read for `locked_out`), and a request with no answer has the client-only code `network`; a 401 `unauthorized` from any call tells the view, which returns to the PIN form. `ui/errorMessage.ts` maps every `ERROR_CODES` entry and `network` to its own catalogue key through a `Record` typed over the codes, so a new code in shared fails `make typecheck` until it has a message, and the screen never shows a code. `DmView` asks `GET /api/setup` and `GET /api/auth` and shows one screen: setup (no PIN, a browser on the server PC: the PIN twice, checked against `PIN_PATTERN` before sending), setup elsewhere (no PIN, a LAN browser: where to go and no control), PIN entry (a wrong PIN as a field error that clears the field, a lockout naming its wait in seconds), or the workspace; a loading line while it asks and, if the request fails, the reason with Try again. The PIN lives only in form state until it is sent. Sign out is in the banner. The workspace is the tree in a left `<nav>` and the selected scene in `main`; the library panel and the live bar come with part 2 rather than as empty placeholders. The tree is nested lists in the disclosure pattern: a campaign or session is a button with `aria-expanded` whose children load on first open; each item shows Rename and Delete, and sessions and scenes also Move up and Move down (off at the ends), which are the keyboard alternative to dragging (Q-089); create and rename are inline forms whose submit button is off while the request runs (G-014), Escape cancels; after a change the tree reads the list it changed again, and keyboard focus returns to the same control of the same item, or its name when that control is off. Dragging uses native HTML5 drag and drop on session and scene rows among siblings only and sends the whole order (D-078); an `order_mismatch` reloads the list and keeps its message. Deletion is a native `<dialog>` opened with `showModal()`: it shows the counts of `GET …/deletion` relevant to the kind (a campaign its sessions, scenes and tokens; a session its scenes and tokens; a scene its tokens) and, when `live`, that the TV will show the idle screen; Delete sends the count back, and a `confirmation_mismatch` shows the new count and deletes nothing. `Button` gains a `danger` variant (dark text on the danger colour, a pair `tokens.test.ts` now checks) and a `small` size; a disabled button is muted text on the surface, never dimmed by opacity, which the contrast checks cannot see. The expand triangle is CSS content with empty alternative text, so the button's accessible name is the item's name alone. Component tests run against `client/src/ui/testing/fakeServer.ts`, a scripted stand-in for the SRV-03 routes behind `fetch` that also stands in for `showModal`, which jsdom lacks.
Why: PRP-01 (`13` §5) delivers `08` §1 and the first-run and PIN screens of `07` §1, §2, §6; splitting it keeps each `Now` item narrow. Visible buttons in the Tab order are what the generic keyboard gate of D-072 walks, so every tree action is proven operable by keyboard without a gate of its own, and nothing depends on a shortcut the DM must know. A native modal dialog gives the focus trap and Escape without code, in the evergreen browsers the DM view supports (`08` §7). Re-reading what changed keeps the server the only source of truth (`02` §3). A map typed over the codes is what G-014 asked for and cannot drift.
Alternatives: An ARIA tree with a roving tabindex and keyboard shortcuts for rename, delete and move (rejected: the actions are invisible until learned, and the Tab-order gate could not reach them); Alt+Arrow keys as the only keyboard reordering (rejected for the same reason); a drag-and-drop library (rejected: a dependency for sibling reordering that the platform does); a custom modal with its own focus trap (rejected: more code for what `<dialog>` does); optimistic updates (rejected: a refused change would show state the server does not have); keeping placeholder library and live-bar regions now (rejected: an empty stub where a gap belongs); showing the server's English `message` (rejected: `08` §6 keeps every UI string in the catalogue); dimming disabled buttons with opacity (rejected: lowers contrast invisibly to the checks).
Affected specs: `07` §1, `07` §2, `07` §6, `08` §1, `08` §6, `08` §7, `08` §8, `03` §7.

## D-086 (2026-09-24) — End-to-end tests share one server in a known order; the keyboard gate walks the signed-in workspace
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-072. `e2e/playwright.config.ts` runs one worker, not fully parallel, and has two projects: `first-run` runs `first-run.spec.ts` alone on the server's fresh data directory, and `chromium` runs every other spec after it (`dependencies`). The First run journey (`08` §10) sets the PIN through the DM view on `127.0.0.1`, which the server treats as the server PC (D-077). Other tests sign in through `e2e/tests/dm.ts`: the context's request client posts the PIN to `/api/auth` (setting it first if a spec ever runs alone), sharing the page's cookies and sending no Origin, which the server accepts (D-076). The keyboard gate's `openDm` signs in before every opening and the suite seeds one campaign with a session, so the walk covers the workspace and a tree item; operating Sign out during the walk is undone by the next opening. The contrast test checks the PIN form, the workspace with a campaign open and a create form, and the delete dialog.
Why: Every test uses the one server that `webServer` starts, so parallel workers would race on the one PIN and on shared data; the PIN can be set only once per data directory, so the journey that sets it must run first. A gate that walked only the PIN form would miss every control the DM uses.
Alternatives: A server per test file (rejected: the build and start take most of the run, and `webServer` starts one); resetting the data directory between tests (rejected: needs a server restart the harness does not offer); signing in through the form in every test (rejected: slower and tests the form many times over); leaving the gate on the signed-out view (rejected: covers two controls).
Affected specs: `10` §2, `08` §8, `08` §10.

## D-087 (2026-09-24) — Sign-out shows the PIN form only once the server ended the session; focus after a deletion (PRP-01 part 1 review)
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-085. Sign out switches to the PIN form only when `DELETE /api/auth` succeeded, or answered `unauthorized` (the session had already ended, and the 401 handler shows the form); any other failure keeps the workspace and shows, under the banner, that this browser is still signed in, with the reason (`dm.signOutFailed`). After a confirmed deletion, keyboard focus moves to the deleted item's parent: a scene's session, a session's campaign, and for a campaign the New campaign button.
Why: The PRP-01 part 1 review (2026-09-24) found that sign-out showed the PIN form in a `finally`, so a request that never reached the server left a valid session on a laptop that looked signed out (medium, security; `client/src/dm/DmView.test.tsx` "stays signed in and says so when sign-out fails, so a shared laptop is not left looking signed out (review M-1)" is red against the old code), and that a confirmed deletion left keyboard focus on the page body (low, accessibility; `client/src/dm/tree/SceneTree.test.tsx` "moves focus to the parent after a deletion, and to New campaign after a campaign goes (review L-1)" is red against the old code).
Alternatives: Clearing the view on any failure and relying on the next request's 401 (rejected: the DM leaves believing the device is signed out); retrying the sign-out automatically (rejected: hides a server that is down); focusing the tree heading after a deletion (rejected: not operable, and farther from where the DM was working).
Affected specs: `07` §2, `08` §8.

## D-088 (2026-09-24) — DM view: the library panel, upload then create, the live bar; keyboard gate for fields; G-014 residuals accepted (PRP-01 part 2)
Type: implementation
Decision: The workspace is the live bar on top of three columns: the tree, the selected scene and the asset library (`08` §1). `Workspace` reads `GET /api/settings` when it opens and after every deletion or rename in the tree, for the live scene and `upload_limit_bytes`. The live bar is a labelled region whose status line names the live scene (`GET /api/scenes/:id`) or says nothing is live and the TV shows the idle screen; going live and the one-click return are LIV-04. The library (`client/src/dm/library/`) sends its search, once typing pauses for 250 ms, its one category and every selected tag to `GET /api/assets`, so the server's rules decide (D-022, D-083), and drops an answer that a newer query overtook; the tags to pick are every tag of the whole library, as toggle buttons with `aria-pressed`. Each asset shows its thumbnail (decorative, `alt=""`, the name beside it), name, category and size, and that its tokens start hidden. Create and edit share one native dialog: image (PNG, JPEG or WebP), name, category, size, tags separated by commas, notes and a "start hidden" checkbox. For a new asset the checkbox follows the category until the DM sets it and is otherwise left out of the request, so the server applies D-020; an edit sends the asset's own flag, which a category change does not move (D-083). A file larger than `upload_limit_bytes` is refused in the form, before anything is sent, naming its size and the limit in MB of 1,048,576 bytes (so the default reads 50 MB, D-075); the server still judges both (`05` §6). Saving uploads the image first (`upload()` in `dm/api.ts`, the file as the whole body, D-080) and then creates or changes the asset to use it; an image uploaded for a create that then fails is removed at the next start (D-084). Deleting asks for a confirmation; an `asset_in_use` refusal lists every scene of `error.usages` as campaign › session › scene with its token count and offers only Close; `ApiError` carries `usages`. The tree marks the row a drag is over and keeps Move up and Move down off in a list while its reorder request runs (G-018). `ui/Dialog.tsx` is the one native-dialog component. The keyboard gate (D-072, D-086) counts a text field as operated when a typed key changes its value and a select when ArrowDown does, and a checkbox by Space; the contrast test also covers the asset dialog. The two residuals of G-014 are accepted: a create sent twice by anything but the button makes two entities, and a deletion's confirmation proves its counts, not which rows.
Why: PRP-01 part 2 (`13` §5) completes `08` §1 and the library of `05` §1. Filtering on the server keeps one rule for the library, the picker (PRP-04) and the tests. Checking the size before sending is what G-017 asks, and naming both numbers tells the DM what to do. Upload-then-create is one action for the DM without a multipart route (D-080). Every control the gate walks has to be operable its own way: a text field is not activated by Enter. For G-014: the only UI that creates holds its button off while pending, and a changed tree with equal counts needs a concurrent edit from a second DM browser, which the MVP's one-DM table makes rare, while a per-row confirmation would put server state or long lists in every dialog.
Alternatives: Filtering the whole library in the browser (rejected: a second copy of D-083's rules to keep in step); searching on every keystroke (rejected: a request per letter); a tag filter as a free-text field (rejected: typos match nothing; the known tags are few); a thumbnail with the asset's name as alt text (rejected: the name would be read twice); showing the size in bytes (rejected: not what the DM sees in the file manager); a combined multipart create (rejected in D-080); a live bar that polls settings (rejected: LIV-01's `dm` room is the way changes arrive); leaving G-014 open until a server-side idempotency key (rejected: a contract change for a double submit the UI already prevents).
Affected specs: `08` §1, `08` §8, `05` §1, `05` §4, `05` §5, `05` §6, `03` §7, `10` §2.

## D-089 (2026-09-25) — Library review fixes: focus after deleting an asset, and each request keeps its own failure (PRP-01 part 2 review)
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-088. After an asset is deleted, keyboard focus moves to the library's New asset button, since the button that opened the dialog went with the asset (as D-087 does in the tree). The library's two requests, the whole library for the tags and the filtered results, each keep their own failure message, so one answering does not hide that the other failed.
Why: The PRP-01 part 2 review (2026-09-25) found both (low, accessibility and correctness): `client/src/dm/library/Library.test.tsx` "moves focus to New asset once the asset it was on is deleted (review L-1)" and "keeps saying the tags could not be read when the results could (review L-2)" are each red against the old code. A third low, no progress shown while an upload is sent, is deferred to PRP-02's map upload (G-017), where files are large.
Alternatives: Focusing the search field (rejected: the DM was acting on the list, and New asset is the next likely action); retrying the tag list automatically (rejected: hides a failing server); a progress bar now (rejected: `fetch` reports no upload progress, so it needs XMLHttpRequest, which belongs with the large maps of PRP-02).
Affected specs: `08` §8, `05` §1.

## D-090 (2026-09-25) — Scene map and grid visibility over REST; the shared map canvas, its camera and the uncalibrated overlay; map upload with progress (PRP-02)
Type: implementation
Decision: Contract (`shared/src/campaigns.ts`): `PATCH /api/scenes/:id` takes, in a strict body with at least one field, `name`, `map_image_id` (a lowercase sha256; null is refused, since no specification describes removing a map, only attaching one later, `03` §6) and `grid: { visible }` (`SceneGridUpdateSchema`); every other grid field, and any unknown field, is `validation_failed` until PRP-03 adds calibration. This is a contract change although D-037 lists PRP packages as consumers: D-078 deferred the scene setup fields to PRP-02 and PRP-03. Server (`updateScene` in `server/src/db/campaigns.ts`), in one transaction: a `map_image_id` different from the scene's replaces the whole grid with that image's preset, or with the stored defaults (size null, offsets 0, visible, 5 ft, 30 × 20) while it has none, exactly as a new scene starts (`03` §5, §6, D-078), because the old grid is in the old map's pixels; the same id again changes nothing; a missing image is 400 `reference_not_found` and nothing changes; tokens are never touched, their positions being grid units (`03` §4); the previous map is deleted when no asset and no scene references it any more and its files removed after the commit, as a deletion does (`03` §7, Q-002, D-080); `name` and then `grid.visible` apply after the map. Toggling `visible` writes neither the image preset nor any other scene: only calibration (PRP-03) updates the preset (`03` §5). A change to the live scene is saved but pushed to no one until LIV-04 (`04` §10, G-019). Client: `client/src/canvas/geometry.ts` (pure) and `MapCanvas.tsx` (react-konva, D-006) are the one canvas of both views. World pixels are the display version's; a map-less scene is its columns × rows at 64 world px a square on a neutral dark grey (D-016). Grid size and offsets are multiplied by display ÷ original width (`06` §2), the display width being `image.variants.display`, or the loaded display version's natural size for a record without variants. Until calibration a scene with a map has no square size, and the overlay reads the stored columns as the map's width in squares (size = original width ÷ columns), the known-dimensions reading of `06` §1, so a freshly attached map shows a grid that PRP-03 then corrects. Lines are one Konva `Line` each, one screen pixel wide at every zoom; more than 2,000 on one axis are not drawn. The overlay's opacity is 0.7 when players see the grid and 0.25 in the DM view when they do not (D-026); the player mode draws none then. Only `/images/<sha256>/display` is ever requested (`07` §5). Camera: screen = world × scale + offset, starting fitted and centred; the DM mode zooms about the pointer with the wheel (×1.1 a notch) and about the centre with + / = and − (×1.25) or the Zoom in and Zoom out buttons, pans by dragging the stage and with the arrow keys (64 px, ×4 with Shift), and 0 or Fit map fits again; scale is kept within 0.02–8; another map or extent fits afresh. The viewport is a focusable `role=application` element named after the scene and described by visible help text; keys with Ctrl, Alt or Meta are left to the browser. The player mode has no control, nothing focusable, does not listen to the pointer and always fits (`04` §9, `08` §9); the player view mounts it from LIV-03. The camera and the overlay state are exposed as `data-camera-*` and `data-grid` attributes, which the end-to-end tests read to sample the drawn pixels. DM view: the workspace mounts `ScenePanel` keyed by the selected scene, which reads the scene and its map's record from the server rather than the tree's copy; attaching a map checks the file against `upload_limit_bytes` before sending, uploads it and sets it on the scene in one action (G-016), and shows a labelled progress bar and percentage meanwhile (G-017); `upload()` in `dm/api.ts` now sends by XMLHttpRequest, for its progress events, and the library shares it. The players-see-the-grid checkbox shows the requested state while its PATCH runs, disabled, and falls back to the stored state on a refusal. Tests: jsdom has no 2D context, so `ui/testing/canvas2d.ts` gives canvases a no-op one and fakes ResizeObserver and image loading, and the component tests read the real Konva node tree; `FakeServer` also answers XMLHttpRequest.
