The contrast check of the e2e keyboard spec moved to `e2e/tests/contrast.ts`, shared with `canvas.spec.ts`. Dependencies: `konva` ^10.7.0, `react-konva` ^19.3.0, bundled by Vite.
Why: PRP-02 (`13` §5) delivers `08` §3's map background with zoom and pan in both views, the overlay of `06` §2 with player visibility, and map-less scenes (`03` §6). Resetting the grid to the new image's preset is the same rule a new scene follows and the only one that keeps size and offsets meaningful, since they are original-image pixels of one particular map. Refusing null keeps to what the specifications describe; a map-less scene is one New scene away. Reading uncalibrated columns as the map's width gives the DM a visible, adjustable grid at once, which is what the acceptance's attach-and-see journey needs, and it is the extent the scene already stores. One Konva node per line makes every position testable against the geometry, and at the acceptance map (10,000 px, `10` §3) a 70 px grid is about 250 nodes. XMLHttpRequest is the only browser API that reports upload progress; one upload function keeps a single error mapping. Keying the panel by scene makes a stale answer land on an unmounted component instead of the new scene. Showing the checkbox's requested state keeps the control responsive (Playwright's own check caught the lag), while the canvas follows only what the server stored.
Alternatives: Accepting `map_image_id: null` to detach a map (rejected: not described in any specification, `specs/README.md` conflict rule 3); keeping the scene's grid when the map changes (rejected: offsets and size in another image's pixels misplace every square); writing `visible` into the image preset (rejected: `03` §5 ties the preset to calibration, and an uncalibrated image cannot hold a preset without a size); no overlay until calibration (rejected: the DM could not see or check the grid, and the attach journey would show nothing); one Konva `Shape` drawing every line in a scene function (rejected: faster at huge line counts but opaque to tests; revisit if the 10,000 px acceptance map proves slow); the canvas's own drag-to-pan through a custom handler rather than Konva's draggable stage (rejected: re-implements pointer capture); a PixiJS canvas (rejected in D-006); a scene selection kept in the URL so that a reload reopens it (rejected: not asked; G-018 already records that the tree opens collapsed); `fetch` with a streamed request body for progress (rejected: browsers report no upload progress for it, and streaming bodies need HTTP/2 or half-duplex support that the target TV browser may lack); mocking react-konva in component tests (rejected: the real node tree is what the geometry must reach); a canvas-drawing library such as node-canvas for jsdom (rejected: a native dependency on both CI runners for pixels the end-to-end tests already read in a real browser).
Affected specs: `02` §5, `02` §6, `03` §4, `03` §5, `03` §6, `03` §7, `04` §9, `04` §10, `05` §6, `06` §1, `06` §2, `07` §5, `08` §3, `08` §8, `08` §9.

## D-091 (2026-09-25) — The keyboard gate waits for the workspace to load and checks focus on every reopening
Status: superseded by D-092
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-072 and D-086. `openDm` in `e2e/tests/keyboard.spec.ts` now also waits until no "Loading…" text is on the page, so the tree and the library have answered before the walk counts elements or presses Tab; and when an element is operated by a second key (Space after Enter), the walk asserts that Tab reached it again before pressing the key, as the first pass already did. The contrast check moved to `e2e/tests/contrast.ts`, shared with `e2e/tests/canvas.spec.ts`.
Why: During PRP-02 the walk failed in 2 of 5 runs, once inside `make clean-start`, with "Space did not operate button Delete": after reopening the page it tabbed while the campaign list was still arriving, so focus landed on another element and the listener on the intended one never fired. The larger DM bundle (Konva) and the campaigns `canvas.spec.ts` seeds made the race likelier; it was there since D-086. With both changes, three consecutive `make e2e` runs and `make clean-start` passed. The gate is stricter than before: a mis-aimed key press now fails at once, naming the element.
Alternatives: Retrying the test (rejected: `retries: 0` is deliberate, and a retry would hide the race); a fixed wait after loading (rejected: slow and still racy); waiting for the network to go idle (rejected: the library's debounced search makes idleness timing-dependent); leaving the second-key reopening unchecked (rejected: it is what let the key land elsewhere without saying so).
Affected specs: `08` §8, `10` §2.

## D-092 (2026-09-25) — The keyboard gate waits for the workspace to load and checks focus on every reopening (count corrected)
Type: implementation
Decision: Supersedes D-091, whose record miscounted the failing runs; the decision is unchanged. Refines D-072 and D-086. `openDm` in `e2e/tests/keyboard.spec.ts` now also waits until no "Loading…" text is on the page, so the tree and the library have answered before the walk counts elements or presses Tab; and when an element is operated by a second key (Space after Enter), the walk asserts that Tab reached it again before pressing the key, as the first pass already did. The contrast check moved to `e2e/tests/contrast.ts`, shared with `e2e/tests/canvas.spec.ts`.
Why: During PRP-02 the walk failed in 2 of 4 runs, the second inside `make clean-start`, with "Space did not operate button Delete": after reopening the page it tabbed while the campaign list was still arriving, so focus landed on another element and the listener on the intended one never fired. The larger DM bundle (Konva) and the campaigns `canvas.spec.ts` seeds made the race likelier; it was there since D-086. With both changes, three consecutive `make e2e` runs and `make clean-start` passed. The gate is stricter than before: a mis-aimed key press now fails at once, naming the element.
Alternatives: Retrying the test (rejected: `retries: 0` is deliberate, and a retry would hide the race); a fixed wait after loading (rejected: slow and still racy); waiting for the network to go idle (rejected: the library's debounced search makes idleness timing-dependent); leaving the second-key reopening unchecked (rejected: it is what let the key land elsewhere without saying so).
Affected specs: `08` §8, `10` §2.

## D-093 (2026-09-25) — PRP-02 review fixes: focus kept through saves, one change at a time, a halo under the grid, functional camera updates, a narrowed canvas prop
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-090 after the PRP-02 review (prompt 2, four passes, 2026-09-25). Scene panel: while a map upload or a grid change runs, the file field, the Attach or Replace map button and the players-see-the-grid checkbox carry `aria-disabled` and refuse activation instead of being disabled, so keyboard focus stays where it was (a disabled control drops it to the page); only one change runs at a time, so a grid toggle cannot race the map it is about to be reset by. The upload's progress and a "Map attached." message are in one `role=status` region. A type or size refusal of the file is shown beside the file field (D-069) and focus moves there, as it does for a missing file; other failures stay in the alert above. A failure to read the map's record no longer leaves an endless loading line. Replace map is described by a note that a new map starts with its own grid and the current image is deleted if nothing else uses it; a confirmation that also names a lost calibration is PRP-03's. The map-failed message also suggests replacing the map. `.eg-button[aria-disabled='true']` looks like a disabled button. Canvas: each grid line is a light 1 px line over a 3 px dark halo, under the same opacity, so the overlay and its faint form show on light maps; camera changes from the wheel, drag, keys and buttons are functional updates from the latest camera, since several wheel events can arrive before a render; a wheel event without a vertical part is ignored; the display version is loaded again only when the map id changes; the `map` prop is `CanvasMap` (id, width, height, variants), so the player view can build it from its snapshot without the DM record (LIV-03); the viewport's minimum height is 12rem, the file field shrinks inside its column, and the cursor shows grabbing while pressed. Accepted, not fixed: selecting a scene again while its upload still runs shows the scene as read before that upload's map change landed, until it is selected once more (the server holds the right state; nothing wrong is stored); the fake server still gives every upload a new image rather than de-duplicating by content.
Why: Review findings, each verified: the UX pass reproduced focus falling to the page body after Space on the checkbox and Enter on Attach map (high), the file input overlapping the library at 1,024 px, the canvas below the fold at 1,280 × 720, the contradictory error and loading line, the reset on replace being silent, and the grid nearly invisible on a parchment map; the correctness pass found the stale camera closure under several wheel events and the sideways swipe zooming out; the test pass showed that removing the panel's key, the stale-record guard or the pointer anchoring left every test green. Tests now pin each: `client/src/dm/ScenePanel.test.tsx`, `client/src/dm/Workspace.test.tsx`, `client/src/canvas/MapCanvas.test.tsx`, `client/src/dm/api.test.ts`, `server/src/http/scene-setup.test.ts`, `e2e/tests/canvas.spec.ts`, and ten mutations turn them red. The re-selection case needs an upload in flight across two scene switches; fixing it would move per-scene request state into the workspace for a view that corrects itself on the next selection.
Alternatives: Restoring focus after the request ends instead of keeping controls focusable (rejected: focus would still jump to the body meanwhile, and a screen reader would announce the page); allowing a grid toggle during an upload with a request sequence number (rejected: the map's reset would still overwrite the toggle, which is the surprise to avoid); a dashed line for the faint overlay (rejected for now: opacity plus the halo is enough and keeps one line style for PRP-03's live overlay); Konva shadows for the halo (rejected: a blurred shadow per line costs more to draw than a second line); keeping `visible` across a map replacement (rejected: D-090 resets the grid to the new image's preset, which may itself hide it; the note tells the DM instead); keeping every upload and grid change in the workspace per scene (rejected, see why).
Affected specs: `08` §3, `08` §7, `08` §8, `06` §2, `07` §5, `03` §7.

## D-094 (2026-09-25) — Grid calibration: the calibration fields of the scene update, the preset written with them, the three methods, the far-corner magnifier and the replace confirmation (PRP-03)
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-090 and D-093. Contract (`shared/src/campaigns.ts`, a contract change although `13` §5 marks PRP-03 `Contract change: no`): `SceneGridUpdateSchema` takes, strict and with at least one field, `visible` and the calibration fields `size` (a decimal > 0, at most 1e6), `offset_x` and `offset_y` (decimals within ±1e6) and `columns` and `rows` (integers 1–100,000), all in the original image's pixels (`06` §2); `CALIBRATION_FIELDS` names them. Feet per square is not set here: nothing uses it before the ruler, so LIV-07 adds it to the body and the UI, and until then every scene keeps its stored 5 (`06` §5); `type` stays refused (`03` §8). A new error code `calibration_needs_map` (409) refuses any calibration field on a scene without a map, checked before anything of the body is written, so a rename in the same body is not applied either (`06` §1, `03` §6); `visible` stays allowed there. Server (`updateScene` in `server/src/db/campaigns.ts`), in the one transaction D-090 set up: the map step first, then the grid fields merge over the scene's grid; when a calibration field is present, a size still null is read as original width ÷ columns, the reading the overlay already uses (D-090), so a body with only offsets or only columns and rows calibrates, and the whole resulting grid, `visible` and `feet_per_square` included, is written as the image's preset with `updateGridPreset` before the scene row, so a failure of either leaves both as they were (`03` §5, Q-001). A body that attaches a map and calibrates at once calibrates the new map. A `visible` change alone still writes no preset. Other existing scenes and token positions are never touched (`03` §4, §5). Numbers are stored as SQLite REAL, so a decimal such as 70.123456789 round-trips exactly. Client: `client/src/canvas/calibration.ts` (pure) holds the arithmetic in original pixels: known dimensions give size = original width ÷ columns with offsets 0 and the typed rows; a rectangle over N × N squares gives size = the mean of its width and height ÷ N and the offset from its corner, wrapped into one square; fine tuning takes size and offsets as typed; in the last two the extent is the whole squares that cover the map (at least 1 × 1). `client/src/dm/calibration/draft.ts` keeps what the panel edits: the method, every field as typed, the rectangle and the calibration they give, which is the last one every field of the method produced; each method's result is written back into the other methods' fields, so fine tuning continues from known dimensions or the rectangle. Fine-tuning fields step by 0.1 px with the up and down arrow keys, 1 px with Shift, rounded to four decimals so 70.4 + 0.1 shows as 70.5; a size too fine to draw (more than 2,000 lines on an axis, D-090) or a field that is not a number is refused beside the field (D-069), and Save then focuses it and sends nothing. `MapCanvas` gains an optional `measure` prop for the DM mode: while set, the stage is not draggable and a pointer drag draws a dashed accent rectangle, reported through `onDraw` in original pixels (world × original ÷ display width); a press shorter than 4 screen pixels either way is a click and reports nothing; the player mode never measures. The rectangle method is operated with the pointer only; known dimensions and fine tuning are the keyboard's way to calibrate, and fine tuning finishes a rectangle's result by keyboard (`08` §8). `CornerMagnifier` draws, only while calibrating and only in the DM view, the original (the calibration version, `05` §7, which only a DM session may fetch, `07` §5) cropped to the map's bottom-right corner, a square three draft squares a side, between 48 and 200 original px and never larger than the map, into a 200 px Konva stage with image smoothing off, so the magnification is between 1 and about 4, with the draft grid over it; it floats over the canvas's bottom-right corner, lets the pointer through, and says when the original cannot be loaded. Scene panel: a scene with a map shows Calibrate grid beside the players-see-the-grid checkbox; a map-less scene shows none. Calibrating replaces the map and grid setup row with the calibration panel above the canvas (method radios, the method's fields, the measured rectangle, the result line, Save calibration and Cancel), so the map cannot be replaced meanwhile and the canvas keeps its height; the overlay draws `{ ...scene.grid, ...draft }` at every change, before anything is saved; opening moves focus to the chosen method, and Save or Cancel returns it to Calibrate grid, with `Grid calibrated.` in the status region. Save sends `{ grid: { size, offset_x, offset_y, columns, rows } }`; while it runs the controls refuse with `aria-disabled` (D-093); a refusal keeps the draft and shows the error above. Replacing the map of a calibrated scene (`grid.size` not null, which includes a scene that started from a preset) opens a native modal confirmation that says the calibration goes; Keep the current map or Escape closes it, changes nothing and returns focus to Replace map; Replace map proceeds with the upload. `Button` takes a `ref`, a prop in React 19. Tests: `server/src/http/scene-setup.test.ts` (calibration describe, including a trigger that makes the scene write fail to prove the preset rolls back), `shared/src/campaigns.test.ts`, `client/src/canvas/calibration.test.ts`, `client/src/canvas/MapCanvas.test.tsx` (measuring), `client/src/dm/calibration/Calibration.test.tsx`, and `e2e/tests/calibration.spec.ts`, which uploads generated maps with a drawn grid (`griddedPng`, Q-088) and reads the overlay's light line from the canvas pixels, placing it by the lightness centroid and requiring it within 0.6 screen px of each drawn line, out to the far edge; a draft 0.1 px off fails it. The canvas pixel helpers moved to `e2e/tests/canvas-view.ts`, shared with `canvas.spec.ts`.
Why: PRP-03 (`13` §5) delivers `06` §1's three methods with a live overlay, a decimal size and the far-corner magnifier, `06` §2's storage in original pixels and `03` §5's presets. Writing the preset from the scene update in the same transaction is the only way to keep the scene and its preset from disagreeing; the SRV-04 `PUT /api/images/:id/preset` stays for other callers but the DM view no longer needs a second request that could fail alone. Reading a missing size as width ÷ columns keeps what the DM saw and what is stored the same. A dedicated error code tells a map-less scene's refusal apart from a malformed body, which the client could not word otherwise. Leaving feet per square to LIV-07 keeps a field without a consumer out of this package, and the preset still carries the stored value, so nothing is lost when the ruler arrives. Averaging the rectangle's two sides tolerates a drag that is a pixel off square; wrapping the offset keeps the same grid stored one way. The magnifier uses the original because the display version of a 10,000 px map is at most 4,096 px and loses the sub-pixel detail calibration is for, and three squares show whether the last lines sit on the drawn ones. Replacing the setup row while calibrating was chosen after a screenshot at 1,280 × 720 showed a side panel squeezing the canvas to 240 px and the strip pushing it below the fold. The end-to-end check first read only whether any grid pixel was near a line, which a halo 3 px wide satisfied with a size 0.1 px wrong; the centroid of the light line is what catches drift.
Alternatives: Calibrating through `PUT /api/images/:id/preset` and a separate scene PATCH (rejected: two requests, and a failure between them leaves the preset and the scene apart); `validation_failed` for calibration on a map-less scene (rejected: the body is valid, the scene is not, and the DM would read a schema error); accepting calibration fields on a map-less scene as its extent (rejected: `06` §1 and D-016 say calibration applies only with a map, and changing a map-less extent is described by no specification, `specs/README.md` conflict rule 3); refusing a calibration without a size on an uncalibrated scene (rejected: the overlay already reads width ÷ columns, and the DM would have to retype it); setting feet per square here with its own field (rejected for now: no consumer until LIV-07); the rectangle's size from its width alone (rejected: a drag a pixel off square would bias one axis); a keyboard-driven rectangle with arrow keys moving its corners (rejected: fine tuning already calibrates by keyboard, and moving two corners by key is slower than typing the size); the magnifier from the display version (rejected: loses the detail calibration needs, and `05` §7 names the original as the calibration version); a fixed 4× magnifier (rejected: large squares would show less than one square); the calibration panel beside the canvas (rejected: 240 px of canvas at 1,280 px wide) or below it (rejected: off screen at 720 px tall); keeping the map setup visible and refusing while calibrating (rejected: it cost the canvas its height, and the refusal is moot once the row is gone); a confirmation for every map replacement (rejected: an uncalibrated map has nothing to lose, and D-093's note already says a new map starts with its own grid).
Affected specs: `03` §4, `03` §5, `03` §6, `05` §7, `06` §1, `06` §2, `06` §3, `06` §5, `07` §5, `08` §3, `08` §8.

## D-095 (2026-09-25) — PRP-03 review fixes: a size too fine is refused, the drag ends wherever it is released, one status region, a canvas in view at 1,280 × 720, and the magnifier's states
Status: superseded by D-096
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-094 after the PRP-03 review (prompt 2, four passes, 2026-09-25: one high, eight medium, eight low findings; no critical and no isolation finding). Server and contract: `MAX_GRID_LINES_PER_AXIS` (2,000) moves to `shared` and the canvas's `MAX_LINES_PER_AXIS` is it; `updateScene` refuses a calibrated size that gives more squares than that across the map's longer side as 400 `validation_failed` with the detail `/grid/size`, thrown inside the transaction so that a rename or a map attached in the same body rolls back too; a size stored before this, or by a request made outside the DM view, cannot hang the view either: `cornerView` counts its lines instead of stepping and draws none past the same bound, and calibration starts from the finest drawable size. Client arithmetic: the extent is the whole squares covering the map (`Math.ceil`), as D-094 said and the code did not; fine tuning wraps the offsets into one square, which draws the same grid, so no offset the DM types is refused by the server; the field keeps what was typed. Canvas: on press the pointer is captured on the stage's content, a `pointerup` or `pointercancel` on the window while dragging ends the drag (the release at the last point dragged to, the cancel with nothing), and a move with no button held ends it too; the rectangle is drawn only while measuring; a dark 4 px halo lies under the dashed accent rectangle (as D-093 did for grid lines); while dragging, its size in original pixels is shown beside it (`canvas.measureSize`), kept upright at any zoom; the toolbar help says how to measure while measuring (`canvas.helpMeasure`). Scene panel: one `role=status` region beside the scene's name stays mounted through calibrating, so `Grid calibrated.` is announced where it appears; Calibrate grid refuses while an upload or a save runs (a test now pins it). Calibration panel: two rows, the method radios on one line, then the method's fields with Save calibration and Cancel, with the hint and the result below; its heading and the radios' legend are kept for assistive technology and visually hidden (`.eg-visually-hidden` in `tokens.css`), the strip sitting where Calibrate grid was; each field is described by the method's hint, the error after it (`TextField` now merges a caller's `aria-describedby`); Escape cancels as Cancel does; `Rectangle over squares` is labelled `Rectangle`, its hint saying the rest; the result reads `{size} px squares, first lines at x {x}, y {y}; {columns} × {rows} squares.` Magnifier: 144 px, two squares of the draft a side (between 40 and 144 original px, so 1 × to about 3.6 ×), its caption over the top of the box (the bottom edge is the corner that matters); the caption says while the original loads and, on failure, replaces the box with the reason, in an `aria-live=polite` caption; no grid is drawn before the original has arrived; `.eg-scene__canvas` is at least 15rem tall. `e2e/tests/calibration.spec.ts` now asserts that at 1,280 × 720, unscrolled, the canvas ends within the window, the magnifier lies inside it and the page does not scroll. Test tooling: the fake server refuses as the real one does, a calibration of a map-less scene before the rename, and a calibration value of the wrong kind as `validation_failed` (a parity test runs the same bodies); `images.held` in `ui/testing/canvas2d.ts` keeps an image loading.
Why: Each finding was verified against the code or a screenshot. The high one: at 1,280 × 720 the canvas showed 148 px while calibrating and the magnifier hung over the toolbar, contradicting G-022, and the end-to-end tests passed only because Playwright scrolls; the new assertion failed at 799 px and then at the magnifier's top edge before the layout held. The tiny size was reproduced as a loop that never ends (952 + 1e-14 === 952), reachable by any DM request that bypasses the view; refusing it where the image's size is known, and bounding the magnifier anyway, keeps both the store and the view safe. Konva hears a release only over its own content, so a drag ended over the panel was lost and its rectangle followed the pointer. A live region inserted with its text is not announced by most screen readers. Ten mutations (the window release, the button check, the rectangle after measuring, Calibrate grid while busy, Escape, the magnifier's line bound, and the four server ones the test pass left alive) now each turn a test red.
Alternatives: A schema minimum on `size` alone (rejected: whether a size is too fine depends on the map it calibrates, which only the server row knows); clamping a too-fine size on the server instead of refusing it (rejected: storing a value the DM did not send); ending a drag released outside the stage without reporting it (rejected: the DM meant the rectangle they saw); keeping the panel beside or below the canvas (rejected in D-094); shrinking the scene's heading or the canvas toolbar's help to make room (rejected: PRP-02's, and the calibration strip was the part that grew); a magnifier that collapses on short canvases (rejected: it is required throughout calibration, `06` §1); dropping the magnifier's visible caption (rejected: the loading and failure states need it on screen); refusing offsets beyond one square in the panel (rejected: wrapping draws the same grid and needs no error); the live size in the panel rather than on the canvas (rejected: it would need the drag state lifted out of the canvas for a number the DM reads next to the pointer).
Affected specs: `06` §1, `06` §2, `03` §6, `05` §7, `08` §6, `08` §7, `08` §8.

## D-096 (2026-09-25) — PRP-03 review fixes: a size too fine is refused, the drag ends wherever it is released, one status region, a canvas in view at 1,280 × 720, and the magnifier's states (count corrected)
Type: implementation
Decision: Supersedes D-095, whose record miscounted the mutations that were checked; the decision is unchanged. Refines D-094 after the PRP-03 review (prompt 2, four passes, 2026-09-25: one high, eight medium, eight low findings; no critical and no isolation finding). Server and contract: `MAX_GRID_LINES_PER_AXIS` (2,000) moves to `shared` and the canvas's `MAX_LINES_PER_AXIS` is it; `updateScene` refuses a calibrated size that gives more squares than that across the map's longer side as 400 `validation_failed` with the detail `/grid/size`, thrown inside the transaction so that a rename or a map attached in the same body rolls back too; a size stored before this, or by a request made outside the DM view, cannot hang the view either: `cornerView` counts its lines instead of stepping and draws none past the same bound, and calibration starts from the finest drawable size. Client arithmetic: the extent is the whole squares covering the map (`Math.ceil`), as D-094 said and the code did not; fine tuning wraps the offsets into one square, which draws the same grid, so no offset the DM types is refused by the server; the field keeps what was typed. Canvas: on press the pointer is captured on the stage's content, a `pointerup` or `pointercancel` on the window while dragging ends the drag (the release at the last point dragged to, the cancel with nothing), and a move with no button held ends it too; the rectangle is drawn only while measuring; a dark 4 px halo lies under the dashed accent rectangle (as D-093 did for grid lines); while dragging, its size in original pixels is shown beside it (`canvas.measureSize`), kept upright at any zoom; the toolbar help says how to measure while measuring (`canvas.helpMeasure`). Scene panel: one `role=status` region beside the scene's name stays mounted through calibrating, so `Grid calibrated.` is announced where it appears; Calibrate grid refuses while an upload or a save runs (a test now pins it). Calibration panel: two rows, the method radios on one line, then the method's fields with Save calibration and Cancel, with the hint and the result below; its heading and the radios' legend are kept for assistive technology and visually hidden (`.eg-visually-hidden` in `tokens.css`), the strip sitting where Calibrate grid was; each field is described by the method's hint, the error after it (`TextField` now merges a caller's `aria-describedby`); Escape cancels as Cancel does; `Rectangle over squares` is labelled `Rectangle`, its hint saying the rest; the result reads `{size} px squares, first lines at x {x}, y {y}; {columns} × {rows} squares.` Magnifier: 144 px, two squares of the draft a side (between 40 and 144 original px, so 1 × to about 3.6 ×), its caption over the top of the box (the bottom edge is the corner that matters); the caption says while the original loads and, on failure, replaces the box with the reason, in an `aria-live=polite` caption; no grid is drawn before the original has arrived; `.eg-scene__canvas` is at least 15rem tall. `e2e/tests/calibration.spec.ts` now asserts that at 1,280 × 720, unscrolled, the canvas ends within the window, the magnifier lies inside it and the page does not scroll. Test tooling: the fake server refuses as the real one does, a calibration of a map-less scene before the rename, and a calibration value of the wrong kind as `validation_failed` (a parity test runs the same bodies); `images.held` in `ui/testing/canvas2d.ts` keeps an image loading.
Why: Each finding was verified against the code or a screenshot. The high one: at 1,280 × 720 the canvas showed 148 px while calibrating and the magnifier hung over the toolbar, contradicting G-022, and the end-to-end tests passed only because Playwright scrolls; the new assertion failed at 799 px and then at the magnifier's top edge before the layout held. The tiny size was reproduced as a loop that never ends (952 + 1e-14 === 952), reachable by any DM request that bypasses the view; refusing it where the image's size is known, and bounding the magnifier anyway, keeps both the store and the view safe. Konva hears a release only over its own content, so a drag ended over the panel was lost and its rectangle followed the pointer. A live region inserted with its text is not announced by most screen readers. Eight mutations now each turn a test red: the window release, the button check, the rectangle after measuring, Calibrate grid while busy, Escape and the magnifier's line bound, and the two other survivors of the test pass, a first map attached and calibrated in one body and a drag thin on one axis.
Alternatives: A schema minimum on `size` alone (rejected: whether a size is too fine depends on the map it calibrates, which only the server row knows); clamping a too-fine size on the server instead of refusing it (rejected: storing a value the DM did not send); ending a drag released outside the stage without reporting it (rejected: the DM meant the rectangle they saw); keeping the panel beside or below the canvas (rejected in D-094); shrinking the scene's heading or the canvas toolbar's help to make room (rejected: PRP-02's, and the calibration strip was the part that grew); a magnifier that collapses on short canvases (rejected: it is required throughout calibration, `06` §1); dropping the magnifier's visible caption (rejected: the loading and failure states need it on screen); refusing offsets beyond one square in the panel (rejected: wrapping draws the same grid and needs no error); the live size in the panel rather than on the canvas (rejected: it would need the drag state lifted out of the canvas for a number the DM reads next to the pointer).
Affected specs: `06` §1, `06` §2, `03` §6, `05` §7, `08` §6, `08` §7, `08` §8.

## D-097 (2026-09-25) — Token numbers issued are stored with the scene (Q-091)
Status: superseded by D-101
Type: spec-amendment
Decision: `03` §1's Scene row gains the key field `token_numbers` (the highest number issued per asset on the scene, never sent to a client), and its statement's tag gains Q-091; `05` §3 gains a bullet: only the first token of an asset placed on a scene takes the bare name, a later one is numbered even when alone, and the highest number issued for each asset is stored with the scene, so no number, the highest included, is issued twice on it [Q-091]. Migration 0002 adds `scene.token_numbers TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'`, CHECK a JSON object, additive; nothing is backfilled, because the server also reads the numbers the scene's labels carry (D-098). Both files changed through `make unlock`, recorded in `UNLOCKS.md`.
Why: The owner answered Q-091 with A on 2026-09-25 (recommendation accepted). `05` §3 and Q-063 forbid reusing a freed number, and deleting the highest-numbered token would otherwise let its number return, since nothing else remembers it; a column keeps the entity count at eight, which the register locks.
Alternatives: B, a ninth table (rejected by the owner: contradicts the locked "eight entities" bullet); C, deriving the next number from the current labels alone (rejected by the owner: reuses the highest freed number); backfilling `token_numbers` from existing labels in the migration (rejected: parsing labels in SQL duplicates the rule the server applies at every placement anyway); the bare name again once no token of the asset remains (rejected by the owner: a bare label could then name two creatures over time).
Affected specs: `03` §1, `05` §3.

## D-098 (2026-09-25) — PRP-04 lists the data surface (Q-091)
Status: superseded by D-102
Type: spec-amendment
Decision: `13` §5's PRP-04 now reads `Surfaces: data, security, scope, ux`. The field is derived by `make check-docs` from the sections the package cites, and `05` §3 gained a data-surface tag with Q-091 (D-097). Changed through `make unlock`, recorded in `UNLOCKS.md`. `Contract change: no` stays as the plan author wrote it; D-099 records that PRP-04 changes the contract, as D-090 and D-094 did for PRP-02 and PRP-03.
Why: The owner's answer to Q-091 put a data rule in a section PRP-04 cites, so the derived characteristic changed; `check-docs` fails until the field says so, and the review then covers data.
Alternatives: Dropping `05` §3 from PRP-04's citations (rejected: numbering is the package's outcome); tagging the new `05` §3 bullet with a non-data card (rejected: Q-091's surface is data).
Affected specs: `13` §5.

## D-099 (2026-09-25) — A hidden token is numbered only when first shown to players (Q-092)
Type: spec-amendment
Decision: `05` §3 gains a bullet: a token placed hidden takes the asset's bare name and no number, and is numbered by the rules of D-019 and Q-091 only when it is first shown to players, placed visible or revealed; a lone visible token carrying the bare name becomes "<name> 1" only then, so numbering never reveals a hidden token (`04` §4); a label the DM typed is never renumbered [Q-092]. Numbering therefore counts, per scene and asset, the tokens that are visible or no longer carry the bare name; a hidden token still carrying it takes no part, and hiding a numbered token again keeps its label. A reveal that numbers a token answers the renamed lone token too. Changed through `make unlock`, recorded in `UNLOCKS.md`.
Why: The owner answered Q-092 with B on 2026-09-25, not the recommendation: nothing about numbering then depends on a hidden token, in preparation or in play, so LIV-02's `token.add` and `token.setVisibility` can apply one rule without a live exception. Raised by the PRP-04 security review: adding a hidden second token of an asset would otherwise rename the visible first one on the TV.
Alternatives: A, numbering hidden tokens as before and never renaming a visible token when a hidden one is added live (recommended, not chosen: the rule would differ between preparation and play); C, accepting the leak as an exception to `04` §4 (rejected by the owner); D, deferring to LIV-02 (rejected by the owner); numbering a revealed token even when the DM relabelled it (rejected: it would overwrite the DM's own label).
Affected specs: `05` §3.

## D-100 (2026-09-25) — Tokens in preparation: token routes, numbering, the token layer, snapping, placing and the token bar, with the PRP-04 review fixes (PRP-04)
Type: implementation
Decision: Contract (`shared/src/tokens.ts`, a contract change although `13` §5 marks PRP-04 `Contract change: no`: D-078 deferred the token routes to it): `GET`/`POST /api/scenes/:id/tokens` list and place a scene's tokens; `PATCH`/`DELETE /api/tokens/:id` change and delete one, one level of nesting as `/api/sessions/:id` and `/api/scenes/:id` are (D-015, D-078), all behind the DM-session guard of D-076 and none of `PUBLIC_API_ROUTES`. Responses are `SceneToken`: the Token record of `entities.ts` plus `asset: { name, image_id, size }`, joined, since a token holds no image or size (`05` §3). The placement body is strict `{ asset_id, x, y }`: the server generates the id (D-038), numbers the label, sets `hidden` from the asset's `default_hidden` (`05` §4) and puts the token on top (`z_order` = highest + 1); it answers 201 `TokenChange` `{ token, relabelled }`, `relabelled` holding the lone token renamed to "<name> 1" when a second of its asset is shown beside it. The update body is strict with at least one of `x`, `y` (decimals within ±1e6, grid units: the top-left corner of the footprint in squares from the grid's first line, `03` §4), `hidden`, `label` (1–200 characters, no C0 or C1 control character, at least one that is not white space, stored trimmed) and `stack` (`front` or `back`: above or below every other token of the scene, unless already there); `z_order`, `asset_id`, `scene_id` and `id` are refused. A change answers 200 `TokenChange` too, since a reveal can rename the lone token beside it. Deletion takes no body (the client confirms, as D-083's asset deletion) and answers 204. A write to a token of the live scene is refused inside its transaction as 409 `scene_live`, a new error code, changing nothing: live tokens change only by LIV-02's commands (`04` §2); listing the live scene's tokens is allowed for the DM. An unknown scene or token is 404, an unknown asset 400 `reference_not_found`. Numbering (`shared/src/numbering.ts`, pure, used by the server, the DM view's test server and LIV-02's commands): a token placed hidden takes the asset's bare name and no number, and a token is numbered when it is placed visible or revealed while it still carries the bare name, unless the change gives a label (Q-092, D-099); hiding a numbered token keeps its label. Numbering counts the asset's tokens on the scene that are visible or no longer carry the bare name (`numberingPeers`): per scene and asset, the next number is one above the highest of the stored `token_numbers` entry, the numbers the asset's current labels carry as "<name> N" and 1 when any token of it exists, so a scene numbered before migration 0002 or a label the DM typed as "Goblin 9" continues after it; the first token ever gets the bare name; a single counted token still carrying the bare name becomes "<name> 1" when the second is numbered, but only while nothing above 1 was ever issued, so a freed 1 is never given again, and unless "<name> 1" is taken; a relabelled token is left alone; the name used is the asset's current one. Duplicating a scene copies its `token_numbers`; deleting an asset (only possible once no token uses it) removes its entries with `json_remove`. Client: `client/src/canvas/tokens.ts` (pure) maps grid units to world pixels through the grid frame (square = size × display ÷ original width, origin = the offsets likewise; a map-less scene 64 px from 0), snaps a dropped footprint's corner to whole squares or half squares for Tiny (D-023), keeps an Alt drop free to a thousandth of a square, centres a clicked placement on the click before snapping and keeps it on the map (`clampToWorld`: whole steps inside the map's extent, free positions exactly), and moves by keyboard one step after snapping. `TokenLayer.tsx` draws each token over the grid from its asset's display version, the only version requested (`07` §5), covering its footprint, with its label below at a fixed screen size (Q-032); in the DM mode a hidden token is drawn at 0.5 opacity with a dashed accent outline and a struck-through disc on its top-right corner (Q-054), the selected one outlined light over a dark halo; the player mode draws no hidden token, requests none of their images and listens to nothing. `MapCanvas` takes `tokens`, `tokenControls` (select, move, deselect, delete request), `placing` and `toolbar`, the scene's own controls on the toolbar's row before the view's buttons, flowing with them (`display: contents` on the group, which keeps its role): a pointer press selects a token, a drag moves it and its drop snaps unless Alt is held; a click on the map lets the selection go; with a token selected the arrow keys move it one step instead of panning, Escape lets it go and Delete or Backspace asks to delete it; while placing, a click places the token centred on it, Enter places it at the centre of the view, which a ring and cross mark, and Escape cancels, from the map or the placing bar; tokens are neither selected nor dragged while placing or measuring; the help line says which mode is on; the DM viewport exposes `data-tokens` (id, label, hidden, position, screen box) for the end-to-end tests. DM view: `useSceneTokens` reads the scene's tokens when the scene is selected; a failure to read them says so with a button to load them again, and offers no token control meanwhile. It shows a move or visibility change at once and sends each token's writes, its deletion included, one after another, applying only the answer to the latest and showing only the latest's refusal, which also reads the tokens again; a change from a dialog shows its refusal beside the dialog's field instead. The token bar, on the canvas toolbar's row, holds Add token, the selected-token select (labelled for assistive technology only) and, for the selected token, Hide or Reveal, Rename, To front, To back and Delete, small buttons whose accessible names add the token's label ("Hide token Goblin 2", "Delete token Goblin 2"), so none collides with the tree's. Add token opens `TokenPicker`, a modal dialog with the library's search, category and tag filters (`useAssetSearch`, extracted from `Library.tsx`, which now uses it too); choosing an asset closes it, the bar says what is being placed with Cancel placing, and focus goes to the map. After placing, the token is selected; the status region says what each change did (placed, hidden, revealed, renamed, brought to the front, sent to the back, moved to column and row, deleted), since the canvas says nothing to assistive technology. Rename is a dialog refusing a blank label, or the server's refusal, beside the field, and shows Saving… while it saves; Delete asks first, opening on Keep it; focus returns to the control that opened a dialog, or to Add token once its control is gone. While calibrating, the bar is hidden and tokens are drawn without controls. Tests: `shared/src/numbering.test.ts`, `server/src/http/tokens.test.ts` against a real SQLite file, the token_numbers cases of `server/src/db/schema.test.ts`, `shared/src/tokens.test.ts`, `client/src/canvas/tokens.test.ts`, `client/src/canvas/TokenLayer.test.tsx`, `client/src/dm/tokens/Tokens.test.tsx`, and `e2e/tests/tokens.spec.ts`, Phase 2's exit test; the fake server serves the token routes, numbering with the shared rule and restacking as the server does. `e2e/tests/keyboard.spec.ts`'s walk gets a 90 s budget: it reopens the view once per element and grows with the campaigns earlier specs seed, and took 28.6 s of its 30 s default under load while PRP-04 was verified.
Why: PRP-04 (`13` §5) delivers `05` §2–§5, `06` §4 and the preparation half of `04` §2, and with PRP-03 is Phase 2's exit. Item routes at `/api/tokens/:id` follow the precedent D-078 set for sessions and scenes, whose items also live outside their parent's path; the plan's "under /api/scenes/:id" is kept for listing and placing. Joining the asset's drawing fields keeps one request per scene and cannot disagree with the asset, whose image and size every token follows (`05` §5). Letting the server number and set visibility means a client cannot place a hidden-by-default monster visible by accident or reuse a number. Refusing live writes inside the transaction closes the race with an activation. Reading the labels as well as the stored number keeps the rule true for data from before migration 0002 without a backfill, and prevents numbering from producing a label that already exists. Top-left corner coordinates make snapping a rounding of each axis for every footprint. Serialising a token's writes is the only way the server's last write is the view's last move: two PATCHes sent at once can be handled in either order. A struck-through disc stays readable where text markers overlapped when zoomed out (checked on a screenshot at 1,280 × 720); the select is the keyboard's way to choose a token, since canvas shapes cannot take focus. Review (prompt 2, four passes, 2026-09-25: no critical finding; two high, UX): a refused rename showed its message behind the modal, and the token bar pushed the canvas down (at 1,280 × 720 its top went from about 530 px to 575 px, now 489 px, 528 px with a token selected); the mediums were a freed 1 issued again after a rename, deletion not queued behind a token's moves, a failed token list with no way on, Enter placing off the map with no mark, silent keyboard moves, the delete dialog focusing Delete, and, from the security pass, numbering revealing hidden tokens, which the owner answered as Q-092. Ten mutations the test pass left alive now each turn a test red, among them the transactions of placement and change, a refused placement or deletion, and the latest-answer guard.
Alternatives: `/api/scenes/:id/tokens/:tokenId` for item routes (rejected: two path levels, which D-078 rejected for scenes); a separate `GET /api/assets` join in the client (rejected: two requests that can disagree after an asset change); a client-supplied `hidden` or `label` on placement (rejected: the server alone applies `05` §3 and §4, and a hidden-by-default token could be sent visible); a writable `z_order` (rejected: two browsers computing the top from stale lists could collide, and `stack` states the intent); positions as the footprint's centre (rejected: snapping would depend on the footprint's parity); snapping on the server (rejected: Alt is the client's gesture and positions are free decimals by `03` §4); a PATCH per key press sent at once with only the last answer applied (rejected: found in testing that the server could end on an older move); a text "Hidden" tag as the marker (rejected after the screenshot: overlapping tags at fitted zoom); arrow keys always panning and a separate move mode (rejected: an extra mode for the keyboard's one way to move a token); a token list panel beside the canvas (rejected: the scene column is about 500 px wide at 1,280 px); a confirmation checked by the server on token deletion (rejected: nothing cascades from a token).; review: a grab key (Enter) before the arrows move a selected token (rejected: every move is announced and the help line says what the arrows do, and a second mode is one more thing to learn for the keyboard's one way to move a token); a dark underlay behind hidden tokens on busy maps (not taken: the marker and the outline were read clearly on the review's screenshots; the DM view only); strict body checks in the fake server (not taken: body schemas are the server tests', and the fake follows the server where the view depends on it, numbering and restacking); a player projection of `z_order` here (rejected: LIV-02 owns the players' shape, G-025).
Affected specs: `02` §5, `03` §1, `03` §4, `03` §7, `04` §2, `04` §4, `05` §2, `05` §3, `05` §4, `05` §5, `06` §2, `06` §4, `07` §5, `08` §3, `08` §8, `08` §9.

## D-101 (2026-09-25) — Token numbers issued are stored with the scene (Q-091) (reference corrected)
Type: spec-amendment
Decision: Supersedes D-097, whose record pointed at D-098 for the reading of labels, which D-100 records; the decision is unchanged. `03` §1's Scene row gains the key field `token_numbers` (the highest number issued per asset on the scene, never sent to a client), and its statement's tag gains Q-091; `05` §3 gains a bullet: only the first token of an asset placed on a scene takes the bare name, a later one is numbered even when alone, and the highest number issued for each asset is stored with the scene, so no number, the highest included, is issued twice on it [Q-091]. Migration 0002 adds `scene.token_numbers TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'`, CHECK a JSON object, additive; nothing is backfilled, because the server also reads the numbers the scene's labels carry (D-100). Both files changed through `make unlock`, recorded in `UNLOCKS.md`.
Why: The owner answered Q-091 with A on 2026-09-25 (recommendation accepted). `05` §3 and Q-063 forbid reusing a freed number, and deleting the highest-numbered token would otherwise let its number return, since nothing else remembers it; a column keeps the entity count at eight, which the register locks.
Alternatives: B, a ninth table (rejected by the owner: contradicts the locked "eight entities" bullet); C, deriving the next number from the current labels alone (rejected by the owner: reuses the highest freed number); backfilling `token_numbers` from existing labels in the migration (rejected: parsing labels in SQL duplicates the rule the server applies at every placement anyway); the bare name again once no token of the asset remains (rejected by the owner: a bare label could then name two creatures over time).
Affected specs: `03` §1, `05` §3.

## D-102 (2026-09-25) — PRP-04 lists the data surface (Q-091) (reference corrected)
Type: spec-amendment
Decision: Supersedes D-098, whose record pointed at D-099 for PRP-04's contract change, which D-100 records; the decision is unchanged. `13` §5's PRP-04 now reads `Surfaces: data, security, scope, ux`. The field is derived by `make check-docs` from the sections the package cites, and `05` §3 gained a data-surface tag with Q-091 (D-101). Changed through `make unlock`, recorded in `UNLOCKS.md`. `Contract change: no` stays as the plan author wrote it; D-100 records that PRP-04 changes the contract, as D-090 and D-094 did for PRP-02 and PRP-03.
Why: The owner's answer to Q-091 put a data rule in a section PRP-04 cites, so the derived characteristic changed; `check-docs` fails until the field says so, and the review then covers data.
Alternatives: Dropping `05` §3 from PRP-04's citations (rejected: numbering is the package's outcome); tagging the new `05` §3 bullet with a non-data card (rejected: Q-091's surface is data).
Affected specs: `13` §5.

## D-103 (2026-09-25) — PRP-04 review residuals: an underlay under hidden tokens, and the test server checks token bodies against the contract
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-100, taking two review items it had left. In the DM mode a hidden token is drawn over a dark underlay (the canvas halo colour at 0.6 opacity, `token-hidden-underlay`), under its semi-transparent image, dashed outline and marker; the player mode draws none. The DM view's test server (`client/src/ui/testing/fakeServer.ts`) checks token placement and change bodies with the contract's own `TokenCreateBodySchema` and `TokenUpdateBodySchema` through TypeBox's `Value.Check`, refusing what the server refuses as 400 `validation_failed` before anything else; `typebox` becomes a devDependency of the client workspace, at the version `shared` already uses. Tests: `client/src/canvas/TokenLayer.test.tsx` checks the underlay on hidden tokens only; `client/src/dm/tokens/Tokens.test.tsx` "refuses the bodies the contract refuses, as the server does".
Why: The UX pass found a hidden token nearly lost on a busy map at half opacity; a dark underlay keeps it legible without making it look shown. The test pass found the test server accepting bodies the server refuses, so a view sending one would pass its component tests and fail against the server; checking with the contract's schema keeps both on one definition.
Alternatives: A lower opacity for hidden tokens (rejected: harder to see what the token is); a second copy of the body rules in the test server (rejected: it would drift from the contract); exporting a validation helper from `shared` for the test server (rejected: runtime code in the contract root with only a test consumer).
Affected specs: `08` §9, `10` §2.

## D-104 (2026-09-25) — WebSocket rooms, snapshots, versions and reconnection: WebSocket-only Socket.io on the server's port, a snapshot channel, snapshots at the state's version, the players' projection and dropped sockets of ended sessions (LIV-01)
Type: implementation
Decision: Server (`server/src/ws/live.ts`, attached by `buildApp` so every test app has it): one Socket.io 4 server on the Fastify HTTP server, at `SOCKET_PATH` = `/socket.io` on the server's own port (`02` §2), with `transports: ['websocket']` (no long-polling), `serveClient: false`, messages up to 64 KiB, and `destroyUpgrade: false` so Vite's hot-reload upgrades in development are left alone. `allowRequest` refuses a handshake whose Origin is present and is not `http://` plus its own Host, by the same check as REST writes (`isSameOriginHeaders`, factored out of `isSameOrigin`; Q-043, D-076); a handshake without an Origin is accepted, as a REST write without one is, since a browser always sends one on a WebSocket. On connection the socket's Cookie header is read through `auth.sessionOfCookie`, the same parsing and store as `sessionOf`: a valid DM session joins `dm`, anything else (no cookie, a forged or malformed one, an ended session) joins `players`; the handshake's `auth` and query are never read (Q-046). Each socket then receives `scene.snapshot` on the `event` channel. A new channel `snapshot` (`SOCKET_CHANNELS.snapshot`, not a command of `04` §2, so `COMMAND_TYPES` still equals the table) takes no payload; the server emits a fresh snapshot to the asking socket alone, then acknowledges `{ ok: true }`; either room may use it, since the TV needs it after a gap. Commands on `command`: a socket outside `dm`, or whose session is no longer in the store at the moment of the command, is answered `forbidden` in the error envelope before the content is read, so the apply step is never reached; a DM command goes through `dispatchCommand`, which still refuses every type as `command_unsupported` until LIV-02 registers payload schemas; a message without an acknowledgement is handled and answered to nobody. Sessions: `SessionStore.onEnded` tells its listeners the identifiers every time sessions end (sign-out, a PIN change's `endAllExcept`, a first PIN after `reset-pin`); the socket layer keeps the sockets of each DM session and disconnects them at once (`disconnect(true)`), closing G-011. Before Fastify closes the HTTP server, `preClose` unsubscribes, disconnects every socket and closes the engine. Logging: `ws.connected` and `ws.disconnected` with `role` and `address` (Q-041), never the session or the cookie (`07` §8). Snapshots (`server/src/ws/snapshot.ts`, read in one transaction from the database whenever one is sent): the DM's is `{ role: 'dm', scene: { scene, map, tokens } | null }` with the Scene record, its Image and every token in the `SceneToken` shape; the players' is `{ role: 'players', scene: { map, grid, tokens } | null }`, built field by field: `map` is `{ id, width, height, variants: { display } }` (only the display version is fetched, `07` §5), `grid` the scene's Grid (players need the calibration to place tokens even when they do not see the overlay; `visible` tells the player view whether to draw it, `04` §4), and `tokens` the visible tokens only as `{ id, x, y, size, image_id, z_order, label }` (`04` §4, Q-047, Q-032), `z_order` being the rank among the visible tokens from 0, bottom first; no scene id, name, session, asset, hidden flag or notes. `role` tells a client which room the server put it in. Contract (`shared/src/live.ts`): `SOCKET_PATH`, `ROOMS`, `PlayerTokenSchema`, `PlayerMapSchema`, `PlayerLiveSceneSchema`, `DmLiveSceneSchema`, `PlayerSnapshotSchema`, `DmSnapshotSchema`, `SceneSnapshotSchema`, `SnapshotEvent`, `SnapshotAck`, all strict at every level. Versions (D-017, Q-056): the process counter takes version 1 for the state at start-up (`attachLiveSocket` takes one when the counter is still at 0), so no snapshot carries 0; a snapshot sent to one socket, on connection or on request, carries the version of the state it shows and takes none, so no other client sees a gap because a screen connected or asked; an event that changes the live scene takes the next version (LIV-02 onward), and a snapshot broadcast on activation (D-039) is such an event. `buildApp` takes the counter and, for tests, the command validation and apply step. Client (`client/src/live/`, a directory beside the views because both use it): `versions.ts` holds the gap rule, pure: a snapshot sets the version held; an event applies only when it is exactly one above it; any other version, skipped, repeated or lower (a restart), is a gap, after which events are dropped until a snapshot arrives; a lost connection holds nothing until the next snapshot. `connection.ts` opens `io()` on the page's origin with WebSocket only and Socket.io's own reconnection (0.5 s rising to 5 s), requests a snapshot on a gap and again every 5 s while unanswered, reports `connecting`, `connected` or `reconnecting`, and, because Socket.io does not reconnect after the server ended the socket, connects again at once on `io server disconnect`; the socket is injectable (`setSocketFactory`) and the component tests' `FakeServer.install` puts in a scripted `FakeSocket`. `useLive` keeps the connection for a mounted component. The DM workspace keeps one connection: while reconnecting the live bar's status says the connection to the server was lost and that it is reconnecting (catalogue key `liveBar.reconnecting`), and a snapshot for `players` means the server no longer knows the session, so the view goes back to the PIN form. The player view keeps one connection and still shows only the idle screen (LIV-03 draws the live scene); while reconnecting it keeps its picture and says nothing, since nobody operates the TV and the idle screen shows the product name only (Q-025); its `main` carries `data-live` (the connection state) and `data-snapshots` (how many arrived) for the end-to-end tests, as the DM viewport's `data-tokens` does. Tests: `server/src/ws/live.test.ts` (a real SQLite file, a real port and a real `socket.io-client`), `server/src/auth/auth-units.test.ts` (the store's notification), `shared/src/live.test.ts`, `client/src/live/versions.test.ts`, `client/src/live/connection.test.ts`, `client/src/player/PlayerView.test.tsx`, `client/src/dm/DmView.test.tsx` and `e2e/tests/live.spec.ts`. Dependencies: `socket.io` in the server, `socket.io-client` in the client (bundled by Vite, so nothing is fetched from outside the LAN) and in the server's development dependencies for the tests.
Why: LIV-01 (`13` §6) delivers `04` §1, §5, §6 and the transport half of `04` §2, `07` §2, §3, §7 and §8, and closes G-011 and D-068's over-the-wire half. WebSocket only keeps one transport to reason about for the Origin check and the cookie, and a long-polling request would never let a page reach the network-idle state the end-to-end tests wait for; every browser the MVP accepts, the TV's included (`10` §4), has WebSocket. A snapshot request outside the command table keeps `04` §2's rule that the server rejects every command from `players` exact while letting the TV recover from a gap, which `04` §5 requires of every client. Carrying the state's version in a per-socket snapshot is what keeps a TV connecting from looking like a missed event to every other client; taking version 1 at start-up keeps the envelope's minimum of 1 (D-064) without a special case. Reading the session from the Cookie header with the REST parser gives one definition of a DM; re-reading it at each command closes the moment between a session ending and its socket being dropped. Building the players' snapshot field by field on the server, with the stacking order as a rank among visible tokens, is what `04` §4 and G-025 ask: a player learns nothing a deletion would not also tell. `role` in the payload is how a DM view learns that its session ended elsewhere, since the server decides the room and the view must follow it. The TV has no operator, so a reconnecting message there would only be noise on the table's screen. While implementing this, the version counter shared by both rooms was found to reveal hidden tokens once LIV-02's events differ by room; that is Q-093, opened Blocking, recommending one counter per room; nothing in LIV-01 diverges between rooms.
Alternatives: Long-polling with an upgrade, Socket.io's default (rejected: a second transport for the Origin and cookie checks, and pending requests break waiting for network idle); a separate port for Socket.io (rejected: `02` §2 is one process on one port, and a second port is a second firewall rule); a `snapshot.request` command (rejected: `04` §2 lists the commands exactly, and the players room must have every command refused); requesting a snapshot by reconnecting (rejected: a disconnection and a new handshake for every gap, and a log line pair each time); incrementing the counter for every snapshot sent (rejected: every other client would see a gap whenever a screen connected); a snapshot version of 0 before any event (rejected: D-064's envelope requires at least 1); refusing a handshake without an Origin (rejected: inconsistent with REST writes, D-076, and protects against nothing a browser can do); reading the role from the handshake's `auth` field (rejected by Q-046); checking the session only at the handshake (rejected: a command could slip in between a session ending and its socket being dropped); polling the session store for ended sessions (rejected: `07` §2 says at once); sending the players the stored `z_order` (rejected: holes reveal hidden tokens, G-025); sending players the scene's id or name (rejected: not needed to draw, and a scene name can give away what is coming); hiding the grid's calibration from players when the grid is hidden (rejected: tokens are placed through it); showing a reconnecting message on the TV (rejected: Q-025's idle screen has the product name only, and nobody at the table can act on it); keeping the DM view signed in when its socket lands in `players` (rejected: its REST calls would fail one by one instead); the live module under `client/src/ui/` (rejected: `ui` holds shared components and the catalogue, and the connection is neither).
Affected specs: `02` §2, `04` §1, `04` §2, `04` §4, `04` §5, `04` §6, `07` §2, `07` §3, `07` §7, `07` §8

## D-105 (2026-09-25) — The player view's handshake hint puts its socket in players even in a browser holding a DM session (LIV-01 review)
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-104, which stays in force. The player view connects with `auth: PLAYER_VIEW_AUTH` (`{ view: 'player' }`, `shared/src/live.ts`); the DM view sends none. On connection the server reads the hint first: a socket carrying exactly `view: 'player'` is given no session at all, so it joins `players`, receives the players' snapshot, has every command refused as `forbidden`, and is not among the sockets dropped when the browser's DM session ends. Any other value of `auth`, or none, changes nothing: the room still comes from the DM session cookie alone, so the hint can only lower a socket and nothing a client sends raises one to `dm` (Q-046). The client's `connectLive(view, handlers)` and `useLive(view)` take the view, the socket factory receives it, and the player view's connection ignores any snapshot whose `role` is not `players`, a second guard so that a DM snapshot could never be drawn on the TV; the DM view accepts either room's, since a `players` snapshot is how it learns its session ended. Tests: `server/src/ws/live.test.ts` "puts a player view with the DM cookie in players, with visible tokens only", "gives that socket no DM rights: its commands are refused, and ending the session does not touch it" and "never raises a socket: a DM hint or any other view without a session stays in players" (removing the lowering turns the first two red); `client/src/live/connection.test.ts` "opens the socket of the player view as a player view, and never uses a DM snapshot there (D-105)" and "lets the DM view take a snapshot of either room, so it can learn its session ended"; `e2e/tests/live.spec.ts` "a player view opened in the DM browser still joins the players room (D-105)", and the side-by-side test now checks each page's room separately.
Why: The LIV-01 review (correctness pass: high; security pass: medium, latent until LIV-03) found that the DM session cookie is sent from every page of the origin, so a player view opened in a second window of the DM's browser, the usual set-up when the DM's laptop drives the TV over HDMI, joined `dm` and received every hidden token, against `07` §3 ("The player view MUST receive only visible content") and `04` §4. The server cannot tell the two views apart from the cookie. `04` §1 says a socket joins `dm` only when it carries a valid DM session, a necessary condition, so a socket that asks to be a player may be put in `players` although it carries one; Q-046 forbids a client-declared role granting rights, which a hint that can only remove them does not do; and security overrides convenience (Q-069). Giving the hinted socket no session keeps it from commanding and from being mistaken for a DM socket when sessions end. The client-side check is defence in depth only: filtering stays on the server (`04` §4).
Alternatives: A separate Socket.io namespace or path for the player view (rejected: a second endpoint to guard for the same outcome, and a namespace the DM cookie also reaches); scoping the DM cookie to `Path=/dm` (rejected: the DM view's REST calls go to `/api` and its images to `/images`, which a `/dm` path would stop the cookie reaching); filtering the DM snapshot in the player client (rejected: `04` §4 requires filtering on the server); telling the DM to use another browser for the TV (rejected: a hidden-information guarantee that depends on the DM remembering it is none); reading the view from the page URL or Referer (rejected: not sent on a WebSocket handshake reliably, and no stronger than the hint); keeping the session on the hinted socket so a sign-out also drops it (rejected: it would hold DM rights it must not use, and a TV has no reason to be dropped when the DM signs out).
Affected specs: `04` §1, `04` §4, `07` §3, `07` §7

## D-106 (2026-09-25) — LIV-01 review fixes: stray upgrades answered and closed, snapshot requests throttled with back-pressure, connection lines limited, the reason and focus after a session ends, and the connection notice delayed and styled
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-104 and D-105, which stay in force. (1) Upgrades: `attachLiveSocket` listens for `upgrade` on the HTTP server and answers every upgrade whose path is not under `SOCKET_PATH/` with `404` and `Connection: close`, ending the socket (destroyed after 1 s at the latest), except, when the client source is Vite in development, upgrades whose `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` names `vite-hmr` or `vite-ping` (`isViteUpgrade`); `destroyUpgrade` stays off so that the engine's delayed destruction does not race Vite. (2) Snapshot requests: per socket at most one requested snapshot per `SNAPSHOT_INTERVAL_MS` (1 s); a request inside the interval is served when it ends, and further requests meanwhile are merged into it and not acknowledged (the client asks again after its 5 s retry if it must); the snapshot on connection does not count. Before sending a snapshot or answering a command, a socket whose engine.io write queue holds more than `MAX_PENDING_PACKETS` (256) unsent packets is disconnected and `ws.overloaded` is logged with its address. (3) Connection lines: `ws.connected` and `ws.disconnected` pass through a `createLineLimiter` of their own with the rejected-request defaults (20 per address, 100 in total per 60 s window), and one `ws.lines_dropped` line per window gives the number dropped and the addresses; the window is flushed when the server closes. `buildApp` takes `liveLimits` (interval, queue limit, line limits) for tests only. (4) After a session ends: the DM view's sign-in screen carries `ended` when the server ended an open session, from the live socket landing in `players` or from a REST `unauthorized`; the PIN form then shows a notice (`signIn.sessionEnded`, role alert) saying the browser was signed out because the server restarted, the PIN was changed or it was signed out elsewhere, and focus moves to the main landmark; a sign-out from this view shows no notice. Unsaved typing in the workspace (an open dialog, a rename) is lost when the workspace closes, which the notice explains; this is accepted. (5) The live bar's connection text appears only after `CONNECTION_NOTICE_DELAY_MS` (1 s) down, so a blip is not announced; it reads `Connecting to the server…` (`liveBar.connecting`) until a first connection has succeeded (a failed first attempt is `connecting`, not `reconnecting`) and the connection-lost text after one did, in the accent colour and bold (`eg-livebar__text--warning`, the pair accent on background added to the contrast test) instead of the live style. (6) Tests added: `server/src/ws/live.test.ts` "answers an upgrade to any other path 404 and closes it at once", "still closes the server with a stray upgrade having been sent", "serves WebSocket only: a long-polling request is refused", "closes a socket that sends a message over 64 KiB, changing nothing", "sends a requested snapshot to the asking socket alone", "serves a burst of requests as at most one snapshot per interval, without delaying other sockets", "drops a socket that leaves its messages unread instead of buffering for it", "gives a client that reconnects by itself the snapshot of the new state" and "logs a bounded number of connection lines per address and summarises the rest" (removing the upgrade guard, the throttle, the queue check, the line limit or `transports: ['websocket']` each turns a test red); `client/src/live/connection.transport.test.ts`, the gap loop and the reconnection after the server ends a socket over a real Socket.io client and server (`socket.io` added to the client's development dependencies for it); `client/src/live/connection.test.ts` for the first-attempt status and no snapshot request while disconnected; `client/src/dm/DmView.test.tsx` for the delayed and styled notice, the connecting text, the reason and focus after a session ends, the same reason on a REST `unauthorized`, and none after signing out here; `e2e/tests/live.spec.ts` checks each page's room separately.
Why: The LIV-01 review (prompt 2, four passes, 2026-09-25) found: one high in the correctness and security passes, reproduced: with `destroyUpgrade: false` and no other listener in production, an upgrade to any other path kept its socket open for good (500 stayed open after their clients left) and `app.close()` never resolved, so Ctrl-C hung; before LIV-01 Fastify answered such a request 404. The security pass reproduced a medium: one player socket flooding snapshot requests without reading grew the server from 203 MB to 1.5 GB and stalled the event loop for 15 s, delaying the DM's snapshot by 24 s; and a low: connection churn wrote 6,000 lines in 10 s, rotating away the PIN-failure lines `07` §8 wants kept, which G-006's limiter already prevents for REST. The UX pass found that a DM view sent back to the PIN form by a session ending elsewhere left focus on the page body and said nothing, now without any action of the DM's, and that the connection text was styled as live, claimed a loss on a first connection that never succeeded, and flickered on a blip. The test pass found that a snapshot sent to the whole room, polling accepted or no message limit all passed the tests, that the client's gap loop had never run over a real transport, and that resynchronisation was shown only by a snapshot count; a mutation of each fix above now fails a test. A 404 is what the server answered before the WebSocket existed, so nothing a client could do changes. Merging requests keeps a flood to one snapshot a second per socket without refusing a real gap, and the queue check bounds memory whatever a client does not read.
Alternatives: `destroyUpgrade: true` in production only (rejected: the engine destroys after a 1 s delay without answering, and development would still leak every upgrade that is neither Vite's nor ours); destroying stray upgrades without an answer (rejected: a 404 is what Fastify answered before, and a client sees why); refusing snapshot requests beyond a rate with an error (rejected: a real gap after a burst would go unserved until the client's retry, and an error per request is itself output to flood); acknowledging merged requests at once (rejected: 50,000 acknowledgements are the flood again); disconnecting on request count (rejected: counts what a client asks, not what the server holds for it); a cap on sockets per address (not taken: a LAN of a few screens has no need yet and it would not bound one socket's queue); keeping the workspace open behind a notice until the next action (rejected: D-104 rejected a signed-out workspace whose calls fail one by one); a separate reconnecting region (rejected: two status regions for one bar, D-096); announcing reconnection with a separate message (rejected: the bar's text returning to the live scene says it); no delay on the connection text (rejected: a blip was announced twice).
Affected specs: `04` §5, `04` §6, `07` §2, `07` §8, `08` §8

## D-107 (2026-09-25) — One event version counter per room (Q-093)
Type: spec-amendment
Decision: `04` §5's statement "The version counter MUST be one per server process, starting at 1 on start-up" now reads: the version counter MUST be one per room per server process, each held in memory and starting at 1 on start-up; the `players` room's counts only the events players receive, so a player never sees a version skip that another room's event caused (`04` §4) [Q-056, Q-093]. The register bullet (the live version counter lives in memory and restarts at 1, Q-056) still holds and is unchanged. Changed through `make unlock`, recorded in `UNLOCKS.md`.
Why: The owner answered Q-093 with A on 2026-09-25, the recommendation. With one counter shared by both rooms, an event sent to `dm` only (a hidden token added, moved or deleted, `04` §3) would leave a hole in the versions a TV receives, telling a player that something hidden happened, which `04` §4 forbids; separate counters keep both the gap detection `04` §5 requires of every client and that isolation. Raised while implementing LIV-01; LIV-02's events are the first that differ between rooms.
Alternatives: B, no version on the players' events (rejected by the owner: the TV could no longer detect a missed event, against `04` §5); C, one shared counter accepting the leak (rejected: an exception to `04` §4); D, empty events to players filling the holes (rejected: the TV learns when something invisible happens, the same leak as C).
Affected specs: `04` §5

## D-108 (2026-09-25) — Live event version counters, one per room (after Q-093)
Type: implementation
Decision: Supersedes D-017 and refines the counter of D-064 and D-104. `server/src/domain/version.ts` holds `liveVersions`, a `VersionCounter` for each of `dm` and `players` (`createVersionCounters`), in memory, each first `next()` 1. `attachLiveSocket` takes version 1 of each for the state at start-up; a snapshot sent to one socket carries its room's current version and takes none; an event takes the next version of each room it is sent to, so an event for `dm` only advances `dm`'s counter and the players' sequence has no hole (LIV-02 onward emits events). A snapshot broadcast on activation (D-039) is an event for both rooms and advances both. Every client applies the gap rule of D-104 to its own room's versions; a version lower than the last seen is a gap, as a restart resets both counters. `buildApp` and `attachLiveSocket` take `versions` in place of `version`. Tests: `server/src/domain/version.test.ts` "keeps the rooms apart: an event counted for dm leaves the players sequence unbroken (Q-093)", `server/src/ws/live.test.ts` "counts each room apart: events the players room never received leave its version unchanged (Q-093)".
Why: Q-093's answer (D-107) replaces the one process counter of D-017, whose record now misstates the rule. Advancing only the rooms an event reaches is the whole of the change; the snapshot semantics of D-104 carry over per room.
Alternatives: One counter object carrying a per-room map inside it (rejected: the same thing with a less direct interface); deriving the players' version from the DM's by counting filtered events (rejected: needs the history of what was filtered, and a mistake in it would leak the very holes it exists to hide).
Affected specs: `04` §5

## D-109 (2026-09-26) — Live commands, the role-filtered projection of their events and the players' image entitlement (LIV-02)
Type: implementation
Decision: Contract (`shared/src/live.ts`): strict payload schemas for the six commands LIV-02 implements, registered as `LIVE_COMMAND_PAYLOAD_SCHEMAS` in `server/src/domain/commands.ts`: `token.add` `{ scene_id, asset_id, x, y }`, `token.move` `{ token_id, x, y }`, `token.setVisibility` `{ token_id, hidden }`, `token.delete` `{ token_id }`, `scene.activate` `{ scene_id }`, `scene.deactivate` `{}`; coordinates as the REST placement's. `camera.setPlayer`, the ruler and `undo` stay `command_unsupported` until LIV-05 to LIV-07. A token command acts on the token named and is refused as `scene_not_live` (a new error code, with its catalogue message) when the token's scene is not live; `token.add` names the scene it means and is refused the same way when that scene, known or not, is not the live one, so a placement meant for the scene live a moment ago never lands on the one another DM browser just made live; an unknown token or scene to activate is `not_found`, an unknown asset `reference_not_found`. The label and visibility of a new token come from the server as in preparation (`05` §3, §4, Q-092, D-100). Apply (`server/src/domain/live.ts`): each command is one transaction through `server/src/db/tokens.ts`, whose writes now take a scope (`prep` refused on the live scene as before, `live` refused on any other), and `setLiveScene` in `server/src/db/settings.ts`; last write wins, nothing is compared. The apply step answers effects in the DM's terms, or the error envelope having changed nothing; `dispatchCommand`'s apply may now refuse. Hiding a hidden token, revealing a visible one and deactivating with nothing live are acknowledged and tell nobody.
