Projection (`server/src/ws/projection.ts`), run in the same synchronous step: one event per room per command as `04` §3's table has it. `dm`: `token.added` and `token.updated` carry `{ token, relabelled }` in the `SceneToken` shape (the REST answer's `TokenChange`), `token.removed` `{ id }`, `scene.cleared` `{}`, activation a fresh `scene.snapshot` (D-039). `players`: a visible token's add as `token.added` `{ token, relabelled }`, a reveal as `token.added`, a visible token's move as `token.updated` `{ token }`, a hide and a visible token's deletion as the same `token.removed` `{ id }` (Q-083), nothing for a hidden token's add, move or delete; tokens built by `toPlayerToken`, which the snapshot now uses too. The rename of a lone token to "<name> 1" travels in `relabelled` inside the event that caused it, to both rooms, the players' copy filtered to visible tokens (it is always visible, Q-092), closing G-023 without an event outside the table. A player token's `z_order` in an event is its rank among visible tokens after the event: a client inserts an added token at its rank and closes the gap a removed one leaves, so its ranks always equal a fresh snapshot's (G-025). Emission (`server/src/ws/live.ts`): each room's event takes that room's next version (D-108) and goes to every socket of the room, one whose unread queue is over the limit being dropped as for snapshots (D-106); the sender's acknowledgement follows the events. Images (`server/src/http/images.ts`, `isShownToPlayers` in `server/src/db/images.ts`): a request without a DM session is served only the display version of the live scene's map or of an image a visible token of the live scene uses, read from the database on every request; everything else answers the same 404 as an image that never existed. Tests: `server/src/ws/commands.test.ts` (20), `server/src/ws/hidden-information.test.ts`, the harness `server/src/ws/testing/harness.ts`, `server/src/domain/commands.test.ts`, `server/src/http/scene-setup.test.ts` and `e2e/tests/live.spec.ts`, whose DM page speaks the Socket.io wire protocol from its own WebSocket because the view has no Go live control until LIV-04.
Why: LIV-02 (`13` §6) delivers `04` §2 to §4 and `07` §5 for the live scene. Naming the scene in `token.add` is the only way the server can tell an add meant for another scene from one for the live scene; the token commands need no scene because the token names it. Carrying the rename inside the causing event keeps `04` §3's table exact while every room learns the new label at once; a separate `token.updated` for the rename would add a players' event the table does not list. Ranks rather than stored orders are what G-025 requires; defining them as the order after the event lets a reveal between two visible tokens be inserted without any hole. Telling nobody of a change that changed nothing keeps versions meaningful and gives a second DM browser nothing to misread. Reading the entitlement per request is what G-020 requires, and answering a revoked image exactly as an unknown one tells a player nothing.
Alternatives: `token.add` on the live scene without naming it (rejected: a race between two DM browsers places a token on the wrong scene); a separate rename event, or a `token.updated` for the renamed token (rejected: a players' event `04` §3 does not list for a rename); the stored `z_order` to players (rejected, G-025); a players' snapshot on every reveal or hide (rejected: `04` §4 requires reveal as `token.added` and hide as `token.removed`, and a snapshot on hide only would differ from deletion); a `scene.cleared` even with nothing live (rejected: an event that changes nothing); caching the entitlement per scene (rejected, G-020); answering 403 to a revoked image (rejected: distinguishes an image that exists from one that does not); a `scene_live`-style REST error for live commands (rejected: `scene_live` means the opposite, a preparation write refused because the scene is live); a DM-view Go live control now to drive the end-to-end test (rejected: LIV-04's scope); a test-only command hook in the client (rejected: code in the production bundle for a test).
Affected specs: `04` §2, `04` §3, `04` §4, `04` §5, `05` §3, `05` §4, `07` §3, `07` §5, `07` §7, `10` §3

## D-110 (2026-09-26) — Two gates of `10` §3 promoted from tripwires; the recorded-traffic test runs unmarked until undo (LIV-02)
Type: implementation
Decision: `@gate:player-command-rejection` marks `server/src/ws/commands.test.ts` "refuses every command type from the players room over the wire and changes nothing" (every command type with a payload valid for a DM, from a player socket and from a player view carrying the DM cookie, D-105: each refused as `forbidden`, the database, both version counters and every socket's traffic unchanged), and `@gate:image-revocation` marks "refuses an image a player fetched once its token is hidden, the scene deactivated or replaced, or the map replaced"; both run in `make test`. Their entries leave `GATES` in `scripts/tripwire.mjs`, their jobs leave the tripwire matrix of `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and their rows leave the README's job table. The recorded-traffic test of `10` §3 is `server/src/ws/hidden-information.test.ts`, which covers every step of the scripted session but undo and also proves the count: the players' recording with hidden-only steps equals the one without them, identifiers numbered by first appearance. It carries no `@gate:hidden-information` marker, whose tripwire stays and now names LIV-05, since the gate `10` §3 states includes undo, as `PLAN.md` set out for LIV-02.
Why: `PLAN.md`'s LIV-02 acceptance asks for both gates with their tripwires promoted, and for the traffic test with its marker only once undo is covered or by a recorded decision; marking it now would claim a gate that is not whole. Equality with a session without hidden tokens is the only check that also catches a count leaked by versions, ranks, numbering or timing of events, which a search for identifiers cannot.
Alternatives: Marking the traffic test now and adding undo later (rejected: the gate would pass while its specified script is incomplete); keeping the two tripwires until REL-02 (rejected: a tripwire that stays green while its gate exists is the silent pass the tripwires exist to prevent); a Playwright-only traffic test (not taken: the server test records the same frames with less time and no browser, and the end-to-end test checks the player context's snapshot and images).
Affected specs: `10` §3, `13` §3

## D-111 (2026-09-26) — LIV-02 review fixes: numbering counts only tokens players could have seen numbered (Q-094), a failed command answers instead of ending the server, and the tests the review found missing
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-099, D-100 and D-109, which stay in force except for the counting rule named here. (1) Numbering (Q-094, answer A): `numberingPeers` in `shared/src/numbering.ts` counts every visible token of the asset on the scene and a hidden one only when its label is a number of the asset's name (`numberOf`), so a hidden token with the bare name or a label the DM typed takes no part; `updateAsset` in `server/src/db/assets.ts` gives tokens carrying the old bare name the new one in the same transaction, so a never-numbered token stays recognisable and uncounted; numbered and typed labels stay. A number gap left by a deletion, or by a token numbered and hidden again before the scene went live, still shows, as the answer accepts. (2) `server/src/ws/live.ts`: applying, projecting and emitting a command run inside a try/catch; a throw answers `internal_error` and logs `ws.command_failed` with the error's message and never the payload, since Socket.io calls the listener outside any promise and a throw ended the process. (3) Tests: `server/src/ws/commands.test.ts` "delivers the events of its own command to the sender before the acknowledgement", "never counts a hidden token the DM relabelled, nor one whose asset was renamed (Q-094)", the reveal of a visible token added to the no-op case with versions unchanged, and `dump()` reading every table from `sqlite_master`; `server/src/ws/live.test.ts` "answers a command that fails on the server with internal_error, logs it without the payload and keeps serving"; `server/src/ws/hidden-information.test.ts` records a relabelled hidden kobold, an asset rename with a hidden token of it, every image response header but `date`, a final step after which nothing may arrive, and reconnects as the player view in the DM's browser (D-105); `shared/src/numbering.test.ts` the new counting rule; `e2e/tests/live.spec.ts` deactivates and closes its contexts in `finally`, and its raw socket closes on every failure path. Deferred to gaps: an asset's image or size changed while a visible token of it is live, and a deactivation after a REST deletion of the live scene (G-019, G-012, LIV-04); the cost of encoding a broadcast per socket with no cap on sockets per address (G-029, REL-02).
Why: The LIV-02 review (prompt 2, four passes, 2026-09-26) found no critical or high finding. The security and correctness passes each reproduced the numbering leak, a medium against `04` §4, which the owner answered as Q-094. The security pass found that an exception in the command path would end the server and blank every TV (low); the test pass found that reordering the acknowledgement before the events, or emitting them later, survived every test, that a reveal of a visible token was never tested as a no-op, that the gate's "changes nothing" read five of the eight tables, and that a failed end-to-end run left a scene live for the specs after it (mediums and lows). Each mutation the test pass left alive now turns a test red. Carrying the bare name along a rename keeps the one signal numbering has for a never-numbered token without a new column.
Alternatives: Tracking per token whether players saw it (Q-094 B, not chosen by the owner: a migration for a gap players cannot tell from a deletion); renaming numbered labels with the asset (not taken: those labels were shown to players and the DM may rely on them); a process-wide `uncaughtException` handler (rejected: it would keep a process running in an unknown state for every error, where only the command path is at issue); bringing a revealed token to the front so its rank says nothing of where it lay while hidden (not taken: `04` §4 keeps the stacking order and a reveal at its place is what a fresh snapshot shows; recorded as G-030 for the owner); emitting `scene.cleared` on every deactivation to fix the REST-deletion case now (rejected: D-109 keeps no-op commands silent, and LIV-04 owns the deletion push, G-012).
Affected specs: `04` §2, `04` §3, `04` §4, `05` §3, `07` §8

## D-112 (2026-09-26) — Player view drawing and following the live scene, the hidden pointer, and connecting a screen: GET /api/connect, a server-made QR code and the console banner (LIV-03)
Type: implementation
Decision: Player view (`client/src/player/`): `usePlayerLive` keeps the player view's own connection (`connectLive('player')`, D-104, D-105) and holds the scene to draw; `scene.ts` (pure) builds it from a players' snapshot and applies each players' event the connection passes on as exactly the next version: `token.added` (a reveal included) inserts the token at the rank its `z_order` gives and applies `relabelled` as new labels in place, `token.updated` replaces the token at its rank, `token.removed` removes it and renumbers the ranks, `scene.cleared` returns to null (the idle screen), and the camera and ruler events are ignored until LIV-06 and LIV-07; only the seven player fields of a token are kept whatever an event carried, so the drawn ranks always equal a fresh snapshot's (G-025, D-109). `PlayerView` shows `IdleScreen` while nothing is live (Q-025) and otherwise `MapCanvas` in its player mode (D-090) with the snapshot's map, grid and tokens (all visible, as `hidden: false`), which fits the map, draws the grid only when `grid.visible` and requests only display versions. While reconnecting it keeps its last picture. `useIdleCursor(2000)` hides the pointer after two seconds without `pointermove` or `mousemove` and shows it at the next movement, through `data-cursor` on the player view's `main` and a `cursor: none` rule in `player.css` (Q-054). `main` carries `data-scene` (`idle` or `live`) for the end-to-end tests, and the player mode of `MapCanvas` now exposes `data-tokens` as the DM mode does, listing the tokens it draws (visible only) with id, label, grid position and screen box and no hidden flag; PRP-04's TokenLayer test that asserted the player mode exposed nothing now asserts exactly the visible tokens and no hidden flag. Connecting a screen: a new DM-only route `GET /api/connect` (`shared/src/connect.ts`: `API_CONNECT_PATH`, `ConnectInfoSchema`, strict) answers `{ addresses: [{ address, url, private }], qr: { size, rows } | null }`: every non-internal IPv4 address from `os.networkInterfaces()` read at each request (a laptop may join the Wi-Fi after start), deduplicated, the first in 10/8, 172.16/12 or 192.168/16 moved to the front and the rest in the system's order (Q-053), each with `http://<address>:<port>/` (the player view, never `/dm`; port 80 omitted; the port the server listens on), and the first URL's QR code as rows of `0`/`1` modules without a quiet zone. The route is guarded like every `/api` route (it is not in `PUBLIC_API_ROUTES`). The QR code is encoded on the server by `uqr` ^0.1.3 (MIT, no dependencies), error correction M; `jsqr` (Apache-2.0) is a server development dependency only, so the tests read the code back as a phone would. The DM view's live bar gains a Connect a screen button opening `ConnectDialog`, a native modal (D-085) that says it is loading, then shows the URL in large text, the QR code drawn in React as one SVG path dark on white with a four-module quiet zone (`role=img`, named after the URL; no innerHTML), and the other addresses with a hint, or that the PC has no address, or the request's failure. Console at start (`server.ts`): `connectBanner` writes to stdout the URL, the same QR code drawn with Unicode half blocks (light modules drawn, for the usual dark terminal, two-module quiet zone), the other addresses, and on Windows only the advice to allow Node.js on private networks only (Q-076, `09` §4); the URLs are also logged as `server.addresses`. Catalogue keys `connect.*`.
Why: LIV-03 (`13` §6) delivers `08` §4, §5 and §9 and the start-up line of `09` §2. Applying events to state rather than re-requesting a snapshot is what `04` §3 and §5 require, and keeping ranks by insertion is the only way a reveal between two visible tokens lands where a fresh snapshot would put it (D-109). The addresses are known only to the server, so the panel needs a route; `02` §5 lists the resources the API must offer, and a read-only DM route serving `08` §5 adds no capability outside the MVP, so it is recorded here rather than as an amendment of the hard-locked specs. Encoding on the server gives the console and the panel one encoder and keeps the client free of a QR library, and sending modules rather than an SVG string keeps markup from the server out of the DOM. Decoding in the tests proves the code carries the URL rather than that some matrix exists. Reading interfaces per request handles a Wi-Fi joined late without a restart. A QR code is a picture, not a log line, so it goes to stdout directly; the logger would escape its newlines. The firewall advice is printed on every start because the server cannot tell the first; on other systems the prompt does not exist. Exposing the drawn visible tokens is how the end-to-end tests check the TV's drawing, and it carries nothing the player's own snapshot does not.
Alternatives: The connect data inside the DM's WebSocket snapshot (rejected: `04` §3's events and snapshots are the live scene only); amending `02` §5 to list `/api/connect` (not done: the table lists the resources the API must offer, not the only ones, and `specs/**` is hard-locked; an owner can add the row at the next spec revision); a QR library in the client, e.g. `qrcode` (rejected: the console needs one on the server anyway, and `qrcode` pulls in `yargs` and `pngjs`); an SVG string from the server set as innerHTML (rejected: server markup in the DOM); one QR code per address (rejected for now: the panel highlights one address, Q-053, and lists the rest as text); a QR code in the log file (rejected: noise, and the logger escapes newlines); printing the firewall advice on every system (rejected: only Windows asks); validating each players' event with the TypeBox schemas in the client (rejected: the filtering is the server's, `04` §4, the connection already drops any DM snapshot, D-105, and keeping only the player fields bounds what is held; it would add TypeBox's value module to the TV's bundle); requesting a fresh snapshot after every event (rejected: `04` §5 makes events the normal path and snapshots the gap recovery); hiding the pointer with the Pointer Lock API (rejected: needs a user gesture on the TV); reading the TV's drawing only from pixels (rejected: exact labels and ranks are not readable from pixels; the tests read both).
Affected specs: `02` §5, `02` §6, `04` §3, `04` §4, `04` §5, `04` §9, `08` §4, `08` §5, `08` §9, `09` §2, `09` §4

## D-113 (2026-09-26) — Each view's loading and load-failure states come from the entry chunk; the TV retries by itself (G-007, LIV-03)
Type: implementation
Decision: `client/src/boot.tsx` replaces the body of `main.tsx`: it renders `BootScreen` (`client/src/ui/BootScreen.tsx`, in the entry chunk with the catalogue and `Button`) in the loading state at once, then the guarded view when `loadGuardedView` resolves, or `BootScreen` in the failed state when it rejects (the error goes to the browser console only). The DM view's states: a status line 'Loading Emberglass…', then on failure an alert naming the likely cause and a primary Reload button (`window.location.reload()`); class names are `eg-boot*`, so no DM markup enters the player view's scripts. The player view's states keep the idle screen's look, the product name only (Q-025), with no control; on failure it sends `HEAD /` with `cache: 'no-store'` every 5 s (`BOOT_RETRY_MS`) and reloads the page once the server answers 2xx. Both carry `data-view="boot"` and `data-boot` for the tests. The keyboard gate reaches the DM's failure state in the production build by refusing the DM view's chunk with Playwright's routing, checks its contrast, that Reload is the only focusable element, is reached by Tab and shows a focus ring, and operates it with Enter once the chunk is served again; `e2e/tests/player.spec.ts` does the same for the TV, which comes back by itself.
Why: G-007: before, the page rendered nothing until the view's chunk arrived and a failed chunk left the dark background with an unhandled rejection. The failure UI must not depend on another chunk, since a server that stopped or restarted with a new build fails every chunk of the old page. Nobody operates the TV, so its failure state must recover on its own, and a reload while the server is down would replace the page with the browser's own error page, which never retries; asking the server first avoids that. A dynamic import that failed is cached by the browser, so retrying the import in place would keep failing; a reload fetches the fresh `index.html` (served `no-cache`) and the current chunk names.
Alternatives: Retrying `import()` in place (rejected: browsers cache a failed module fetch for the page's lifetime); reloading the TV on a timer (rejected: while the server is down the reload lands on the browser's error page and stops); a message on the TV's failure state (rejected: Q-025, and nobody at the table can act on it); the failure state inside the view's own chunk or the error boundary's fallback chunk (rejected: those chunks fail too); an inline script in `index.html` (rejected: text outside the catalogue, D-073, and no React); a service worker caching the chunks (rejected: new moving part, and a stale cache after an upgrade is its own failure).
Affected specs: `02` §6, `08` §4, `08` §6, `08` §8

## D-114 (2026-09-26) — LIV-03 review fixes: the TV reloads only when that can help, hides the pointer while booting, focus returns after Connect a screen, and the tests the review found missing
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-112 and D-113, which stay in force except for the TV's retry rule. TV load failure (`client/src/ui/BootScreen.tsx`): every 5 s it fetches `/` (GET, `cache: 'no-store'`) and reloads when the page names another entry script than the one it loaded (a new build), or answers after at least one check failed (the server restarted); while the same build answers and the server never went away, it reloads only after a fallback wait of 30 s, 60 s, 120 s, then 300 s for each further such reload, counted in the tab's session storage (`emberglass.bootReloads`, every access guarded) and cleared when a view loads. The player's loading and failure states hide the pointer after two seconds still like the player view: `useIdleCursor` and `CURSOR_IDLE_MS` moved to `client/src/ui/`, and the `cursor: none` rule to `tokens.css` as `main[data-cursor='hidden']` (`player.css` removed). The workspace keeps a ref to Connect a screen and focuses it when the panel closes, by Close or Escape. The panel's loading line is a `role=status` region; the Other addresses heading loses its default bottom margin; the hint ends with a full stop. The player reducer skips a `token.added` or `token.updated` without a token and treats a missing `relabelled` as none, instead of throwing to the error boundary. Tests added: the three retry paths, the bounded fallback and its reset, stopping after unmount with an answer in flight, the boot screen's pointer; focus after Close and Escape; the pointer timer and listeners gone after unmount and `pointermove`; an event before the first snapshot; a snapshot of another map re-fitted with only its images; replayed, unknown and malformed events; an update to another rank; the IPv4 family as a number. `e2e/tests/player.spec.ts` waits for the fitted scale before reading positions (the Windows runner measured the viewport after the first read), and its recovery test refuses one check of `/`, as a restart would.
Why: The LIV-03 review (prompt 2, four passes, 2026-09-26) found no critical or high issue. Its mediums: the correctness pass showed a TV whose chunk kept failing while the server stayed up (a browser missing an API, a refused CSS preload) reloaded every 5 s forever; the UX pass that the boot screens left the pointer on the TV (Q-054) and that focus was not returned to the opener, unlike the other dialogs; the test pass that the cleanup paths were untested (four mutations survived). Comparing the entry script is how a page learns its chunks changed without any version endpoint, and a failed check is the only sign of a restart the TV can see. The fallback keeps recovery from a transient refusal while bounding a broken build to one reload per five minutes.
Alternatives: A version endpoint for the build (rejected: a new route for what the page already names); reloading only after a failed check (rejected: a restart faster than the 5 s check, or a refused chunk with the server up, would never recover); a fixed 5 s reload capped by a counter (rejected: stops recovering altogether after the cap); keeping the reload count in localStorage (rejected: survives into the next evening's session); returning focus from inside the Dialog component for every dialog (rejected: changes dialogs the review did not look at; the other dialogs already return focus themselves); validating event payloads with TypeBox in the client (rejected in D-112; skipping a malformed event is enough to keep the TV drawing).
Affected specs: `02` §6, `04` §3, `08` §5, `08` §8, `08` §9

## D-115 (2026-09-26) — Live and prep modes: REST changes pushed to the rooms by comparing snapshots, the DM view following the dm room, live commands from the canvas, Go live and Blank TV in the live bar, and the owner's answer on reveal stacking (LIV-04)
Type: implementation
Decision: Server (`server/src/ws/live.ts`): `LiveSocket.refresh(work)` runs a REST change and tells each room what it changed of the live scene: each room's snapshot is read before and after, in the same synchronous step (better-sqlite3 is synchronous, so no command can come between); when the change cleared the live scene both rooms receive `scene.cleared` `{}`, otherwise a room whose snapshot changed receives the fresh `scene.snapshot`, taking its room's next version, and a room whose view is unchanged receives nothing. `PATCH /api/scenes/:id`, the three deletions and `PATCH /api/assets/:id` run their database step through it (`registerCampaigns` and `registerAssets` take it as a required argument). So a map, calibration or players' grid change of the live scene reaches both rooms (`04` §10, Q-015); a rename of the live scene only the DM room, since players never see the name; an asset's image, size or bare name reaches both rooms when a visible token of it is on the live scene and only the DM room when all of them are hidden (`04` §4, `05` §5, D-111); deleting the live scene, its session or its campaign sends both rooms the idle state with no id of the deleted scene (`03` §7, Q-031), closing G-012 and G-019; a refused change or a change to a scene that is not live sends nothing. No contract type changed: the events are those of `04` §3 and D-109. Client connection (`client/src/live/connection.ts`): `connectLive` answers `{ close, command, reconnect }`; a command is emitted only while connected (Socket.io would buffer it and replay it after a reconnection) and answered `{ ok: true }` or `{ ok: false, code }`, `network` when not connected or unanswered after `COMMAND_TIMEOUT_MS` = 10 s. DM view: `client/src/dm/live/useDmLive.ts` keeps the workspace's connection and the live scene from the `dm` room (`dmScene.ts`, pure: a snapshot replaces it, `token.added`/`token.updated` replace the token and its `relabelled`, `token.removed` drops it, `scene.cleared` empties it), exposing `current()` and `lastAdded()` read from refs the handlers write as messages arrive; `useLive.ts` is removed. The live bar reads the live scene's name from it instead of REST, so it follows another DM browser (G-018's live-bar half); it holds Go live (primary, named `Go live: <scene>`) when a scene other than the live one is selected, Show live scene when something else is live, which selects it in one click, Blank TV (`scene.deactivate`) while something is live, and Connect a screen; a refused Go live or Blank TV says why in the workspace's notice. The scene panel shows the live scene in live mode: `data-mode` `live` or `prep`, a 4 px outline in the live colour around the canvas (`eg-scene__canvas--live`, an outline so the canvas keeps its size), a short 'Live on the TV' line in the head, and the canvas named 'Live map of <name>: every change shows on the TV'; the scene, its map and tokens come from the live scene, and the REST reads of the scene and its tokens are skipped while live and made again when it returns to prep mode. In live mode the token controls send `token.add` (the placed token is `lastAdded()`: the server emits a command's events before its acknowledgement on the same socket, D-111, so the last `token.added` before the ack is the command's own), `token.move` on a drop or an arrow key, `token.setVisibility` and `token.delete`; a move or visibility change is shown at once as a pending overlay and dropped at the answer, the events having brought the scene in step or the refusal leaving the token where it was, with the reason above the canvas; the token bar offers no Rename, To front or To back (`04` §2, Q-014). Setup edits stay REST in live mode (Q-015) and are shown from the snapshot they cause. G-027: a `players` snapshot in the DM view is checked with `GET /api/auth`; a browser the server still knows reconnects once, and signs out if the socket lands in `players` again. G-030 (Q-095, answer A): a revealed token keeps its place in the stacking order, as `04` §4 and a fresh snapshot show it; the residual that players can tell a reveal from a new placement is accepted. Test tooling: the DM view's fake server applies live commands, delivers the dm room's events before the acknowledgement and pushes REST changes by the same before/after comparison; `FakeSocket.onCommand` and `commands()`. Tests: `server/src/ws/refresh.test.ts` (15), `server/src/ws/hidden-information.test.ts` (setup edits, a hidden-only asset change and deleting the live scene added to the script), `server/src/ws/commands.test.ts` "keeps a revealed token at its place in the stacking order, not brought to the front (Q-095, G-030)", `client/src/live/connection.test.ts` (commands), `client/src/dm/live/dmScene.test.ts`, `client/src/dm/LiveMode.test.tsx` (11), `client/src/dm/DmView.test.tsx` (G-027), `e2e/tests/live-mode.spec.ts` and `e2e/tests/keyboard.spec.ts` "Go live, Show live scene and Blank TV are reached, shown and operated by keyboard alone".
Why: LIV-04 (`13` §6) delivers `08` §2's one canvas with an unmistakable live mode (Q-024), Go live and Blank TV (Q-025), `04` §10 (Q-015) and the idle state on deleting the live scene (Q-031), and closes G-012, G-019, G-024, G-027 and G-030 and the live bar's part of G-018. Comparing each room's snapshot before and after a REST change needs no route to know which fields players see, so it cannot send players a snapshot for a change only the DM can see, which a snapshot to players would reveal through its timing and version (`04` §4, `04` §5, Q-093); the same comparison makes a no-op or refused change silent. `scene.cleared` rather than an idle snapshot is the event Blank TV sends, so both paths look the same to a TV. Reading the placed token from the events rather than extending the acknowledgement keeps `CommandAck` as D-064 fixed it. Refusing to emit while disconnected keeps a command from arriving minutes late, when the DM no longer means it. The prep-mode Go live first sat in the scene's head; at 1,280 × 720 it wrapped the head and pushed the canvas to 581 px, past the 560 px bound `e2e/tests/tokens.spec.ts` keeps (G-022), so it moved to the live bar, which has the room and is where Show live scene and Blank TV already are.
Alternatives: Per-route rules for what each room must receive (rejected: every route would have to know the players' projection, and a missed field would leak or go stale); a snapshot to both rooms on every live-scene change (rejected: players would learn of a change touching only hidden tokens); an idle `scene.snapshot` on deletion (rejected: Blank TV sends `scene.cleared`, and one idle event is simpler for the TV); returning the new token in the command's acknowledgement (rejected: a contract change for what the events already carry); Socket.io's own buffering of commands while disconnected (rejected: replays stale moves after a reconnection); Go live in the scene's head (rejected after the layout check above); a split view with the live scene beside the edited one (rejected by Q-024); keeping REST token reads while live (rejected: the dm room is the live scene's source and a REST read could race its events); bringing a revealed token to the front (Q-095 B, not chosen by the owner); signing out at the first `players` snapshot (rejected: G-027's race would ask for the PIN in a browser that holds a session); reconnecting indefinitely until `dm` (rejected: a session that really ended would loop).
Affected specs: `03` §7, `04` §2, `04` §3, `04` §4, `04` §5, `04` §7, `04` §10, `05` §5, `07` §2, `08` §1, `08` §2, `08` §8

## D-116 (2026-09-26) — LIV-04 review fixes: a demoted DM view applies no players' event, focus after the live bar's actions, the live controls while disconnected, a double live frame, and the tests the review found missing
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-115, which stays in force except as stated. (1) `useDmLive` keeps the room of the latest snapshot and applies events only while it is `dm`: after a `players` snapshot (G-027's window, while `GET /api/auth` is asked) the players' events, whose tokens lack the DM's fields, no longer reach the DM's live scene, which had crashed the view to its error screen (M1). (2) The workspace moves focus once the change an action caused has rendered: to Blank TV after Go live, to the main landmark after Show live scene, to Go live, or Connect a screen when no scene is selected, after Blank TV (M2). (3) While the connection is not `connected`, Go live and Blank TV stay in place with `aria-disabled` and do nothing, so focus is kept and no refusal contradicts the bar's reconnecting line; a refusal of either is shown until the next snapshot, which every reconnection brings (M3). In live mode while disconnected the scene's head says 'Live, not connected: changes are not reaching the TV', the frame is dashed and the canvas's name says the same (`scene.liveOffline`, `canvas.labelOffline`). (4) The live frame is a 6 px double outline, which a focus ring (one 3 px line) cannot be taken for; the head's live line and the heading share one line in `eg-scene__title`, the heading cut short with an ellipsis, so the line never adds a row (M4). (5) The DM view's fake server checks each command's payload against `LIVE_COMMAND_PAYLOAD_SCHEMAS` and refuses `validation_failed`, as the server does (M7). (6) `e2e/tests/live-mode.spec.ts` waits for at least one more TV snapshot and the TV's `data-grid` of `none` after the setup edit; the keyboard test's `tabTo` starts from Sign out, just before the live bar, and gives up after 12 presses, and asserts where focus lands after each live-bar action (M8). Tests added (M5, M6): `client/src/dm/LiveMode.test.tsx` "draws a move before the server answers, and an earlier answer does not take back a later move", "draws a hide at once, and puts the token back when the server refuses it", "goes to Blank TV after Go live, to the scene after Show live scene, and to Go live after Blank TV", "goes to Connect a screen after Blank TV when no scene is selected", "names live mode in the head, and says when it is not connected", "keeps Go live and Blank TV in place but refusing, sends nothing, and clears a stale refusal once back", "applies none of the players room’s events to the DM’s live scene while it asks the server"; `client/src/dm/DmView.test.tsx` "asks the server again at a later race, once a reconnection brought it back to dm (G-027, review M6)" and "signs out when the check with the server fails (G-027, review M6)". Each fix's mutation now turns a test red. The review's lows are deferred as G-032 and G-033.
Why: The LIV-04 review (prompt 2, four passes, 2026-09-26) found no critical or high finding. The correctness pass reproduced M1 in a test; the UX pass found M2 to M4 in the production build at 1,280 × 720 and 1,024 × 720; the test pass found M5 to M8, among them five mutations of live-mode code that no test caught and two races in the end-to-end tests likely on the Windows runner. The owner approved fixing M1 to M8 and deferring the lows. Keeping the disabled controls mounted keeps keyboard focus where it was, which removing them would lose. Scoping a refusal to the snapshot count needs no effect that sets state and clears it exactly when a reconnection brings the fresh state.
Alternatives: Resetting the DM's scene on a `players` snapshot (not taken: the live bar would say nothing is live during the one round trip before a reconnection); hiding Go live and Blank TV while disconnected (rejected: focus would be lost again); a prep-mode line 'Preparing: not on the TV' in the head (tried and rejected: beside a long status it pushed the canvas to 581 px at 1,280 × 720, past the 560 px `e2e/tests/tokens.spec.ts` keeps, G-022; prep mode is told by the absence of the frame and the live bar); a 'LIVE' tab on the frame's edge (not taken: over the canvas's toolbar or the setup row it would hide controls); a timeout on the G-027 auth check (deferred, G-032).
Affected specs: `04` §2, `04` §3, `04` §6, `07` §2, `08` §2, `08` §8

## D-117 (2026-09-27) — Undo: inverses kept per live scene in server memory, a deletion undone by restoring the same token, the history emptied whenever the live scene changes, and Ctrl+Z in live mode (LIV-05)
Type: implementation
Decision: Contract (`shared/src/live.ts`): `undo` carries the strict empty payload `UndoPayloadSchema`, registered in `LIVE_COMMAND_PAYLOAD_SCHEMAS`; its acknowledgement stays `CommandAck` (D-064), and no event or error code is added. Server (`server/src/domain/undo.ts`, `server/src/domain/live.ts`): `inverseOf(command, effects)` gives the inverse of an applied token command from its effects: `token.add` is undone by `token.delete` of the token it placed, `token.move` by `token.move` back to the position before, `token.setVisibility` by the opposite visibility, and `token.delete` by `token.add` in a restoring form that exists only on the server, never on the wire, and puts back the same token (id, label, position, visibility, stacking order; `restoreToken` in `server/src/db/tokens.ts`), so the rooms receive exactly what an add sends and the live token commands stay the four of `04` §2. A command that changed nothing (hiding a hidden token) and a refused command record nothing; `undo` itself is not recorded (there is no redo). `UndoHistory` holds the inverses of one live scene, newest last, at most `UNDO_LIMIT` = 100, the oldest dropped; `createLiveCommands(db)` keeps one beside the database, one per server process (`attachLiveSocket`'s default), so it dies with the process (Q-005). It is emptied whenever the live scene changes: activating another scene, `scene.deactivate` (Blank TV), and the live scene deleted over REST (seen when the history is next used, since the live scene it belongs to is no longer live); activating the live scene again keeps it, and a refused activation keeps it. After Blank TV, going live on the same scene starts a new history. `undo` pops the most recent inverse and applies it through the same steps as the command it stands for, so its effects are projected and emitted as that command's (D-040, D-109); with nothing to undo it is acknowledged and tells nobody, taking no version. An inverse that no longer applies (its token deleted by another DM browser, its asset deleted since, its scene not live) is dropped and refused with the code its command would get (`not_found`, `reference_not_found`, `scene_not_live`), changing nothing. One history serves every DM browser: undo takes back the most recent command on the live scene, whoever sent it. The reveal that undoes a hide keeps the token's label instead of numbering it, since the token was shown with it (`05` §3 numbers a token only when first shown); undoing an add or a reveal is a deletion or a hide, which leave the numbers issued and a lone token's "<name> 1" as they are (Q-063, Q-091, Q-092). Setup edits over REST are not commands and never enter the history (Q-050). DM view (`client/src/dm/ScenePanel.tsx`): in live mode a document-level listener sends `undo` on Ctrl+Z or Cmd+Z without Shift or Alt, the letter read from `key` (an AZERTY Z counts) and, on a layout whose letters are not Latin, from `code` `KeyZ` (a Greek keyboard reports `ζ`); it is ignored in a text field, which keeps its own undo, and while a dialog is open, and not installed in prep mode. The status line says 'The last change on the live scene was undone.' or 'Nothing to undo on the live scene.', told apart by whether the undo's events changed the live scene before its acknowledgement (D-111); a refusal shows 'The last change could not be undone. <reason>' above the canvas. The DM view's fake server keeps the same history. Tests: `server/src/domain/undo.test.ts` (18), `server/src/ws/undo.test.ts` (12), `server/src/ws/hidden-information.test.ts` with undo of each token command, of hidden tokens included, now marked `@gate:hidden-information` (D-110), `shared/src/live.test.ts`, `server/src/domain/commands.test.ts`, `client/src/dm/LiveMode.test.tsx` (5 new) and `e2e/tests/undo.spec.ts`.
Why: LIV-05 (`13` §6) delivers `04` §8 and `08` §3's Ctrl+Z, and the undo step of the recorded-traffic gate of `10` §3 (G-028). Restoring the deleted token itself is what makes an undo restore the database, which the package's acceptance asks for; a plain `token.add` would give a new id and a new number, place the token on top of the stack and take its visibility from the asset. Emptying the history when the live scene changes, Blank TV included, keeps an inverse from acting on a scene prepared again after it left the TV: once nothing is live there is no live scene to undo on, and Q-005 scopes the history to the live scene. Refusing a stale inverse rather than skipping to an older one never undoes a change the DM did not mean. Keeping the label on the reveal that undoes a hide is the only way undoing a hide of a lone "Goblin" gives back "Goblin" rather than "Goblin 2"; the same rename on an ordinary hide and reveal predates this package and is Q-096. Reading the outcome from the events leaves the acknowledgement as D-064 fixed it.
Alternatives: Undoing a deletion with an ordinary `token.add` (rejected: a new id, label and stacking order, and the asset's default visibility, so the database is not restored and a hidden token could come back visible); a new wire command or event for the restore (rejected: `04` §2 fixes the live token commands, and an add's events already say it); keeping the history across Blank TV until another scene is activated (rejected: prep-mode edits made while the scene is off the TV would leave its inverses acting on a different state); one history per DM browser or kept in the client (rejected by D-040: histories that diverge across devices and reconnections); skipping an inverse that no longer applies and applying the next one (rejected: one Ctrl+Z would undo an older, unintended change); recording no-op commands (rejected: the next Ctrl+Z would visibly do nothing); telling the client in the acknowledgement whether anything was undone (rejected: a contract change for what the events already show); matching only `code` `KeyZ` (rejected: on AZERTY that is the W key); a Ctrl+Z hint in the canvas help or the live head (not taken: the head's row is bounded by G-022, and Ctrl+Z is the convention `08` §3 names).
Affected specs: `04` §2, `04` §3, `04` §4, `04` §8, `05` §3, `08` §3, `10` §2, `10` §3

## D-118 (2026-09-28) — LIV-05 review fixes: a move that moved nothing is not recorded, a restored bare-named token takes its asset's current name, one undo at a time, named undo announcements, and the tests the review found weak
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-117, which stays in force except as stated. Server: (C1) `inverseOf` records nothing for a `token.move` whose position is unchanged, which the DM view sends for a drag that snaps back to its square, so the next Ctrl+Z undoes the move before it; the move itself still reaches the rooms as before (D-109). (C2) `restoreToken` gives a token that carried its asset's bare name when deleted the asset's name at the time of the undo, as an asset rename renames its bare-named tokens (D-111); a numbered or typed label is restored as it was. DM view (`client/src/dm/ScenePanel.tsx`): (U5) one undo at a time: Ctrl+Z pressed again before the server answers, and a held key's repeats (`event.repeat`), send nothing; (U3) while the live connection is not `connected` nothing is sent and the status line says 'Not connected: nothing was undone. Try again once the connection is back.', with no alert, as D-116 keeps refusals from contradicting the reconnecting line; (U6) nothing is sent while the calibration panel is open; (U4) a refusal with `not_found`, `reference_not_found` or `scene_not_live`, meaning the inverse no longer applied and was dropped, reads 'That change can no longer be undone, so it was skipped. Ctrl+Z again undoes the change before it.', any other refusal 'The last change could not be undone. <reason>'; (U11) the status line is cleared when an undo is sent; (U1) every announcement of the scene's status line is a new node (keyed by a counter), so a message equal to the last one is read out again; (U2) the answer names what the undo's events changed, compared token by token before and after its acknowledgement: 'Undone: <label> removed.', 'is back.', 'is back, hidden.', 'hidden again.', 'shown again.', 'moved back to column C, row R.', 'Nothing to undo on the live scene.' when nothing changed, and the generic line otherwise (catalogue keys `scene.undone*`, `scene.undoOffline`, `scene.undoStale`). The DM view's fake server keeps its history per live scene, emptied when used with another scene live, capped at 100, refuses a restore whose asset is gone and follows C1 and C2 (T5, C3). Tests: `server/src/domain/undo.test.ts` (C1, C2), `server/src/ws/undo.test.ts` (the DM room's copy rebuilt from the events and compared with a fresh snapshot after each undo, T3; invalid `undo` payloads refused and the history left to the next valid undo, T6), `server/src/ws/commands.test.ts` (invalid `undo` payloads in the envelope list), `client/src/dm/LiveMode.test.tsx` (7 new or changed), and `e2e/tests/undo.spec.ts`, which now checks the hidden token's position on the DM canvas before and after its undo, waits for each move's server-confirmed announcement before Ctrl+Z (T1), and checks that Ctrl+Z while preparing another scene, with a move in the live history, sends nothing before the Blank TV sent after it on the same socket and leaves the move standing (T2). Each client guard and both server fixes were removed in turn and a test turned red.
Why: The LIV-05 review (prompt 2, four passes, 2026-09-27) found no critical or high finding; the owner approved fixing the ten medium ones and deferring the lows (G-034). C1 contradicted D-117's rule that a command that changed nothing records nothing; C2 let an undo restore a label numbering then took for a typed one (Q-094). A held Ctrl+Z could unwind many changes on the TV, a hide turning back into a reveal, before the DM saw any of them. The canvas says nothing to assistive technology, so an undo must say which token it changed. 'Reload and try again' was wrong advice for a skipped undo, and a network alert contradicted the offline head.
Alternatives: Refusing a no-op `token.move` or sending it to nobody (not taken: a change to D-109's events outside LIV-05's scope; recording no inverse is enough for undo); keeping a restored token's old bare name (rejected, C2); queuing Ctrl+Z presses made while an undo is in flight (rejected: a burst still unwinds several changes unseen); allowing key repeat (rejected, U5); sending the undo while disconnected and showing the `network` refusal (rejected, U3 and D-116); a Ctrl+Z inside calibration that undoes the draft (not taken: the calibration panel has no history of its own; recorded as out of scope); comparing only events that arrived after the send to decide the message (deferred with the race it addresses, G-034).
Affected specs: `04` §8, `05` §3, `08` §3, `08` §8

## D-119 (2026-09-28) — Cameras: the player camera as a world rectangle in server memory, reset on every activation and on a new map, steered by the TV frame, whose shape follows the screen connected longest (LIV-06)
Type: implementation
Decision: Contract (`shared/src/live.ts`): `PlayerCameraSchema` `{ centre_x, centre_y, width, height }`, strict, is the rectangle of the live scene's world every screen must show, as fractions of the world's width and height: the centre within [0, 1] (on the map) and each side within `CAMERA_BOUNDS` [0.001, 100]. `FIT_CAMERA` `{ 0.5, 0.5, 1, 1 }` is the whole map, or the whole grid extent of a map-less scene. `camera.setPlayer` carries `{ scene_id, camera }` (`CameraSetPlayerPayloadSchema`, in `LIVE_COMMAND_PAYLOAD_SCHEMAS`). `camera.player` carries `{ camera }` to players (`PlayerCameraPayloadSchema`) and `{ camera, screen }` to the DM room (`DmCameraPayloadSchema`). Both rooms' live scenes carry `camera` in their snapshot; the DM's also carries `screen` (`ScreenSchema` `{ width, height }`, integers 1 to 32,768, or null). A new socket channel `viewport` (`SOCKET_CHANNELS.viewport`, not a command of `04` §2) takes a `Screen` and answers `ViewportAck`. Server (`server/src/domain/camera.ts`, `server/src/domain/live.ts`, `server/src/ws/live.ts`, `snapshot.ts`, `projection.ts`): `PlayerCameraState` holds the camera for the live scene id it was set on and answers `FIT_CAMERA` for any other, so a live scene replaced or deleted by any path starts fitted; `createLiveCommands` keeps it beside the undo history and resets it on every successful `scene.activate` (the live scene's own included) and `scene.deactivate`, one per server process, never stored (Q-038, D-018). `camera.setPlayer` is refused as `scene_not_live` unless it names the live scene (A2, as `token.add`, D-109). A camera equal to the current one is acknowledged and tells nobody. Otherwise it answers the effect `camera`, projected as `camera.player` to both rooms, one version each (D-108), and it never enters the undo history (`04` §2). `refresh` fits the camera again when a REST change gives the live scene another map, or a first map (A1); a recalibration or the players' grid keeps it, and the snapshot the change sends carries the reset camera. A map-less scene's extent cannot change while it has no map (`calibration_needs_map`), so the map is the world's only key. Frame shape, the owner's choice in this session (2026-09-28): each player view reports its window size on `viewport` when it connects, on every reconnection and once a resize has settled (250 ms). The server accepts reports from `players` sockets only (`forbidden` from `dm`), validates each strictly (`validation_failed`, ignored) and takes at most one per socket per snapshot interval, the latest winning. `ScreenRegistry` keeps each socket's report in connection order and forgets it when the socket goes. The TV frame follows the screen connected longest among those that reported, so a phone opened later changes nothing; every other screen shows at least the frame, since a screen draws the camera as large as it fits, centred. When that screen's shape changes while a scene is live (a report, or the screen leaving), the DM room alone receives `camera.player` with the camera unchanged and the new `screen`; players never receive a screen. Client (`client/src/canvas/geometry.ts`, `MapCanvas.tsx`, `client/src/dm/ScenePanel.tsx`, `client/src/player/`): `viewOf` draws a camera on a screen, and `FIT_CAMERA` draws exactly `fitCamera`. `frameOf` widens the camera to the TV's shape (`screenAspect`: the reported screen's width ÷ height, clamped to [¼, 4], 16:9 while none has reported). `rectOf` turns a frame back into a camera within the bounds, rounded to 1e-6. `resizeBox` resizes from a corner keeping the shape and the opposite corner, never below 5 % of the world's larger side. `frameForKey` handles the keys. The player view draws `viewOf(scene.camera)`. In live mode the DM canvas draws the frame in live mode's colour, with a halo and a TV label: its border drags to pan and its four corner handles resize it, sent on the drop (A3, as a token's move, `04` §7). The Steer the TV toggle (`aria-pressed`) in the canvas's view group makes the arrow keys move the frame an eighth of its size (Shift, half), + and − zoom the TV, 0 fit it to the map and Escape stop steering, and turns Zoom in, Zoom out and Fit into their TV versions. Each key or button press sends at once. The DM's own camera never moves. The frame stays where it was put until the answer, and a refusal puts it back and says why ('The TV's view was not changed. <reason>'); a success says 'The TV now shows the part of the map in the frame.' or 'The TV now shows the whole map.'. The DM's live scene follows `camera.player`, so the frame follows another DM browser. The viewport carries `data-tv-frame` and `data-tv-steering` for the end-to-end tests. Tests: `server/src/domain/camera.test.ts` (12), `server/src/ws/camera.test.ts` (11), `server/src/ws/hidden-information.test.ts` (two camera steps with viewport reports), `server/src/ws/commands.test.ts` (the player-command gate sends `camera.setPlayer` with a valid payload), `shared/src/live.test.ts`, `server/src/domain/commands.test.ts`, `client/src/canvas/geometry.test.ts` (7 new), `client/src/dm/LiveMode.test.tsx` (6 new), `client/src/player/PlayerView.test.tsx` (2 new), `client/src/player/scene.test.ts`, `client/src/dm/live/dmScene.test.ts`, `client/src/live/connection.test.ts` (2 new) and `e2e/tests/camera.spec.ts`.
Why: LIV-06 (`13` §6) delivers `04` §9, the camera rows of `04` §2 and §3, the camera in `04` §5 and §6's snapshots, and `08` §2's frame (Q-080, D-046). Fractions of the world mean the same part of the map on every screen whatever its pixels, the display version's size or a map-less scene's square size, so the camera needs no screen to be defined and fit-to-map is one constant. A rectangle that each screen fits, rather than a centre and a scale, is what lets screens of different shapes all show at least what the DM framed. Keying the camera by the live scene id makes every path that changes the live scene start fitted, as keying the undo history does (D-117). The frame's shape comes from the screens because D-046 keeps the TV's aspect ratio and the plan asks for the frame to follow the TV's viewport. The owner chose, over a fixed 16:9, that the player view reports its size. The screen connected longest is the stable choice with several screens: the table's TV is usually connected first and stays. A report is not a command, so the player-command gate of `10` §3 is untouched, and it only ever reaches the DM room. A new map is a different world, and a camera framed on the old one would show an arbitrary part of the new one; the TV fits it as it would on going live. Naming the scene in the command keeps a frame dragged for the scene just replaced off the next one's TV.
Alternatives: A fixed 16:9 frame with no report from the player view (offered to the owner, not chosen: the frame would not follow a 4:3 screen or a browser whose toolbar takes room, though no new message would come from players). The frame following the most recently connected screen (rejected: a phone opened to look at the table would reshape the DM's frame) or the largest screen by pixels (rejected: a laptop's browser can be larger than a TV's reported viewport). A camera as a centre and a zoom scale (rejected: meaningless across screens of different sizes). A camera in grid units, as token positions are (rejected: a recalibration would move what the TV shows, and the calibration is in the original's pixels while the canvas draws the display version). A camera in display-version pixels (rejected: a regenerated display version, G-015, would move it). Keeping the camera across a new map (rejected, A1). Sending the camera continuously while dragging (not taken: `04` §7 sends a drag on the drop, and a stream of commands would fill the players' version counter). A `camera.setPlayer` without the scene (rejected, A2). Carrying the screen in the players' events (rejected: players need nothing of other screens). A separate DM event for the screen (rejected: `04` §3 lists the events exactly, and `camera.player` to the DM room with its screen is a change to what the TV sees). Steering by keyboard through separate buttons for each direction (rejected: the toolbar's row is bounded, G-022; one toggle reuses the canvas's keys and view buttons).
Affected specs: `04` §2, `04` §3, `04` §5, `04` §6, `04` §7, `04` §9, `08` §2, `08` §4, `08` §8, `10` §3

## D-120 (2026-09-28) — LIV-06 review fixes: a live-mode fit that shows the whole TV frame, frame limits the TV can show, the DM room told of screens at most once per interval, and steering that sends once, stops when it should and says so offline
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-119, which stays in force except as stated. (U-H1) In live mode the DM's own view fits `liveFitBox`: the union of the map and the TV's fitted frame (`frameOf(FIT_CAMERA)` in the TV's shape), widened by `LIVE_FIT_MARGIN` = 1.1, through the new `fitBox` (`fitCamera` is now `fitBox` of the whole world). So every edge and corner of the frame is inside the canvas when the scene goes live, and Fit (button or 0) returns there. The fit depends only on the world and the TV's shape, never on the player camera, so steering never moves the DM's view. A new screen shape refits it while the DM has not moved their own view. (C-M1, C-M2) `frameLimits(world, aspect, screen)` bounds the frame's width in world pixels. The lower bound is the TV's width ÷ `MAX_SCALE` (1920 CSS pixels while no screen has reported), 5 % of the world's larger side, and `CAMERA_BOUNDS.minSize`. The upper bound is the TV's width ÷ `MIN_SCALE` and `CAMERA_BOUNDS.maxSize` on either side. `boundFrame` applies the bounds by one factor about the centre, keeping the TV's shape. It applies to every drop, key and button, to `resizeBox` (which now takes the limits) and to drawing another browser's camera, so the frame never shows less than the TV does. A camera equal to the current one is not sent: a drop in place, 0 when already fitted, zooming out at the limit. (S-M1) The DM room hears of the chosen screen's shape at most once per snapshot interval in all, however many player sockets report or leave. `tellShape` sends the shape chosen by then, and only when it differs from the one the DM room was last told in a snapshot or a `camera.player`. Nothing is scheduled once the server is closing. The oldest-socket rule is unchanged: its residuals are G-035. (U-M1) A held key's repeats send nothing while steering. (U-M2) While the live connection is down the frame is drawn at half opacity and cannot be dragged. A key or button then sends nothing and the status line says 'Not connected: the TV's view was not changed. Try again once the connection is back.' (`scene.tvOffline`), with no alert, as for undo (D-116, D-118). (U-M3) Steering ends whenever the frame goes: Blank TV, another scene live, the calibration panel. It also ends when placing starts, and the toggle is pressed only while steering is in effect and shown only when the frame is. (U-M4) Choosing a token, on the canvas or in the token bar, ends steering, so the arrow keys move the token. (U-M5) T on the canvas turns steering on and off, and the help lines say so ('The frame labelled TV …', no longer by colour). (U-M6) The pressed toggle keeps the ordinary button face with an inset ring and a dot in live mode's colour, so it never reads as the danger button. Test tooling: the jsdom canvas stub answers `getTransform`, which Konva uses to draw a semi-transparent shape. Tests: `client/src/canvas/geometry.test.ts` (limits, bound frame, live fit), `client/src/dm/LiveMode.test.tsx` (the frame inside the canvas after Go live and after Fit; gone while another scene is shown, after Blank TV and while calibrating, T-M2; held until the answer while another browser steers, T-M1; never smaller than the TV can zoom to; nothing sent when unchanged; one command per held key; steering ended by choosing a token and by Blank TV; offline; T), `server/src/ws/camera.test.ts` (twenty screens changing the chosen one reach the DM room as at most three events, ending on the right shape) and `e2e/tests/camera.spec.ts`, which now grabs the frame straight after Go live and checks that it lies inside the canvas.
Why: The LIV-06 review (prompt 2, four passes, 2026-09-28) found one high and eleven medium findings, and no critical one. The owner approved fixing the high and the mediums and deferring the lows (G-035). With the DM's view fitted to the map alone, the 16:9 frame of a fitted TV lay on or past the canvas edge, so the steering control could not be grabbed without zooming out first. A frame smaller than the TV's zoom limit, or one whose sides clamped separately, told the DM something different from what players saw. Any LAN browser could make the DM room take a version per report. The other fixes bring the frame in line with how D-116 and D-118 treat the live token controls and undo.
Alternatives: Fitting the DM's view to the frame only when it exceeds the map (not taken: the frame's edges would still sit on the canvas edge when map and frame coincide); following the player camera with the DM's view (rejected: `04` §9 keeps the cameras independent). Clamping only the camera's fractions (rejected, C-M2). A cap on the number of screens kept (not taken: memory is already bounded by the sockets, and a cap filled by early sockets would keep the TV out). Letting the DM choose which reported screen is the TV (not taken: a new control and a new message outside LIV-06's acceptance; G-035). Debouncing announcements (not taken: with repeats ignored each announcement is one deliberate press, as token nudges are). Moving focus to the canvas when steering is turned on (not taken: a toggle keeps focus; T on the canvas is the shortcut).
Affected specs: `04` §9, `08` §2, `08` §8, `07` §3

## D-121 (2026-09-28) — Ruler: whole squares, the distance counted on the server, the measurement in server memory for the live scene and what takes it off the TV, feet per square in the scene update and setup (LIV-07)
Type: implementation
Decision: Contract (`shared/src/live.ts`, `shared/src/ruler.ts`, `shared/src/campaigns.ts`): a measurement's ends are squares, `RulerSquareSchema` `{ column, row }`, integers within ±100,000 in the grid's own units (a square before a map's first grid line is negative), and the line runs between their centres (Q-048). `ruler.update` carries `{ scene_id, from, to }` and `ruler.clear` `{ scene_id }`, both strict and in `LIVE_COMMAND_PAYLOAD_SCHEMAS`, which now names every command of `04` §2. `ruler.shown` carries `{ ruler: Measurement }` and `ruler.cleared` `{}` to both rooms alike, `MeasurementSchema` being `{ from, to, feet }`: two squares and the distance, no scene, no token. Both rooms' live scenes carry `ruler: Measurement | null` in their snapshots. `rulerSquares` and `rulerFeet` count the squares moved (PHB: the larger offset; DMG: straight + diagonals + ⌊diagonals ÷ 2⌋) times the scene's feet per square, rounded to 1e-6 ft (D-045). `SceneGridUpdateSchema` takes `feet_per_square` within `FEET_PER_SQUARE_BOUNDS` [0.5, 100,000]. Server (`server/src/domain/ruler.ts`, `live.ts`, `server/src/ws/live.ts`, `snapshot.ts`, `projection.ts`, `server/src/db/campaigns.ts`): `RulerState` holds the path for the live scene id it was made on and answers null for any other, and remembers the DM socket that sent it; the distance is counted whenever a snapshot or an event is built, from `settings.ruler_rule` and the live scene's feet per square, so a new scale reaches both rooms in the snapshot its REST change pushes. `ruler.update` and `ruler.clear` are refused as `scene_not_live` unless they name the live scene, so a measurement on a prepared scene never leaves the DM view (Q-086); a path already shown, or a clear with nothing shown, is acknowledged and tells nobody; neither enters the undo history (`04` §2). Each change reaches both rooms once, one version each (D-108). What takes a measurement off the TV besides `ruler.clear`, the decision the plan asked for: every `scene.activate` (another scene or the live one again) and `scene.deactivate`, whose snapshot or `scene.cleared` carries no ruler; a REST change giving the live scene another map, as for the camera (D-119), while a recalibration or the players' grid keeps it; and the disconnection of the DM socket that drew it (`LiveCommands.release`), which sends `ruler.cleared` to both rooms, so a DM laptop that sleeps mid-measurement or a session that ends leaves no line on the TV; the last DM socket to measure owns the line, and any DM socket's clear clears it. A screen reconnecting, or asking for a snapshot after a gap, keeps the line: its snapshot carries it. `PATCH /api/scenes/:id` writes `grid.feet_per_square` on any scene, a map-less one included; with a calibration it is part of the grid the preset copies (D-094), and alone on a scene whose map has a preset it becomes the preset's feet per square, so later scenes of the map measure the same; a map with no preset yet gets none, and the next calibration carries it. Client (`client/src/canvas/ruler.ts`, `MapCanvas.tsx`, `client/src/dm/ScenePanel.tsx`, `client/src/player/`, `client/src/dm/live/dmScene.ts`): both canvas modes draw a measurement as a line between square centres over a dark halo, its ends marked and its distance ('{feet} ft') beside the end, upright at any zoom and larger on the TV. The DM canvas's Ruler toggle (`aria-pressed`) or M turns measuring on: a drag measures from the square pressed to the square under the pointer, instead of panning, reported on each new square and on release; tokens and the TV frame are left alone; arrows move the end a square, Shift the whole ruler, Enter starts again at the centre of the view, Escape clears and then stops measuring. Measuring, steering the TV, placing, calibrating and a selected token take the pointer and the keys in turn. In prep mode the measurement stays in the panel, counted by the rule `GET /api/settings` answered and the scene's feet per square; nothing is sent. In live mode each new path sends `ruler.update` and clearing or turning the ruler off sends `ruler.clear`, at most one ruler command in flight with the latest waiting, so a fast drag sends a command per answer, not per square; the canvas shows what was sent until it is answered, then what the `dm` room says the TV shows, another DM browser's measurement included; a refusal says why ('The measurement was not shown on the TV. <reason>'); while not connected nothing is sent and the status line says so. Going live or back to prep drops the other mode's measurement, and leaving the scene takes this panel's measurement off the TV. The status line announces each finished measurement ('Distance: {feet} ft.') and a clear. Feet per square is a number field in the scene's setup, its label beside it in two short lines so the setup keeps one row at 1,280 × 720 (G-022), saved on Enter and when focus leaves it, refused before sending outside its bounds. The canvases carry `data-ruler` (squares, feet, ends on screen) and the DM canvas `data-ruler-tool` for the end-to-end tests; a measurement names no token. Tests: `shared/src/ruler.test.ts` (6), `shared/src/live.test.ts`, `shared/src/campaigns.test.ts`, `server/src/domain/ruler.test.ts` (5), `server/src/domain/commands.test.ts`, `server/src/ws/ruler.test.ts` (10), `server/src/http/scene-setup.test.ts` (4 new), the gates `server/src/ws/hidden-information.test.ts` (four ruler steps, one on a scene that is not live) and `server/src/ws/commands.test.ts` (valid ruler payloads from player sockets, refused), `client/src/canvas/ruler.test.ts` (6), `client/src/canvas/MapCanvas.test.tsx` (5 new), `client/src/dm/LiveMode.test.tsx` (12 new), `client/src/player/PlayerView.test.tsx` (2 new), `client/src/player/scene.test.ts`, `client/src/dm/live/dmScene.test.ts` and `e2e/tests/ruler.spec.ts`.
Why: LIV-07 (`13` §6) delivers `06` §5 and `04` §11 with the ruler rows of `04` §2 and §3 (Q-027, Q-037, Q-048, Q-086, Q-087) and closes G-021. Whole squares are what Q-048 measures between, and they keep a measurement meaning the same squares when the map is recalibrated, as token positions do (`03` §4). Counting on the server keeps the TV free of the rule and the scale and keeps the server the only source of truth (`02` §3); one shared function makes the DM view's number the TV's. `04` §11 says measurements are never stored; memory is not storage, and without it a TV reconnecting mid-measurement, or asking for a snapshot after a gap, or receiving a live setup edit's snapshot, would lose a line the DM still shows. A line nobody is holding should not stay on the table's TV, which is why the drawing socket's going clears it. Naming the scene keeps a measurement made a moment before another activation off the next scene's TV, as D-109 and D-119 do. Feet per square belongs to the map's scale as much as its square size does, so a preset that exists takes it; a visibility change alone still writes no preset (D-094). One command in flight bounds the players' versions and the traffic a drag makes, since squares change faster than a round trip. The Set button the field first had wrapped the setup onto a third row at 1,280 × 720, pushing the canvas 107 px lower than `e2e/tests/tokens.spec.ts` and `e2e/tests/live-mode.spec.ts` allow (G-022).
Alternatives: Ends in world pixels or decimal grid units (rejected: Q-048 measures square centres, and pixels move with the display version). The DM client sending the distance (rejected: the server would show players a number it did not check) or the TV counting it (not taken: players would need the rule, and two counts could disagree). No server state, each screen keeping what it last heard (rejected: a reconnection, a gap's snapshot or a live setup edit would drop the line on one screen only). Keeping the line when the drawing DM socket goes (not taken: a sleeping laptop would leave it on the TV until someone found the Blank TV button). Clearing on every refresh snapshot (rejected: renaming an asset would take the line off). A `ruler.clear` without the scene (rejected, as for `camera.setPlayer`). Sending every square of a drag (not taken: a version per square per room; the latest one is what the TV must show). Writing feet per square into the preset only through a calibration (not taken: a scale set after calibrating would never reach later scenes of the map). Feet per square in the calibration panel only (rejected: a map-less scene has no calibration). A Set button beside the field (tried, rejected: G-022's layout budget). Streaming the measurement with a throttle instead of one in flight (not taken: a timer adds latency and still queues on a slow link).
Affected specs: `06` §5, `04` §2, `04` §3, `04` §5, `04` §11, `03` §5, `03` §6, `08` §3, `08` §8, `10` §3

## D-122 (2026-09-28) — LIV-07 review fixes: ending measuring takes the line off, panning while measuring, a distance label kept on screen, one clear only for this panel's own line, announcements once shown, and the feet field saved without surprises
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-121, which stays in force except as stated. (C-M1) Choosing a token ends measuring through the same step as every other way of ending it, so the measurement leaves the DM canvas and the TV. (U-M2) While measuring, a drag with the right or middle button pans the DM's view and measures nothing, and the canvas opens no context menu; both ruler help lines say so. (U-M3) `labelOffset` (`client/src/canvas/ruler.ts`) places the distance label above and right of the end, flipped below near the top of the view and to the left near its right edge, its width estimated from the text, in both views. (C-L1) Going back to prep mode empties the ruler command waiting, a command still in flight sends nothing after it, and its refusal shows nothing over the prep-mode panel. (C-L2) Leaving the scene sends `ruler.clear` only while the TV shows the path this panel last had accepted, so another DM browser's line stays. (C-L3) `isMeasurement` (`shared/src/ruler.ts`) checks a `ruler.shown` in both views: two whole squares and a finite distance of zero or more, a malformed one skipped. (U-L2) In live mode the distance, and 'Measurement cleared.', are announced once the server has accepted the command and nothing newer waits; a refusal announces none. (U-L4) With the ruler off, Escape on the canvas takes a shown measurement off (another browser's included) unless steering or a selected token has Escape, and the help line says so ('A measurement is shown: …'). (U-L3) The prep-mode help says only the DM sees the measurement. (U-L1, C-L4) Feet per square: on Enter a value out of bounds is refused beside the field; on leaving the field it is put back and the status line says so ('Feet per square must be from {min} to {max}, so it stays {feet}.'); a save no longer holds the other setup controls; what is typed while a save runs is kept for the next; a ref keeps Enter-then-leave to one PATCH. (U-L9) `formatNumber` shows distances and bounds with thousands separated ('100,000'). Test tooling: the client test server keeps which DM socket measured last and clears the measurement when it goes (`FakeSocket.onGone`), and compares paths field by field (T-M6, C-L5). Tests: `server/src/ws/ruler.test.ts` (4 new: the live scene deleted, ownership of the same path, a map-less live scene, no socket id to players), the gate `server/src/ws/hidden-information.test.ts` (a second DM browser measures and disconnects), `shared/src/ruler.test.ts`, `client/src/canvas/ruler.test.ts`, `client/src/canvas/MapCanvas.test.tsx` (4 new), `client/src/dm/LiveMode.test.tsx` (9 new, one extended), `client/src/player/scene.test.ts`, `client/src/dm/live/dmScene.test.ts`.
Why: The LIV-07 review (prompt 2, four passes, 2026-09-28) found no critical or high finding, eight mediums and twenty-nine lows; the owner approved fixing the mediums and the listed lows and deferring the rest (G-037). The token path was the only way of ending measuring that left the line on the TV. Without panning, a zoomed-in DM could not measure to a square off screen. The label is the reason players see the ruler at all (Q-027). The other fixes keep the panel from sending or announcing what the TV does not show, and bring the feet field in line with how the setup's other controls behave.
Alternatives: Only telling the DM in the help text that measuring stops panning (offered, not chosen: squares off screen stay out of reach). Space and drag to pan (not taken: Space on a focused canvas is not otherwise used, and a mouse button needs no key held). Clamping the label inside the view rather than flipping it (not taken: a label over the line's end hides the end). Showing a Clear measurement button (not taken: the toolbar's row is bounded, G-022; Escape and the help line suffice). Refusing an out-of-bounds value on leaving the field with the field's own error (rejected: focus has left, so it is not heard).
Affected specs: `06` §5, `04` §11, `08` §3, `08` §8

## D-123 (2026-09-28) — A server-wide PIN failure budget, IPv6 counted by /64, and the README's PIN advice (Q-097)
Type: spec-amendment
Decision: `07` §6 gains three statements, each tagged [Q-097]: beside the per-address limit, after 20 failed attempts across all client addresses within 10 minutes, PIN entry from every address except the server machine's own (loopback) MUST be refused for 10 minutes, the pause doubling on each further run, each pause logged with the addresses that caused it, DM sessions already open still working; an IPv6 client address MUST be counted by its /64 prefix for both limits; the README MUST state both limits and advise a PIN of 6 to 8 digits. The existing statements, and the register bullet of `12` (4–8 digits, per-client lockout after 5 failures, doubling, Q-009), are unchanged: the budget is added beside the per-client lockout, not in its place. Changed through `make unlock`, recorded in `UNLOCKS.md`.
Why: The owner answered Q-097 with C on 2026-09-28, the recommendation, to close G-010: a device on the Wi-Fi that changes its IPv4 address got five fresh guesses per address, a 4-digit PIN within hours, and a guessed PIN defeats the hidden-information isolation of `04` §4. The pause spares loopback so that the DM can always sign in on the server PC.
Alternatives: A, the budget without the README advice; B, accepting the residual with README advice only (both not chosen by the owner, see Q-097).
Affected specs: `07` §6, `09` §8

## D-124 (2026-09-28) — Operations: the settings route and screen, display versions regenerated in the background, the server-wide PIN budget as built, the PIN change in the DM view, the README and LICENSE (REL-01)
Type: implementation
Decision: Contract (`shared/src/settings.ts`): `SettingsUpdateSchema` is strict with at least one of `upload_limit_bytes` (integer, `UPLOAD_LIMIT_BOUNDS` 1 MiB to 1 GiB), `display_variant_size` (integer, `DISPLAY_SIZE_BOUNDS` 512 to 16,383 px) and `ruler_rule` (`phb` or `dmg`). Route: `PATCH /api/settings` (`server/src/http/settings.ts`), behind the session guard and Origin check of D-076, answers `SettingsSchema` (no PIN hash); `updateSettings` (`server/src/db/settings.ts`) writes only those three columns, by name, in one statement; a refused body changes nothing. The write runs through the live socket's `refresh` (D-115), so a measurement shown on the TV is counted again by the new rule and both rooms get one fresh snapshot, and nobody hears of anything else; `settings.changed` logs the fields that changed. The upload limit needs nothing more: `POST /api/images` reads it on every upload. Display versions (`server/src/images/regenerator.ts`, `store.ts`): one background worker brings every image to the current setting, one image at a time, skipping an image whose display long edge already equals min(original long edge, setting, 16,383) (`isDisplayCurrent`), and reading the setting again before each image so a change mid-run starts a new pass; each new file and its dimensions are stored in one synchronous step inside `refresh`, so the live map's rooms get its new size, and the canvas requests the display version again when its size changes (`MapCanvas`). The worker is kicked by every PATCH that names the display size (saving it unchanged finishes a run a restart cut short) and by an upload that stored a display version at a size that is no longer the setting, which covers an upload received during a run or started before the change (G-015); `app.onClose` stops it after the image in hand. Guessing protection (`server/src/auth/lockout.ts`, `07` §6 as amended by D-123): the per-address counter is keyed by `lockoutKey`, an IPv6 address by its /64 (expanded, zone dropped), IPv4 and loopback as themselves; beside it a sliding 10-minute window of failures from non-loopback addresses, counted like the per-address one when an attempt starts; the 20th opens a pause of 10 min × 2^(n-1) for the n-th, exponent capped at 30, empties the window, and is logged once as `pin.paused` with the seconds and the distinct addresses; attempts during a pause are refused as 429 `locked_out` without counting, with `Retry-After` the longer of the address's wait and the pause; loopback is neither counted nor paused (its own per-address lockout still applies); a correct PIN forgives its own failure in the window but ends no pause and resets no doubling; sessions are untouched, so a signed-in DM keeps working. Client: a Settings button in the live bar beside Connect a screen opens `SettingsDialog` (`client/src/dm/SettingsDialog.tsx`): the three settings read from `GET /api/settings` (upload limit in whole MB, display size in px, the rule as two radio buttons), refused beside the field before sending when out of bounds, saved in one PATCH with a Saving state and Save off, a failure notice, and a saved message that says display versions are being made in the background when the size changed; the workspace takes the saved settings at once (library and scene upload checks, the ruler's rule). The same dialog holds the PIN change of `07` §1 (current, new, again), calling `PUT /api/settings/pin`, with pattern and mismatch errors beside the fields, a wrong current PIN beside its field, a lockout with its wait, and the fields emptied after each attempt.
