An open DM view reads `GET /api/settings` again each time the ruler is turned on, so a rule another browser saved is used without a reload (G-036). Messages: the lockout text no longer says 'from this device', since a pause is server-wide; setup's PIN label advises 6 to 8 digits. Documents: `README.md` is rewritten for a DM (install and start, update with `npm run build`, first run, settings and environment variables, data directory and backups, the PIN hash in every database copy and backup file and how to remove old ones (G-008), one server per data directory and the images folder's growth (G-017), plain HTTP on a trusted network (`07` §4), both PIN limits and the 6-to-8-digit advice (Q-097), the Windows firewall on private networks only and ufw and firewalld commands for Linux (`09` §4), logs, the AGPL and the 5th-edition description without the trademark (`01` §8, `09` §8)); `LICENSE` is the AGPL-3.0 text from gnu.org, unchanged. `09` checked against the running server (2026-09-28, `npm start` with an empty data directory on port 3219): §1 (build if none, migrations, refusal on Node 12 with the message), §2 (URL, QR code, PIN hint, backup only when a migration is pending on an existing database), §5 (`emberglass.db`, `images/`, `logs/`), §6 and §7 (environment variables) match; the one mismatch found is that `npm start` does not rebuild a stale build after an update, which D-033 decided, so the README states it rather than the code changing.
Why: REL-01 (`13` §7) delivers `09` §7 (Q-051) with `05` §6, `05` §7 and `06` §5, the repository documents of `09` §1, §4, §5, §8 and `07` §4, and Q-097's rule, closing G-008, G-010, G-015 and G-036. The bounds keep every value one the pipeline handles: uploads are streamed to disk (D-044), and WebP stores 16,383 px at most. Skipping current images makes the worker idempotent, which is what lets any kick be cheap and safe and closes the race G-015 names without a per-image record of the size used. Running the write and each regenerated image through `refresh` reuses the one mechanism that already decides which room sees what (D-115), so players are told only when their view changed. Re-reading the rule when measuring starts keeps the WebSocket to the live scene (`04` §1); a live measurement's distance comes from the server anyway. The PIN change was a MUST of `07` §1 with no screen and no plan item; the owner chose to add it to the settings screen (2026-09-28). Loopback is exempt from counting so a DM mistyping on the server PC cannot pause the table, and a correct PIN does not lift a pause because nineteen wrong ones came first.
Alternatives: A settings event on the WebSocket for other DM views (rejected: `04` §1 keeps it to the live scene). PUT with the whole settings object (not taken: a partial PATCH lets one field change alone, and D-076 left PATCH to REL-01). Regenerating everything on every kick (rejected: an upload during a run would rewrite every image again). Storing the size each display version was made at (not taken: needs a migration for what the dimensions already say). Starting the worker at every start-up (not taken: a scan each start for a case the next save covers, and the tests would run it on fixtures without files). Counting loopback in the server-wide budget (rejected: the DM's own typos would pause the table's devices). Lifting a pause when the attempt that opened it is correct (not taken: nineteen wrong PINs came first). Keying IPv6 by /128 (rejected by Q-097) or /48 (not taken: one household's prefix is a /64). A separate PIN screen (not taken: the settings screen is where the DM looks, and a dialog keeps the workspace). Changing `npm start` to rebuild when the build is older than the source (not taken: D-033 decided the behaviour; the README states the update step).
Affected specs: `09` §1, `09` §2, `09` §4, `09` §5, `09` §6, `09` §7, `09` §8, `05` §6, `05` §7, `06` §5, `07` §1, `07` §4, `07` §6, `01` §8

## D-125 (2026-09-29) — REL-01 review fixes: focus returned after a dialog, a pause logged whatever the PIN, the worker stopped before the database closes, only changed settings sent, and the tests the review found missing
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-124, which stays in force except as stated. (U-H1) Focus after a dialog: `Dialog` returns focus, when it unmounts, to what had it before it opened, and owners that move focus when a dialog closes do it through `useFocusLater` (`client/src/ui/useFocusLater.ts`), in an effect after the dialog has left the document, since the page behind an open modal is inert: Settings, Connect a screen, a deleted asset (focus to New asset) and a deleted campaign (to New campaign), the last three of which lost focus in a real browser before this. The jsdom fake dialog now makes the page behind an open modal inert, so these tests can fail. (C-M1) `pin.paused` is logged as soon as the attempt that opened the pause starts, before the PIN is checked, so a pause opened by a correct 20th PIN is logged too. (C-M2) The regenerator stops in a `preClose` hook, before the `onClose` hook in which `server.ts` closes the database. (C-L2, U-L11/C-L3) The Settings dialog sends only the fields the DM changed; saved with nothing changed it sends the display size alone (finishing an interrupted regeneration, changing nothing else), and a stored upload limit that is not whole MB no longer blocks saving the others. (C-L1) The workspace numbers each settings read and save, and applies a read only if nothing newer began after it was sent. (U-M1, U-M2, U-M3) Focus goes to the upload limit once the settings arrive; Save settings and Change PIN are `aria-disabled` while their request runs, a second press sending nothing; a refusal puts focus on the first refused field, a wrong current PIN on that field. (U-M4) The live bar's status keeps one line, cut with an ellipsis and shown whole on hover (title) and to assistive technology, so the Settings button never wraps the bar (G-022). (U-M5, U-L1, U-L2) The dialog has two headed sections ('Uploads, TV and ruler', 'Change the PIN'), the PIN change set apart by a rule; the fieldset loses the browser's border; an empty status takes no room. (S-L4, T-M7) Every PIN field is emptied after each attempt, whatever the answer, as D-124 said. (U-L5) PIN fields use current-password and new-password. (U-L6) A wait of two minutes or more is given in minutes, at sign-in too. (U-L3, U-L4, U-L7, U-L8, U-L9, U-L10) Bounds shown with thousands separated, clearer hint, saved and intro texts, the saved message cleared on the next edit, Try again after a failed read, the dialog in the rendered-catalogue test, rule labels from a typed map. README: the terminal and install time, the port in the setup URLs, the PowerShell form, localhost as the way in on the server PC during a pause (setup and pause exemption are by loopback, not the printed LAN address), that only a restart resets the pause's doubling and that a PIN change during a pause is made on the server PC (owner's choice: document, not change, S-L1, S-L2, C-L8), the sustained rate a device below the budget can keep (S-M1, now card Q-098), what a second server on the same folder deletes (C-L6), and firewalld's active zone (C-L7). Test tooling: `createDisplayRegenerator` takes `regenerate`, so a pass can be held part-way. Tests: `server/src/images/regenerator.test.ts` (5: a kick during a pass, the per-image setting read, idempotency with no file written or commit made, a failed image logged, stop after the image in hand), `server/src/http/settings.test.ts` (an unchanged size finishing a regeneration, closing with the database closed in onClose, the mid-upload test bounded), `server/src/auth/auth-units.test.ts` (forgiveness keeping the other failures and the doubling, the longer Retry-After when the address's lock is longer, /64 forgiveness), `server/src/http/auth.test.ts` (a pause opened by a correct PIN logged, 40 parallel guesses from 40 addresses: 20 checked, one pause, no failure counted for a paused address), `shared/src/settings.test.ts` (per-body cases, bounds accepted), `client/src/dm/SettingsDialog.test.tsx` (6 new), `client/src/dm/LiveMode.test.tsx` (a failed quiet read, a stale read after a save), `client/src/dm/DmView.test.tsx` (minutes and seconds), `client/src/ui/messages.test.tsx` (the dialog's states), `e2e/tests/settings.spec.ts` (opened, edited, saved and closed by keyboard with focus returned, the live bar one line at 1,024 px with a long name, the settings restored as read and checked). Each new server test was checked against a mutation of the code it covers.
Why: The REL-01 review (prompt 2, four passes, 2026-09-29) found no critical finding, two highs (U-H1, T-H1), sixteen mediums and about twenty-five lows; the owner approved fixing the highs, every medium but S-M1, and the cheap lows, a question card for S-M1, and documenting rather than changing the pause's doubling and the PIN change during a pause. Focus lost after a dialog is a keyboard failure of core DM actions (`08` §8), and the fake dialog's missing inertness is why the tests had not seen it. An unlogged pause breaks `07` §6 as amended by D-123. Sending only changed fields is what keeps another browser's setting, which the whole-object save silently reverted.
Alternatives: Moving Settings into the banner beside Sign out (not taken: the dialog's state lives with the workspace, and truncating the status keeps every button in one row at any name length). Putting the PIN change in a dialog of its own (not taken: two headed sections with a rule separate them, and one Settings button keeps the bar short). A distinct server message for the server-wide pause (not taken now: needs a new error detail; minutes and the README cover the DM's question). Resetting the doubling after a quiet day, and exempting a DM session from the pause for a PIN change (offered, not chosen by the owner: documented instead).
Affected specs: `07` §1, `07` §6, `08` §8, `09` §4, `09` §5, `09` §7, `05` §7

## D-126 (2026-09-29) — A 24-hour PIN failure budget beside the 10-minute one (Q-098)
Type: spec-amendment
Decision: `07` §6 gains one statement, tagged [Q-098]: beside the 10-minute budget of Q-097, after 100 failed attempts across all client addresses except loopback within 24 hours, PIN entry MUST be paused in the same way, from every address except loopback, the pause doubling with the 10-minute one (one run count for both) and logged like it. Two existing statements are reworded without changing their rule: IPv6 is counted by its /64 'for every limit' (was 'both limits'), and the README states 'the limits' (tagged Q-097, Q-098). The per-address lockout, the 10-minute budget and the register bullet of `12` (Q-009) are unchanged. Changed through `make unlock`, recorded in `UNLOCKS.md`. The code does not implement it yet: G-039 records it, and REL-02 carries it.
Why: The owner answered Q-098 with A on 2026-09-29, the recommendation. The REL-01 review (S-M1) showed a device keeping 19 wrong PINs per 10 minutes, changing its address, is never paused by Q-097's rule alone: about 2,700 guesses a day, a 4-digit PIN within days. A second window of 100 per day bounds that trickle to about 100 guesses a day with the same loopback exemption, pause and logging, and a home table's few wrong PINs a night never reach it.
Alternatives: B, a decaying score in place of the window (not chosen: harder to state and test, and Q-097's wording would no longer describe the behaviour); C, accepting the residual with the README's rate and advice (not chosen).
Affected specs: `07` §6, `09` §8

## D-127 (2026-09-29) — Acceptance suite: the five journeys and the large-scene fixture as specs, the offline run by a blackhole proxy and a server guard, the external-URL build check, the tripwires promoted, and the browser matrix (REL-02)
Type: implementation
Decision: Journeys (`10` §5, `08` §10): one Playwright spec per journey under `e2e/tests/journeys/`, each with a DM context and a player context against the production server: `first-run.spec.ts` starts a server of its own (`startOwnServer` in `support.ts`: `node --import ./e2e/offline/guard.mjs scripts/start.mjs`, the command `npm start` runs, on a free port and a fresh data directory), reads the console's PIN hint and player URL, is refused setup from this PC's LAN address (the setup-elsewhere screen with no control, `POST /api/setup` 403), sets the PIN from localhost, then signs in from the LAN browser, and a second setup is 409; `prepare.spec.ts` is Phase 2's exit test moved from `tokens.spec.ts` (its helpers moved to `e2e/tests/prep.ts`), with a TV connected throughout that stays idle and hears no scene, asset, token or name; `connect.spec.ts` opens the URL the Connect a screen panel shows, as typed, and checks the idle screen; `run.spec.ts` is the whole Run journey in one test (go live, move, reveal, hide, delete, undo, measure on the TV, steer the TV camera, a token placed and the grid hidden in prep mode on the next scene, go live on it, Blank TV), asserting that the first TV frame naming the next scene is its activation's snapshot; `recover.spec.ts` puts each view behind its own TCP relay (`relay.ts`) that goes silent as a dropped Wi-Fi does (open connections carry nothing, new ones are accepted and never answered), moves and reveals tokens from a third browser during the cut, and after the restore checks both views show the current state, the DM view with no PIN prompt and still commanding; it also checks the DM view says it is reconnecting within the heartbeat's bound (D-128). Journeys delete their campaigns, since the keyboard walk visits every tree control. Large-scene fixture (`10` §3, Q-060, Q-088, D-036): `e2e/fixtures/large-scene.ts` draws a 10,000 × 7,000 px map (a 10 × 7 patchwork of 1,000 px regions under a 2 px grid of 100 px squares, 1.3 MB as PNG, about a second to make) and five 256 px token discs, builds the scene with 50 visible tokens plus a map-less scene through the REST API, and as a command (`node e2e/fixtures/large-scene.ts <folder>`, Node's type stripping) writes the images for the owner's TV run; `journeys/large-scene.spec.ts` activates it from the DM view and checks the TV fetched the map's display version and never the original, draws each of four regions in its colour and each of the 50 tokens in its asset's colour, then the map-less scene. Offline run (`10` §6, `02` §6, Q-020): every browser of the run uses `e2e/offline/blackhole.ts` as its proxy, started in `e2e/global-setup.ts`, bypassed only for localhost, 127.0.0.1, [::1] and this machine's IPv4 addresses; it refuses and records every request and CONNECT; the server under test runs with `e2e/offline/guard.mjs` loaded by `--import`, which blocks every TCP or TLS connection beyond loopback and this machine's addresses and every DNS lookup but localhost, recording each; both write one JSON line per attempt to `EMBERGLASS_E2E_OUTBOUND_LOG`. `e2e/tests/offline.spec.ts`, `@gate:offline-e2e`, runs last (project `offline`, depending on every other) and fails on any recorded line, then proves the block: an image request from a view to a documentation address fails and is recorded, and a Node process under the guard gets ECONNREFUSED and ENOTFOUND, both recorded. External URLs (`10` §6): `scripts/check-external-urls.mjs`, `@gate:external-url-build`, runs after `vite build` in the client's build script, so `make build`, CI's build job and `npm start`'s first build fail when the built client references a script, style, font or image on another host: HTML loading attributes, srcset and inline styles, CSS url() and @import, SVG hrefs, and in JavaScript URL imports, importScripts and absolute URLs naming such a file; namespace URIs and libraries' documentation links pass. Tripwires: both registry entries leave `scripts/tripwire.mjs`, whose registry is now empty (`make tripwire` says every gate is implemented; the mechanism stays for a later gate), the tripwire job leaves `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, and `README.md` describes the gates instead; the ruleset's required checks for the two tripwire jobs are the owner's to remove. Browser matrix (`10` §4): `EMBERGLASS_E2E_BROWSERS` (default `chromium`) names the browsers; Chromium runs every spec and each other browser named runs `tests/journeys/`; CI names `chromium,firefox,webkit` on Linux and `chromium,firefox,webkit,msedge` on Windows, where Edge is preinstalled and driven through Playwright's `msedge` channel, installs Firefox and WebKit in `make setup` (`SETUP_BROWSERS`) and keys the browser cache on the set. Safari is accepted through WebKit, Playwright's build of Safari's engine, on both runners; a run on a real Safari is not part of the suite, and the owner may add one by hand.
Why: REL-02 (`13` §7, `PLAN.md`) makes `10` §5 and §6 executable, promotes the last two tripwires (`13` §3, D-061) and runs the matrix of `10` §4 where CI can. A server of its own is the only way First run can run in every browser, since the PIN is set once per data directory (D-086). A relay that goes silent, rather than one that resets, is what a sleeping device or a dropped Wi-Fi does, and is the case G-026 names. Blocking in the proxy and the guard, rather than in the operating system, runs the same on the Linux and Windows runners and records what was attempted, so an empty log is evidence and not just a pass. Checking the built files rather than the sources judges what a browser receives, whatever a dependency adds. Running the journeys, not every spec, in the other browsers keeps the Windows job within its time budget while every step a DM or a TV takes is covered in each.
Alternatives: Blocking outbound traffic in the operating system (a network namespace or firewall rule on Linux, the Windows firewall on Windows; not taken: needs root, differs per runner, and records nothing); Playwright's page.route aborting non-local requests (not taken: sees only what a page asks, not the browser's own traffic or the server's); Chromium's host resolver rules (tried and rejected: they also refuse IP literals, the local ones included); `context.setOffline` for the Recover journey (rejected: leaves open WebSockets untouched in Chromium); a relay that resets connections on the cut (rejected: shows only the easy case, a close each end hears at once); every spec in every browser (not taken: triples the Windows e2e job for specs whose browser-specific surface the journeys cover); a Vite plugin for the URL check (not taken: the check in the build script also covers what plugins emit after it and needs no TypeScript seam); keeping the tripwire script's registry with the two gates marked done (rejected: a tripwire that stays green beside its gate is the silent pass D-061 forbids); a manual Safari run on the owner's device (not chosen now: WebKit runs on every change; the owner may still add one).
Affected specs: `10` §3, `10` §4, `10` §5, `10` §6, `08` §10, `02` §6, `09` §3, `13` §3, `13` §6, `13` §7

## D-128 (2026-09-29) — The 24-hour PIN budget as built, a 15-second heartbeat with reconnection on waking, a cap on player sockets per address, and three routes that tell the DM room (REL-02: G-039, G-026, G-029, G-033)
Type: implementation
Decision: (G-039, `07` §6 as amended by D-126) `server/src/auth/lockout.ts` keeps a second sliding window of the failures from non-loopback addresses, 24 hours long (`DAILY_WINDOW_MS`), beside the 10-minute one; a failure counts in both when its attempt starts. When the day's window reaches 100 (`DAILY_FAILURES_PER_PAUSE`), or the 10-minute one 20, the attempt opens one pause from the same run count, so either doubles the other; a pause always empties the 10-minute window and empties the day's only when the day's budget opened it, since the failures before a 10-minute pause are still that day's guesses; when one attempt spends both, the pause is the day's. `Pause` gains `budget` ('10m' or '24h'), logged with `pin.paused`. A correct PIN takes its own failure out of both windows; loopback counts in neither. `README.md` states both budgets and that a device kept under both gets at most 99 guesses a day. (G-026) `shared/src/live.ts` gains `HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_MS` (10 s) and `HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 s), which the server passes to Socket.io as `pingInterval` and `pingTimeout`, so either end drops a silent connection within 15 s instead of Socket.io's 45 s; the client (`client/src/live/connection.ts`) also opens its connection afresh when the window fires `online`, and when the page is shown after being hidden for 15 s or more (`HIDDEN_RECONNECT_MS`), and removes both listeners when closed. (G-029) `server/src/ws/live.ts` counts the player sockets each address holds, keyed as the PIN limits key it (`lockoutKey`, an IPv6 address by its /64), and drops one past `MAX_PLAYER_SOCKETS_PER_ADDRESS` (16) before it joins a room or hears anything, logged as `ws.too_many` under the connection-line limit; loopback and DM sockets are not capped. `LiveSocketOptions` gains `maxPlayerSocketsPerAddress` and `clientAddress` for tests, passed through `liveLimits`. (G-033) `PUT /api/sessions/:id/scenes/order`, `POST /api/scenes/:id/duplicate` and `PUT /api/images/:id/preset` run through the live socket's `refresh` (D-115), so the DM room gets one fresh snapshot when the live scene's order or its map's preset changed, and players nothing; `registerImages` takes `refresh`.
Why: REL-02 carries G-039 (the owner's answer to Q-098) and closes G-026, G-029 and G-033 with tests. One run count is what D-126 states; emptying the day's window only on its own pause keeps a trickle after a 10-minute pause from getting a fresh 100. Fifteen seconds bounds the Recover journey with a silent network and costs a ping of a few bytes every 10 s per socket on a LAN; `online` and `visibilitychange` cover the wake-up before the heartbeat notices. Sixteen sockets is a TV, a spare screen and tabs, with room for a sleeping device's dead sockets until the heartbeat drops them; a cap needs no change to how broadcasts are encoded. The three routes are the only REST changes to the DM room's copy of the live scene that did not reach it.
Alternatives: (G-039) One weighted score in place of two windows (rejected by the owner in Q-098); emptying both windows on every pause (rejected: a 10-minute pause would reset the day); a pause per budget with its own doubling (rejected: D-126 says one run count). (G-026) Only the listeners, keeping Socket.io's default heartbeat (rejected: a TV that stays awake behind a dead Wi-Fi still waits 45 s); a 5-second interval (not taken: three times the pings for a bound the journey does not need). (G-029) One encoding per room broadcast (not taken: needs per-socket version handling changed, and one address still holds hundreds of sockets); a cap on all sockets per address, DM ones included (rejected: a DM laptop is signed in and its tabs are the DM's own); capping loopback (rejected: the server PC is trusted, as for the PIN pause). (G-033) A separate event for order or preset (rejected: `04` §1 keeps the WebSocket to the live scene, and a snapshot is how the DM room already hears setup changes).
Affected specs: `07` §6, `09` §8, `04` §1, `04` §4, `04` §6, `04` §10, `03` §5, `08` §10

## D-129 (2026-09-29) — REL-02 residuals: what the MVP ships with, accepted by record; the narrowest supported DM window; how Windows runs the developer targets; the connect address kept for the owner's hardware
Type: implementation
Decision: Each gap below meets its own closing condition by this record, which states the residual and keeps it as behaviour of the MVP; its row leaves `GAPS.md`. (G-003) On Windows the product runs natively (`npm install`, `npm start`, `npm run dev`); the root `Makefile` targets run from MSYS2, Git Bash or WSL, as CI runs them, except `make clean-start`, which needs `setsid` and `tar` and is supported on Linux and WSL only; the Windows developer uses `make verify` there. (G-017) An oversized upload is still refused only after its body arrives, `Expect: 100-continue` is still told to continue, concurrent uploads and the images folder's size are unbounded, and the asset dialog shows no upload progress: all open only to a DM session or the server PC, and asset images are small beside maps, which show progress. (G-018) The DM view's tree follows another browser only when that list is next read, and opens collapsed after a reload: `04` §1 keeps the WebSocket to the live scene, and a DM prepares from one browser. (G-022) The DM view is supported from a window of 1,280 × 720; at 1,024 px wide the calibration layout overflows, and at 1,280 × 720 the canvas's lower part is reached by scrolling while preparing tokens (`08` §7 names no minimum; laptops are wider). (G-032, G-034, G-035, G-037, G-038) The DM-view and TV-frame residuals the LIV-04 to REL-01 reviews listed and the owner deferred stay as those rows described them, none reaching players or storing anything wrongly: commands in flight settled by their timeout, stale prep state after leaving live mode, silent Ctrl+Z in prep mode and the undo announcement's imprecision, the undo history outliving the session that filled it, a reconnected TV's frame taking an earlier screen's shape, the ruler's rate unbounded for DM sessions and its hue near the frame's, the Settings dialog scrolling at 720 px, and IPv6 link-local keying, latent while the server listens on IPv4 only; the tests those rows asked for beyond them are not written. The ruler label's legibility across a room (G-037 U-L8) is part of the TV run of REL-03. (G-031) Not accepted: the connect panel's prominent address may be a virtual adapter's on a PC with Hyper-V, WSL or Docker; that is checked on the owner's PC and TV, and its row moves to REL-03.
Why: REL-02's acceptance asks that each residual be closed with a test or kept with a recorded decision. D-128 closes the four with a test (G-026, G-029, G-033, G-039); the rest are UX refinements or DM-only limits whose rows ask for 'a recorded decision accepting it' as one way to close, and keeping them does not touch hidden-information isolation (`04` §4), the PIN (`07` §6) or stored data. G-031 names the owner's hardware as its evidence, which REL-03 has.
Alternatives: Fixing every listed residual in REL-02 (not taken: dozens of UX changes, each with its tests, outside the acceptance suite's outcome and after the owner deferred them); keeping every row open against REL-03 (rejected: REL-03 is the TV run and would not close them either, so the rows would describe no plan); a question card per residual (rejected: none changes data, security, scope or product identity, which is what a card is for); 1,024 × 700 as the narrowest window (not taken: needs the calibration and scene layout reworked).
Affected specs: `08` §7, `09` §1, `09` §3, `04` §1, `04` §8, `04` §9, `05` §6, `06` §5, `07` §6, `08` §5

## D-130 (2026-09-29) — REL-02 CI fixes: pointer steps kept on screen, a 5-second connect timeout, project patterns for Windows paths, and traces of failed tests
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-127 and D-128, which stay in force except as stated. The first CI runs of the matrix (36558406339, 36561044420) failed only in WebKit and Edge: a click or drag aimed at a point of the DM canvas below the fold at 1,280 × 720 (G-022) did nothing there, where Chromium and Firefox deliver it. `onScreen` (`e2e/tests/canvas-view.ts`) scrolls the page, when needed, so every point of a pointer action is on screen and answers where each point is after the scroll; `screenOf` and `dragBy` (`e2e/tests/prep.ts`) and the Run journey's ruler drag use it. The live connection (`client/src/live/connection.ts`) gives up a connection attempt after `CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 s) instead of Socket.io's 20 s, so an attempt made while the network was down, which a TV's browser may keep waiting on, is abandoned and retried soon after the network returns; WebKit on Windows failed the Recover journey that way, which is the test for it. `e2e/playwright.config.ts` matches the set-up and journey specs by patterns that accept Windows' backslashes (the First run journey had run in the set-up project there), keeps the trace and a screenshot of each failed test in `test-results/`, which the e2e job uploads, and on CI adds Playwright's `github` reporter, so a failed job names its test in the run's annotations.
Why: The pointer steps are what the journeys measure, not the layout; the layout at 1,280 × 720 is D-129's accepted residual, and a test aiming off screen tested the harness. A connect timeout of 5 s is far above a LAN handshake and completes G-026's bound for the case where the device's own attempt hangs. Annotations and traces make a CI failure diagnosable without the job log, which needs a signed-in user.
Alternatives: A taller viewport for the journeys (not taken: moves them off the 1,280 × 720 laptop window the other specs use); scrollIntoViewIfNeeded alone (rejected: it leaves a partly visible canvas where it is, which is the failure); retries (rejected by D-060); leaving the connect timeout at Socket.io's default (rejected: a TV back on the Wi-Fi could wait 20 s more); traces for every test (not taken: large artifacts for passing runs).
Affected specs: `10` §4, `10` §5, `04` §6, `08` §10

## D-131 (2026-09-29) — The Prepare journey's test budget is 90 s
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-130. `e2e/tests/journeys/prepare.spec.ts` sets `test.setTimeout(90_000)`, as the keyboard walk (90 s) and the Recover journey (120 s) do. CI run 36563039931 (`a225a75`) passed every job but `e2e · windows`, where 60 of 61 tests passed and the Prepare journey in WebKit ran past Playwright's default 30 s at its drag step, every earlier step having passed; the offline gate, which depends on it, did not run there. The same run passed `e2e · linux` whole: all specs in Chromium, the journeys in Firefox and WebKit, and the offline gate after them with nothing recorded.
Why: Prepare is some forty steps; WebKit on the Windows runner takes it at more than twice Chromium's time on the same runner, so the whole exceeds a budget meant for short tests while no step fails or waits on a race.
Alternatives: Splitting Prepare into several tests (not taken: `10` §5 names one journey, and the tests share one server in order, D-086); a longer default for every test (rejected: would let a short test hang for 90 s); leaving WebKit on Windows out of the matrix (rejected: `10` §4 asks for the matrix where CI can run it, and it runs).
Affected specs: `10` §4, `10` §5

## D-132 (2026-09-29) — First loads from the LAN address get 45 s in the journeys; the Firefox-on-Windows slowness is a gap
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-130 and D-131. `LAN_LOAD_MS` (45 s, `e2e/tests/journeys/support.ts`) bounds each navigation to this PC's LAN address in the First run and Connect TV journeys and the first screen it shows, and their test budgets are raised to hold them; every assertion stays as it was. CI run 36564824322 (`7fc69bc`) passed 17 of 18 jobs, `e2e · linux` whole, and 59 of 61 tests on Windows: those two journeys in Firefox, whose first loads from 10.1.0.x took over 26 s there, having taken 9 s and 5.6 s in run 36558406339, against about a second in every other browser and in Firefox on Linux. The slowness is recorded as G-040 against REL-03, with its cause not identified.
Why: The journeys test that a LAN browser reaches the views and what it sees; the time Firefox on the Windows runner takes to load from that address varies from run to run and is not the product's behaviour under test. A bound, not an unlimited wait, keeps a load that never finishes a failure, and the gap keeps the slowness visible instead of absorbed.
Alternatives: Leaving Firefox on Windows out of the two journeys (rejected: hides the case, and `10` §4 asks for the matrix where CI runs it); turning off Firefox's HTTPS-First in the test profile (not taken: the cause is unconfirmed, and a DM's Firefox would have it on); retries (rejected by D-060); investigating from the trace before merging (not taken now: the artifact needs a signed-in download; G-040 names it as the evidence).
Affected specs: `10` §4, `10` §5, `08` §10

## D-133 (2026-09-29) — The offline gate sets aside Edge's own calls to Microsoft's services, and lists them
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-127. The blackhole proxy records the user agent of each attempt. `e2e/tests/offline.spec.ts` sets aside an attempt only when Edge's user agent (`Edg/`) sent it to a host in bing.com, microsoft.com, msn.com, live.com, skype.com, msedge.net or windows.com, reports those in the test's annotations with their hosts, and fails on every other attempt, Edge's to any other host and any attempt without a user agent included. CI run 36569636626 (`75a9287`) passed 61 of 62 tests on Windows and failed only the gate, on about 115 CONNECT attempts to www.bing.com and edge.microsoft.com, all blocked, made while Edge ran the journeys; the gate passes on Linux after Chromium, Firefox and WebKit with nothing recorded.
Why: `02` §6 and `10` §6 bound the running application: that it sends nothing outside the LAN. Edge calls Bing and Microsoft by itself whatever page it shows; those calls were blocked by the proxy, as the gate requires, and name no host the application knows, since the build check (`scripts/check-external-urls.mjs`) proves the built client references no other host and the server's guard recorded nothing. Setting aside only that browser's own vendor domains, by its user agent, keeps any request from a view, to any other host, a failure.
Alternatives: Failing on every attempt whatever its source (rejected: the gate would test Edge's services, and Edge could never be in the matrix); Edge started with its services turned off by flags (not taken: no documented flag set stops them all, and the gate would still fail on the next one Edge adds); running Edge outside the proxy (rejected: its views' traffic would go unwatched); dropping Edge from the matrix (rejected: `10` §4 names it); attributing attempts to pages through Playwright's request events (not taken: needs every spec to register its contexts, and misses traffic the page did not start, which is what the proxy is for).
Affected specs: `02` §6, `10` §4, `10` §6

## D-134 (2026-09-29) — REL-02 review fixes: over-cap sockets refused in the handshake, the offline gate proves it watched, a stricter URL check, commands settled on disconnect, and the tests the review found missing
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-127, D-128 and D-130, which stay in force except as stated. (H1) The per-address cap on player sockets (G-029) is applied in Socket.io middleware (`io.use`), which refuses a socket past it with `SOCKET_REFUSALS.tooManyViews` (`shared/src/live.ts`) before it connects, and releases the count from a `disconnect` listener registered in the same place (C-L3). Socket.io does not retry a refused socket, so the live connection (`client/src/live/connection.ts`) tries again after `REFUSED_RETRY_MS` (5 s) while refused, and not once closed; a lost connection is still left to Socket.io's own backoff. Before this, a socket past the cap connected and was dropped, the client reconnected at once, and one extra tab made about 200 handshakes a second. (H2) The server guard writes an `armed` line to the outbound log when it loads; `e2e/tests/journeys/offline-probe.spec.ts` (`@gate:offline-e2e`) runs in every browser project and makes one image request to a documentation address, which must fail and be recorded with that browser's user agent; `e2e/tests/offline.spec.ts` requires at least one armed line for the suite's server and one per browser's First run server, and that the log holds nothing but those probes, Edge's service calls (D-133) excepted and listed; its Node probe also sends a datagram. The log's reading and D-133's rule moved to `e2e/offline/log.ts`. (M1) `scripts/check-external-urls.mjs` treats every absolute http, https, ws or wss URL anywhere in the built JavaScript as a reference unless its host is in `DOCUMENTATION_HOSTS` (www.w3.org, react.dev, konvajs.org, konvajs.github.io, github.com, socket.io, the namespace and error-documentation hosts of the bundled libraries), and even there if it names a script, style, font or image file; a text that cannot be a hostname (a regular expression describing URLs) is not one. HTML attributes are read with their quotes, `<base>` is a loading element, and CSS `image-set()` strings are references. (M2) The live connection settles every command awaiting its acknowledgement as `network` the moment its socket disconnects, since Socket.io drops the acknowledgement, instead of after `COMMAND_TIMEOUT_MS`. (M3) The real socket's options are `SOCKET_OPTIONS`, asserted by a unit test (connect timeout 5 s). (M4, M6) The Run journey requires the first TV frame after the prep edits to be the next scene's activation snapshot, nothing before it, and the live scene's grid unchanged on the TV; Prepare first checks the TV received its snapshot; Recover requires the TV's `data-live` to read `reconnecting` within the heartbeat's bound during the cut and `connected` after. (M5) `server/src/ws/limits.test.ts` refuses three extras in turn at the cap, and one freed slot admits exactly one. (C-L1) `begin` returns the failure it counted and `succeeded` removes that very entry. (C-L2) A page opened hidden counts as hidden from the start for the wake-up reconnection. (C-L4) `scripts/ci-consistency.test.mjs` finds each gate's marker in a test file and the URL check in the client build. (S-L2) The guard also refuses UDP datagrams and connections, the promise resolvers, `dns.Resolver` instances and `lookupService` beyond this machine. (U-L1) The scene's head, the canvas's offline style and its accessible label say 'not connected' only once the loss has lasted `CONNECTION_NOTICE_DELAY_MS` (1 s, `useHeldFor`), as the live bar does; the TV frame still locks and commands are still refused at once. (U-L2, U-L4, S-L3) README table spacing and CI text, a stale comment, and the README stating that the pause's doubling lasts until a restart. (T-L1) A test fires five wrong PINs at once as the 100th of the day: one checked, four refused, one pause logged. (T-L2) The 45 s LAN-load wait applies to Firefox on Windows only and Prepare's 90 s budget to WebKit and Firefox on Windows only, each LAN load's time recorded as an annotation.
Why: The REL-02 review (prompt 2, four passes, 2026-09-29) found no critical finding and two highs: the cap's reconnect loop (all four passes) and an offline gate that could pass without having watched a server or a browser; twelve mediums across the passes, merged to seven (M1 to M7); and about fifteen lows. The owner approved fixing the highs, M1 to M6 and the cheap lows, a new gap for M7, U-L3 deferred as a gap and S-L3 documented.
Alternatives: (H1) Keeping the drop and delaying the client's reconnection after `io server disconnect` (rejected: an ended session must reconnect at once, G-011, and the two cannot be told apart). (H2) Attributing attempts to pages through Playwright's request events (not taken: the proxy sees what pages do not start). (M1) A list of known CDN hosts (rejected: the next CDN would pass); scanning only string literals (rejected: minified code joins strings). (M2) A distinct message for a dropped command (not taken: `network` already reads as a lost connection). (U-L1) Delaying the refusal of commands too (rejected: a command sent while down reaches no TV).
Affected specs: `04` §6, `07` §6, `02` §6, `10` §5, `10` §6, `08` §2

## D-135 (2026-09-29) — D-129 and D-133 corrected after the REL-02 review: the DM window's height is a gap, and the offline gate's exception does not rest on the build check alone
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-129 and D-133, which stay in force except as stated. D-129's acceptance of G-022 is withdrawn: a 1080p laptop at 150% scaling gives the DM view about 1,280 × 620, below the 1,280 × 720 D-129 named, and there the canvas's lower part and the Settings dialog need scrolling for core preparation (review U-M3); the residual is open again as G-041, against REL-03, since G-022's ID is retired. D-133's reasoning is corrected: the build check did not, when D-133 was written, prove the built client names no other host (review S-M2); D-134 makes it flag every absolute URL in the built JavaScript but documentation hosts, and the exception for Edge's calls to Microsoft's domains rests on that check, the server guard and the Chromium project, which runs every spec with no exception.
Why: The review showed the minimum window D-129 accepted is taller than a common laptop's, and D-133's 'Why' claimed more than the check did. Records are corrected by a new entry, never rewritten.
Alternatives: Superseding D-129 and D-133 whole (not taken: the rest of each stands); keeping 1,280 × 720 (offered to the owner, not chosen).
Affected specs: `08` §7, `02` §6, `10` §6

## D-136 (2026-09-29) — The Prepare journey's 90 s budget is by engine, not by system
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-134 (T-L2): the Prepare journey's 90 s budget applies to the WebKit and Firefox projects on every system, not on Windows only; Chromium and Edge keep Playwright's default 30 s. The 45 s LAN-load wait stays scoped to Firefox on Windows.
Why: Firefox took Prepare past 30 s on the loaded Linux development machine with every step passing, as WebKit did on the Windows runner; the point of T-L2, that a slowdown in the main engine still fails, holds with the budget scoped by engine.
Alternatives: Keeping the Windows-only scope (rejected: local runs of the matrix fail on load alone); 90 s for every project (rejected by T-L2).
Affected specs: `10` §4, `10` §5

## D-137 (2026-09-29) — A token stores whether players have seen it; numbering only at the first showing (Q-096)
Type: spec-amendment
Decision: `05` §3 gains one statement, tagged [Q-096]: whether a token has been shown to players MUST be stored with it, set when it is first shown (placed visible or revealed) and never cleared, and the numbering of Q-092 applies only at that first showing, so a token players saw with the bare name that is hidden and revealed again comes back with the label they saw. `03` §1's Token row gains the field `shown`, never sent to a client, referring to `05` §3, as Scene's `token_numbers` refers to it. The register (`12`) is unchanged. Both files changed through `make unlock`, recorded in `UNLOCKS.md`. The code does not implement it yet: G-043 records it, and REL-03 carries it (an additive migration, the flag set in placement, reveal and numbering, with tests).
Why: The owner answered Q-096 with B on 2026-09-29, the recommendation: it is the only option that makes the code do what `05` §3 already says, and the rename happened in front of players on the token they were watching (found testing LIV-05's undo, `server/src/domain/undo.test.ts`).
Alternatives: A, keeping the rule and amending `05` §3 so a reveal numbers any bare-named token; C, a heuristic without a migration that breaks Q-091's example (both not chosen by the owner, see Q-096).
Affected specs: `05` §3, `03` §1

## D-138 (2026-09-29) — Q-096 as built: migration 0003 adds token.shown, set at the first showing and carried through undo, never sent to a client
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-137, which planned the work for REL-03; it is done with the answer instead, since `server/src/db/schema.test.ts` checks the schema against `03` §1's field list and failed in CI (run 36610443613) once the spec named `shown`. Migration `0003_token_shown.sql` adds `token.shown INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (shown IN (0, 1))` and marks an existing token shown when it is visible or its label is not its asset's bare name (numbered at a showing, or typed by the DM, never renumbered either way); a hidden bare-named token is left not shown, as it was treated before. `server/src/db/tokens.ts`: a token placed visible is stored shown; a reveal sets `shown` and numbers a bare-named token only when it was not shown before (Q-092 applies at the first showing only); `deleteToken` answers whether the token was shown, the `token.removed` effect and the undo inverse (`token.add` in its restoring form) carry it, and `restoreToken` puts it back (by default, whether the token was visible). `shown` is read by its own query and is not in `SceneToken`, so no REST body, event or snapshot carries it; the projection of `token.removed` sends only the id. Test tooling: `readEntities` leaves `shown` out, as it does the token numbers, and the fixture migration test checks the migration's rule row by row.
Why: The owner answered Q-096 with B (D-137). Keeping the flag out of the contract keeps it where the other server-only numbering state is (Q-091's token numbers), and carrying it through the undo inverse is what keeps a token players saw, hidden, deleted and put back, from being renumbered at its next reveal.
Alternatives: Deferring the code to REL-03 (rejected: the schema conformance test fails until the column exists, and weakening it is not an option); adding `shown` to `SceneToken` (rejected: it would reach the DM's and, through a projection mistake, could reach players' payloads, against `03` §1 as amended); marking every existing hidden token shown (not taken: a token placed hidden and never revealed would then come back with the bare name when a second of its asset is already numbered, against Q-092).
Affected specs: `05` §3, `03` §1, `04` §8

## D-139 (2026-09-30) — The 2026-09-30 brief and design bundle as inputs; Q-099 and Q-100 written into the pack as Phase 5, before REL-03
Type: spec-amendment
Decision: Records what the owner's 2026-09-30 redesign brief and design bundle change, before any code. Inputs: the brief is added verbatim as the authoritative `docs/inputs/requirements/2026-09-30-ui-redesign-brief.md`, and `Design.html` as the pack's first non-authoritative input (D-003), look and layout only; both with authority entries in `docs/inputs/README.md`. Cards: Q-099 (scope), answered A by the owner in chat: ping, four condition markers and manual fog regions are built after the MVP's features and before REL-03, area-of-effect templates stay out; it supersedes Q-016. Q-100 (ux), answered A: where the brief's DM view layout, Go idle and idle line differ from `08`, the brief wins; it is recorded as the owner's answer because the brief is the owner's own statement. Spec text amended now: `01` §4 (manual fog and four markers leave Phase 2), §6 (area templates only), new §9; `08` §1, §2, §4 re-worded and new §11; `12` register bullets (Q-016's replaced, Q-023's and Q-025's extended); `13` §2 and new §10, Phase 5 with UIX-01, TBL-01, TBL-02, TBL-03, whose Surfaces and red-line values are the derived ones; `11` rows reclassified; `14` §3 names automatic fog instead of fog of war. AGENTS.md's scope red line names the four additions. Deferred on purpose to the package that builds each: `03` §1 and §7 (Region, `markers`), `04` §2, §3, §4, §8 and new §12 (ping) and §13 (fog), `07` §5 and `10` §3, because `server/src/db/schema.test.ts` and `shared/src/live.test.ts` check those tables against the code (D-138) and would fail before the code exists. The owner also chose: Rename and stacking stay prep-only on the live scene, as `04` §2 already says; Lock TV camera stays in the DM view that sets it (UIX-01 records it as built). Phase 5 runs before REL-03, which stays the last Now item after it.
Why: AGENTS.md makes red lines and register bullets owner decisions that need a record before code (D-002); the owner approved the scope in chat, so the record is the cards and this amendment. Amending the coupled tables with their code keeps every commit green, as D-138 learnt.
Alternatives: Amending every section now (rejected: the schema and contract conformance tests fail until the migrations and commands exist, and a failing gate cannot be committed); treating the brief as non-authoritative (rejected: it is the owner's statement of requirements, so its behaviour must be citable as [input]); one card per UX difference (not taken: the owner answered the brief as a whole, and one card with the three differences in its Source records exactly that).
Affected specs: `01` §4, `01` §6, `01` §9, `08` §1, `08` §2, `08` §4, `08` §11, `11` §Coverage rule, `12` §3, `12` §5, `13` §2, `13` §10, `14` §3

## D-140 (2026-09-30) — UIX-01 as built: redo, the undo state and the screens count, the scene's name and category for players, and the redesigned workspace's behaviour
Type: implementation
Decision: Contract (specs/04-live-sync.md §2, §3, §4, §8 and specs/02-architecture.md §5 amended in the same commit): `redo` is a command with an empty payload. The undo history keeps a redo stack beside its inverses: each undo that changed something records the inverse of the inverse it applied (`inverseOfInverse`, which reads the effects as `inverseOf` does, a restore undone by deleting again), `redo` applies it through `applyInverse` and records its inverse back without emptying the stack, and any other undoable command that changes something empties it; activation, deactivation and a live scene deleted empty both, as `keepOnly` does. Commands that are not undoable (camera, ruler) leave redo alone, the reading of the brief's 'cleared by any new command' that keeps a camera nudge from losing the DM's redo. The DM snapshot's live scene carries `history {can_undo, can_redo}` and the DM room hears `history.changed` when it changes: read before and after each command and each REST change in the same synchronous step, and not sent when the change sent the DM room a snapshot (which carries it) or changed which scene is live. The players' snapshot carries the live scene's `name` for the TV's plate, and each player token its asset's `category` for its ring; the hidden-information gate now checks that a scene's name reaches players only as a snapshot's `name`. The count of connected player views is `GET /api/screens` (DM session only), which the header polls every 3 s, and each scene's token and hidden-token counts `GET /api/sessions/:id/scenes/summary`. Workspace: the session switcher (a labelled region, not a dialog) holds the unchanged SceneTree with an Open action per session; the current session is the live scene's on the first snapshot, else the one remembered in this browser's localStorage (a convenience, wrapped in try/catch, cleared by the test server), else none, with a prompt to choose one. The first snapshot also shows the live scene. Lock TV camera is held by the workspace for this DM view and lost on reload; it refuses Send my view, Fit map, TV zoom and the frame (which loses its corner handles and reads 'locked'), and activation still fits the TV (Q-038). Send my view frames the DM's visible world in the TV's shape (`coverBox` of `visibleBox`). The steer mode and its T key are gone: T is Add token, and the keyboard path to steer the TV is to pan and zoom the DM's own view with the keys, then Send my view, or the TV zoom buttons. Shortcuts V, M, T, H, Shift+N, Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Shift+Z or Ctrl+Y act from anywhere in the DM view but a text field or an open dialog; the canvas's own keys go first and mark themselves handled. Reveal all is one `token.setVisibility` per hidden monster, so each is undone on its own. Duplicate places a token of the same asset a square to the right, numbered by the server (`token.add` live, REST in prep). Rename and stacking stay in the popover on the live scene, refusing with their reason, as the owner chose (D-139). The TV's label and badge scale is 2.55 × the DM's on a 1080-line screen, in proportion to the height, never below 1.6.
Why: The brief asks for redo, the counter and the TV's plate; the owner answered the scope (Q-099, Q-100). Comparing the undo state before and after, rather than tracking what the room was last told, sends nothing spurious after a restart, which a component test caught as a wrong ‘undone’ announcement. Polling a count over REST keeps the WebSocket's event sequences deterministic: a throttled timer event would arrive between any two events of any test.
Alternatives: Redo emptied by every command, the camera and the ruler included (rejected: a TV nudge would lose the redo the DM was about to use). Carrying the undo state in the command acknowledgement (rejected: another DM browser would not see it). A `screens.changed` event (built first, rejected: it lands at timer-driven moments inside every exact event sequence, and the count is a convenience that 3 s of lag does not harm). Keeping the scene's name from players (rejected: the brief asks for the plate; a DM who names scenes after secrets sees the name in the plate on the DM's own boards). Keeping keyboard steering (rejected: the design replaces it with buttons, and Send my view keeps the keyboard path).
Affected specs: `04` §2, `04` §3, `04` §4, `04` §8, `02` §5, `08` §2, `08` §11, `10` §3

## D-141 (2026-09-30) — Where UIX-01 departs from the 2026-09-30 design, and why
Type: implementation
Decision: Kept from the pack where the boards say otherwise: the live double outline around the canvas (specs/08-ux-journeys.md §2, Q-024), in the live colour; the grid drawn light over a dark halo (D-093) at half strength, rather than the boards' dark lines, so it shows on dark maps too; the status bar's diagonal rule in the pack's words ('Diagonals 5 ft (PHB)' by default, 'Diagonals 5/10 (DMG)'), the default staying PHB (specs/06-grid-and-measurement.md §5). Added where the boards draw nothing but an existing flow needs a place: the header's session switcher holding the tree (campaign and session create, rename, delete, reorder); a Scene setup button above the canvas opening the map, grid-visibility, feet-per-square and calibration controls; a per-scene menu in the scene list (Move up, Move down, Rename, Duplicate, Delete), Duplicate being the MVP's scene duplication (specs/03-domain-model.md §7), which had no control before; an OTHERS group in 'In this scene' for npc and object tokens; the popover's note that rename and stacking are prep-only; the placing bar above the canvas with Cancel placing. Drawn differently: a token shows its asset's image in its circle, and its initials only while the image loads, since every asset has an image; the TV's scene plate fades after about 6 s and again on each new scene; the rail's Ping and Fog regions are present and refuse until TBL-01 and TBL-03; the header's screens counter keeps 'Connect a screen' as its name and describes the count; the fitted DM view leaves the rail's strip free. Sign out moved into the Settings dialog, since the header has no room the boards give it.
Why: The design is a non-authoritative input for look and layout (D-003, D-139): where it differs from a specified behaviour, the specification wins, and every MVP flow must keep a place.
Alternatives: Dropping the live outline to match the boards (rejected: Q-024 requires a persistent indicator); dark grid lines as drawn (rejected: invisible on dark maps, against D-093); a separate Sign out button in the header (not taken: the boards' header is full, and Settings is where the DM already manages the PIN).
Affected specs: `08` §1, `08` §2, `08` §11, `06` §5, `03` §7

## D-142 (2026-09-30) — TBL-01: how a ping is sent, drawn and let go
Type: implementation
Decision: `ping {scene_id, x, y}` names the live scene and a point in grid units bounded like token coordinates; the server refuses it as scene_not_live otherwise, keeps nothing and records no undo, and both rooms receive the same `ping {x, y}`, one version each. Each view draws a ping for 2 s from its arrival (a glowing centre and two rings that grow and fade on 1.6 s cycles, 0.5 s apart, still under reduced motion), at most the latest 8 at once, over the tokens and sized in screen pixels, larger on the TV by the label scale; any snapshot lets every ping go. The DM's Ping tool (P, or the rail) is offered on the live scene only, with the reason given on the disabled button; a click pings the point clicked, a drag still pans, Enter pings the centre of the view, and P or Escape on the map, V, M, T or choosing a token leave it. No per-socket rate limit: only the DM's sockets can send, as for the ruler.
Why: specs/04-live-sync.md §12 (Q-099) asks for a point in grid units, stored nowhere, shown for about two seconds; a ping is transient, so each view expires it itself and no snapshot or history needs to carry it. On a scene that is not live a ping would show on no TV (as with the ruler, Q-086).
Alternatives: Holding pings in the server's memory and sending them in snapshots (rejected: §12 says a later view never sees one); a server-side expiry event (rejected: an extra event per ping for no gain); a throttle per socket (not taken: the DM is trusted and the ruler has none).
Affected specs: `04` §2, `04` §3, `04` §12, `08` §11

## D-143 (2026-09-30) — TBL-02: how condition markers are stored, changed and drawn
Type: implementation
Decision: Migration 0004 adds token.markers, TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]' with a CHECK that it is a JSON array; the server writes it in the order bloodied, unconscious, dead, concentrating whatever order it is sent in, and duplication and the undo of a deletion carry it. The contract gives Token and the players' token a `markers` array of distinct names (at most four); prep sets it over REST with the token's PATCH, the live scene with `token.setMarkers {token_id, markers}` naming the whole set after the change: a set equal to the current one tells nobody; undo puts back the set before and redo the set after; players hear of a visible token's change as token.updated and of a hidden one's not at all, and a token revealed arrives with its markers. The popover's CONDITION chips are toggle buttons (aria-pressed, a check mark kept out of the name), live and in preparation alike, and the token list's status line names the markers. Drawn on both views: Bloodied a red ring outside the token, pulsing on the TV (still under reduced motion), and a blood-drop badge; Concentrating a dotted purple ring further out; Unconscious the image desaturated by a saturation blend and a 'z' badge; Dead desaturated and darkened, an ✕ badge and the label struck through. Badges sit down the token's left side, the hidden badge alone on its right.
Why: specs/01-product-scope.md §9 and specs/04-live-sync.md §2 as amended (Q-099); the brief draws Bloodied and Concentrating and suggests Unconscious and Dead, each with a shape or a badge so no marker is told by colour alone (WCAG 1.4.1). One command carrying the whole set keeps undo a single inverse and makes a repeated click idempotent.
Alternatives: A command per marker to add or remove (rejected: two inverses to reason about and a racing pair of DM browsers could leave a set neither chose); a separate marker table (rejected: four fixed names on a token need no relation); a skull for Dead (not taken: an ✕ reads at badge size on a TV).
Affected specs: `03` §1, `04` §2, `04` §3, `04` §4, `04` §8, `08` §11

## D-144 (2026-09-30) — TBL-03: how fog regions are stored, filtered, projected and drawn
Status: superseded by D-155
Type: implementation
Decision: Storage: migration 0005 adds `region` (id, scene_id with ON DELETE CASCADE, name, order, shape as JSON, hidden 1 = fogged), the ninth entity; `order` keeps the order drawn, since the tables have no rowid. A shape is a rectangle of whole grid units at least a square each way, or a polygon of 3 to 64 corners on grid corners; a name is 1 to 100 characters, not all white space. Scene duplication copies the regions; deletion cascades. Visibility: one rule in shared/src/regions.ts, a token is seen by players when not hidden and its centre (position plus half its footprint) is under no fogged region, the edge counting as inside; server/src/db/fog.ts applies it, and the players' snapshot and ranks, every token event, the image entitlement and the DM's scene summary count read it there. Numbering follows it: after every token or region write a pass marks shown, and numbers if still bare-named, each token players see for the first time, bottom of the stack first, so a visible token placed under the fog renames no sibling players see until they see it too; the rename travels with the token that caused it, and an asset resized over REST runs the same pass for every scene it is on, since a new footprint moves each token's centre (review). A polygon edge is judged within 1e-9 grid units, so a centre that decimals put a hair's breadth off a slanted edge counts as on it (review). Events: the DM room hears region.added, region.updated and region.removed with names; the players' room hears fog.updated with every fogged shape and nothing else (no id, no name, no revealed region) whenever the fogged set changes, and a rename nothing; the region's event comes first, then a token.removed for each token it covers, then a token.added for each it shows in ascending rank, all computed in the write's transaction. A token moved across the fog edge reaches players as token.removed or token.added. Commands region.add, region.rename, region.setHidden and region.delete are live and undoable (undoing a deletion restores the same id and place), redo too; in preparation the same over REST under /api/scenes/:id/regions and /api/regions/:id, refused as 409 scene_live on the live scene. Drawing: the fog tool (F or the rail) draws a rectangle by a drag or a polygon by clicking corners, closed by its first corner or its last twice, by keys with Enter at the view's centre, Backspace and Escape; a shape drawn asks for its name, 'Region N' by default. The right panel's FOG REGIONS lists each with its state and the tokens inside players cannot see, Reveal or Fog, and a menu to rename or delete (asked first); a region's tag on the map toggles it; a token's status names the fogged region it stands in. On the TV a fogged region is #0A0806 with an 18 px soft edge over the map and grid, under the tokens; in the DM view a dashed blue outline over a hatch with a '· fogged' tag.
Why: specs/04-live-sync.md §4 and §13 and specs/07-security-and-access.md §5 as amended (Q-099); the brief makes the fog a client-side mask on a trusted table and asks for token.added on reveal and a count of hidden tokens per region. Filtering in one place, read by every path that decides what players see, is what keeps the hidden-information gate meaningful; sending all fogged shapes each time keeps the TV's copy exact without ids that would count regions it cannot see.
Alternatives: Rendering fogged map pixels out on the server (rejected by the brief); sending region ids or names to players (rejected: nothing to draw needs them); numbering at placement whatever the fog (rejected: a count leak, caught by the extended gate); a region reshaping command (not built: the brief asks for draw, name, reveal and fog only).
Affected specs: `03` §1, `03` §2, `03` §7, `04` §2, `04` §3, `04` §4, `04` §8, `04` §13, `07` §5, `10` §3, `02` §5, `08` §11

## D-145 (2026-09-30) — Where the table tools depart from the 2026-09-30 design, and why
Type: implementation
Decision: Added where the boards draw nothing: a revealed fog region stays on the DM's map as a faint dotted outline with a '· revealed' tag, so it can be found and fogged again; deleting a fog region asks first, as every other deletion does (specs/03-domain-model.md §7); the fog tool draws by keys as well as by the pointer, and pinging takes Enter at the centre of the view, so both work without a pointer; the ping tool is offered on the live scene only, with the reason on its disabled button, since a ping shows on no TV otherwise. Drawn differently: Unconscious and Dead as the brief suggests (desaturated with a 'z' badge; desaturated, darkened, an ✕ badge and the label struck through); condition chips carry a check mark besides their pressed colour; the bloodied ring pulses on the TV only and stands still under reduced motion, as the ping's rings do.
Why: The design is a non-authoritative input (D-003, D-139); where it is silent a flow still needs a place, and each tool has to work from the keyboard and never by colour alone (specs/08-ux-journeys.md §6).
Alternatives: Hiding revealed regions from the DM's map (rejected: the DM could not find one to fog it again but through the list); a pointer-only fog tool (rejected: no keyboard path).
Affected specs: `08` §11, `04` §12, `04` §13

## D-146 (2026-10-01) — Animations run a few times and stop: the idle glow, the live dot, the bloodied ring
Type: implementation
Decision: The idle screen's ember glow breathes three times (15 s) and then holds still; the header's LIVE dot pulses five times and then stays lit; the TV's bloodied ring pulses three times from when the marker is drawn and then stays lit. No stylesheet runs an animation for ever, which a test in client/src/ui/tokens.test.ts checks.
Why: Owner's choice on 2026-10-01 after PR #26's Windows WebKit e2e job ran its journeys about three times slower than before the redesign: Playwright's Windows WebKit paints without accelerated compositing, so an endless animation repaints the page every frame, and the idle TV page stays open through the Prepare journey. An idle TV left on for hours repainting a large gradient costs the TV too. The cause could not be reproduced locally (WebKit lacks its system libraries here); the CI run on this change is the test of it.
Alternatives: Running the matrix browsers with reduced motion in the e2e suite (rejected: tests the functions and hides the animation's cost); installing WebKit's libraries locally to measure first (not taken: the owner chose to fix and read CI).
Affected specs: `08` §4, `08` §11

## D-147 (2026-10-01) — A server-wide PIN pause refused with its own code, `pin_paused`, naming the server PC and the wait in hours past two hours (REL-03, G-042)
Type: implementation
Decision: Contract (`shared/src/errors.ts`): the error code `pin_paused` (429, with `Retry-After`) beside `locked_out`. Server (`server/src/auth/lockout.ts`, `server/src/http/auth.ts`): a refused attempt carries its reason; while the server-wide pause of Q-097 or Q-098 is in force for the address, PIN entry and the PIN change answer `pin_paused`, whatever the address's own lockout, with the longer of the two waits as before; otherwise `locked_out`. Client (`client/src/dm/SignIn.tsx`): `pinRefusalText` shows, for `pin_paused`, that PIN entry is paused on every device except the server PC after many wrong PINs on this network, not necessarily from this device, with `http://localhost:<the port the browser used>/dm` and the wait; every wait, the lockout's too, is in seconds below two minutes, minutes up to two hours, and whole hours, rounded up, past two hours (640 minutes reads as 11 hours). Sign-in and the Settings dialog's PIN change use it. README: the pause paragraph says what the refusal shows. Tests: `server/src/auth/auth-units.test.ts` (the reason of each refusal; a lock longer than the pause answers the pause, and the lock alone after it), `server/src/http/auth.test.ts` (every refusal during a pause is `pin_paused` with its Retry-After, a PIN change during a pause too, the per-address lockout stays `locked_out`), `client/src/dm/DmView.test.tsx` (the pause's text, hours, the server PC's address with and without a port), `client/src/dm/SettingsDialog.test.tsx` (the PIN change refused by a pause).
Why: G-042: a DM locked out by a guest's guesses read 'Too many wrong PINs', which blames their own device and hides the one remedy, the server PC by `localhost`, that the README gives; a doubled pause read in hundreds of minutes. A distinct code lets the screen say what happened without the client guessing from the wait. When the address is also locked, the pause is named because the server PC is the way in either way and the pause is what other devices caused. The code tells a LAN client only that the server-wide budget is spent, which the README already publishes and the Retry-After already implied; it names no address and no count.
Alternatives: A recorded decision accepting the README as the only explanation (the gap's other closing condition; not taken: the screen is where a locked-out DM looks); one code with a `reason` field in the envelope (rejected: the envelope is strict and shared by every route, and a code is what the client already maps to the catalogue); naming the pause only when it outlasts the address's lock (rejected: the server PC remedy and the cause hold whichever wait is longer); the server PC's LAN address in the message (rejected: the pause spares loopback only, so the LAN address would be refused too).
Affected specs: `07` §6, `08` §6

## D-148 (2026-10-01) — The DM view fits a laptop's 1,280 × 620 window: the tool rail stops above the grid status and scrolls, and Settings lays out in two columns (REL-03, G-041)
Type: implementation
Decision: Checked in a real browser at 1,280 × 620 CSS pixels and 1.5 device pixels per pixel (a 1080p laptop at Windows' 150% scaling, after the browser's toolbars and the taskbar), since the redesign (UIX-01) changed the layout G-041 was written against. Preparing, calibrating (panel, canvas and magnifier) and placing a token already fitted with the page unscrolled. Two things did not, and are fixed: (1) with the scene's setup open, the canvas is about 280 px tall and the canvas's tool rail (seven tools) ran below it, over the grid status and the shortcut bar, and scrolled the page; the rail (`client/src/canvas/canvas.css`) now ends above the grid status (`max-height`) and scrolls inside itself with no scrollbar drawn, its tools keeping their size; every tool also has its key and Add token is in the side panel. (2) The Settings dialog's PIN change lay below the dialog's fold; the dialog (`client/src/dm/SettingsDialog.tsx`, `client/src/dm/dm.css`) is up to 56 rem wide with the table's settings and the PIN change in two columns set apart by a rule, one above the other at 900 px wide and below, and its rows of buttons closer; `Dialog` takes a class for it. Test: `e2e/tests/window.spec.ts` at that size: prep mode, setup open, calibrating, placing a token by Add token and a click, and the Settings dialog before and after saving and changing the PIN, asserting the page never scrolls, the canvas (and the magnifier) lie whole on screen, the rail ends above the grid status and within the canvas, and the dialog is on screen with nothing scrolled inside it and its Save settings, Change PIN and Close reachable. It fails without either fix (checked by reverting each).
Why: G-041's closing condition is this end-to-end check at about 1,280 × 620; the owner's laptop is the second confirmation, in the REL-03 run sheet. Scrolling the rail inside the canvas keeps every tool the same size and reachable, where shrinking the tools would still not fit seven in 220 px. Two columns fit the dialog in 620 px with room for a status line, and read as the two sections D-125 already made.
Alternatives: Smaller rail tools below a height (rejected: still overflows with the setup open, and 44 px targets were a D-140 choice); wrapping the rail into a second column (not taken: column-wrapping flex boxes size their width wrongly in some engines); the setup panel collapsing the canvas's tools (rejected: hides tools); a recorded decision naming 1,280 × 720 as the narrowest window (offered in D-135 and not chosen by the owner); letting the dialog scroll (rejected: the gap is that it needs scrolling).
Affected specs: `08` §7, `08` §8

## D-149 (2026-10-01) — The owner's TV run: a run sheet, and the large-scene fixture built on a running server (REL-03)
Type: implementation
Decision: `docs/acceptance/rel-03-owner-run.md` is the owner's run sheet for REL-03: the steps on the Windows PC, the TV, the phone and the laptop, and a Record line for each fact the REL-03 acceptance and G-002, G-031, G-040 and G-041 need (TV model and webOS version; the connect panel's prominent address and its adapter; Firefox's first-load times; each TV step's result; the display size that worked; the laptop window's size and what fitted). `node e2e/fixtures/large-scene.ts --seed <server URL>` builds the fixture of `10` §3 (the 10,000 × 7,000 px map, 50 tokens, a map-less scene) in a new campaign 'TV run <local date and time>' on a running server through its REST API, reusing `seedLargeScene`, signing in with the PIN from `EMBERGLASS_PIN` or typed at a prompt, never printing it, and signing out after; the folder mode is unchanged. Tried against a fresh server on Linux: 9 s, and the PIN nowhere in the server's log.
Why: REL-03 is the owner's run and says the steps and what to record are written for the owner first. Placing 50 tokens by hand from the folder's images would take longer than the rest of the run and would not be the fixture the end-to-end test uses.
Alternatives: A Playwright project that drives the TV (rejected: the webOS browser is not automatable from here, and the run is evidence of the owner's hardware); loading the fixture's images by hand (the folder mode stays for that); a seeding route in the server (rejected: product code for a test fixture).
Affected specs: `10` §3, `10` §4, `05` §7, `13` §7

Decision: Refines D-148, which stays in force except as stated. Its alternatives called the rail's 44 px tools 'a D-140 choice'; D-140 does not record that size. The size came with the redesign's rail in UIX-01 (commit f118381, `client/src/canvas/canvas.css`) and no decision records it. The alternative stays rejected for its first reason: smaller tools still overflow the canvas with the scene's setup open.

## D-152 (2026-10-01) — PLAN.md's Now may be empty once every work package is done
Type: implementation
Decision: `scripts/check-docs.py` (`now-items`) accepts a `## Now` with no package item when every work package row in `TRACEABILITY.md` is `done`, and then requires the section to say so in one line ('None: ...'); otherwise the 1-3 items rule stands. PLAN.md's Now names the deferred TV run (G-043, D-151) as what is owed.
Why: With REL-03, the last package of `13` §7 and §10, done by D-151, no package can be a Now item, and the gate would fail for ever on a truthful plan.
Alternatives: Keeping REL-03 as a Now item while done (rejected: the gate rightly refuses a done item in Now); inventing a package for the TV run (rejected: packages come from the locked `13`, and G-043 already carries it).
