## D-153 (2026-10-01) — REL-03 review fixes: a pause's refusal that keeps Settings on a laptop screen, a rail that shows it scrolls, a seed command that hides the PIN, and the tests the review found missing
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-147, D-148, D-149 and D-152, which stay in force except as stated. Review of REL-03 (prompt 2, four passes, 2026-10-01: no critical or high finding; five medium, the rest low). (U-M1) The PIN change says a pause shorter ('PIN changes are paused on every device except the server PC. Try again in {wait}, or on the server PC open {address}'), and a refusal takes the intro paragraph's place, so the Settings dialog keeps every control on screen at 1,280 × 620 with the longest refusal; sign-in keeps the full message. (U-L5) The server PC's address ends both messages, so it is copied without punctuation; it stays plain text in the catalogue string. (U-L4) Every wait goes through `waitText`, the lockout's too ('Too many wrong PINs. Try again in {wait}.'), and one second reads '1 second'. Waits just over two hours still read '3 hours' (rounded up, D-147), by the owner's acceptance of the recommendation. (U-M2) The canvas's tool rail fades at an edge while tools are hidden beyond it, from its scroll position and size (`data-more-above`, `data-more-below`); Tab still scrolls each tool into view. (U-L6) The Settings dialog is never wider than the window less 2 rem, and one column from 940 px wide down. (S-L1, S-L2, S-L3) `large-scene.ts --seed` reads the PIN at a prompt without showing it (raw terminal; from a pipe, the first line), signs out whenever it signed in, warns when the server is not this PC's loopback, and reports a refusal in one line with exit 1. (C-M1, U-L7) The run sheet names what the seed command prints, confirms D-148 in its step 5 instead of the closed G-041, and hands back to G-043. (C-L3) The Settings stylesheet comment describes two columns. (C-L4) D-152's line is `- None:`, with its dash, as `check-docs.py` requires. (C-L6) TRACEABILITY cites D-147 to D-153. Tests: `server/src/http/auth.test.ts` (every refusal in the 40 parallel guesses is `pin_paused` with Retry-After 600; a lock longer than the pause answers the pause with the lock's 960 s, then `locked_out` 360 s; the server PC changes the PIN during a pause), `client/src/dm/DmView.test.tsx` ('1 second', `lockedOutText` at 7,200 and 7,201 s, `locked_out` without Retry-After, the address last), `client/src/dm/SettingsDialog.test.tsx` (the short pause message, the intro replaced), `e2e/tests/window.spec.ts` (the rail overflowing with its lower edge marked, Redo brought into view by focus and uncovered, the upper edge then marked; the dialog fitting with a pause refusal and Change PIN, Sign out and Close reachable; the PIN signing in after the change; the test's asset deleted), `scripts/check-docs-plan.test.mjs` (the now-items rule: an empty Now accepted only with every package done and a `- None:` line; a done Now item refused). The dialog check failed with the U-M1 fix reverted (checked). (U-L3) The rail's toolbar role without arrow-key navigation predates REL-03 and is G-044.
Why: Critical and high findings block merge and there were none; the owner chose to fix every medium and low finding but the toolbar's arrow keys, which belong to UIX-01's rail and are recorded as a gap.
Alternatives: A monospace element for the address (not taken: the catalogue holds plain strings, and ending the sentence with it answers the copying problem); a thin visible scrollbar on the rail (not taken: the fade marks the hidden edge without widening the rail); refusing a non-loopback server in the seed command (not taken: a LAN address of the server PC is legitimate, and the warning says what it costs); starting the hours wording at three hours (offered, not chosen).
Affected specs: `07` §6, `08` §6, `08` §7, `08` §8, `10` §3

## D-154 (2026-10-01) — TBL-04: painted fog replaces fog regions, the region table dropped
Type: adr
Decision: Q-101, answered by the owner: the fog regions of TBL-03 (D-144) are replaced by one painted fog mask per scene. The DM paints fog with a round brush and reveals it with an eraser, the radius on a slider from a quarter square to five; Fog all and Clear all cover the map. The mask is kept in grid units, in cells of a quarter square each way, on the scene (`scene.fog`, never part of the Scene contract, like `token_numbers`). The Region entity, the commands region.add, region.rename, region.setHidden and region.delete, the events region.added, region.updated and region.removed, the region routes and the region list leave; fog.paint and fog.fill replace the commands, each one undoable step on the live scene, and fog.updated carries the whole mask to both rooms. The players' rule is unchanged but for what it reads: a token is seen when not hidden and its centre is under no fogged cell, a centre on a cell's edge counting as inside; the server filters snapshots, events, numbering and image files by it as before, and the fog stays a mask the player view draws. Destructive migration plan (`09` §2, `14` §8): migration 0006 adds `scene.fog` (default no fog) and drops the `region` table in the same step; the regions already drawn are not converted, by the owner's choice, and the runner's dated backup of the database before migrating keeps them recoverable by hand. Painting in preparation goes over REST without undo, as every other preparation edit. A new package, TBL-04 in `13` §10, builds it; TBL-03 stays done as the record of what was built before.
Why: The owner tested the fog regions and asked for a paintbrush and an eraser instead (Q-101); a single mask is the simplest model that answers it and keeps the hidden-information rule in one place. Dropping the regions rather than converting them is the owner's choice: the data drawn so far is test data, and converting polygons needs code a SQL migration cannot hold.
Alternatives: Keeping the regions beside a brush (rejected by the owner: two fog models); named painted regions toggled whole (rejected by the owner: erasing would have to pick a region, overlaps need a rule); vector strokes replayed in order (rejected: storage grows with every stroke and the server replays them all to judge a token); whole-square cells (rejected by the owner: blocky edges); converting the fogged regions into the mask at start-up (recommended, not chosen by the owner); preset brush sizes (recommended, the owner chose a slider).
Affected specs: `01` §9, `02` §5, `03` §1, `03` §2, `03` §7, `04` §2, `04` §3, `04` §4, `04` §8, `04` §13, `07` §5, `08` §11, `10` §3, `13` §10
Owner approval: granted 2026-10-01

## D-155 (2026-10-01) — TBL-04: how the painted fog is stored, painted, filtered, projected and drawn
Type: implementation
Decision: Supersedes D-144, and the fog parts of D-145 (the revealed region's dotted outline, asking before deleting a region, drawing by keys), as D-154 decided. Mask (shared/src/fog.ts): cells of a quarter square each way in grid units, kept as rows of fogged [start, end) runs, sorted, apart and merged, no empty row, so equal fog is equal JSON; at most 2000 squares each way. A stroke is a mode (paint or erase), a radius from 0.25 to 5 in quarter steps and 1 to 1000 points in decimal grid units; it covers each cell whose centre lies within the radius of its polyline, worked out per row in closed form (a capsule's slice is one interval) and clipped to the map: a map's whole image by the grid's size and offsets (the size read from the columns before calibration), a map-less scene's columns and rows. Fog all fills that extent, Clear all takes every cell off. The client previews a stroke with the same function. Players' rule (seenByPlayers): not hidden and the token's centre under no fogged cell, a centre on a cell's edge judged by every cell it touches, so the rule errs toward hiding. Storage: migration 0006 adds scene.fog (JSON array, default '[]') and drops region; scene.fog is internal like token_numbers, outside the Scene contract, read by snapshots and GET /api/scenes/:id/fog; duplication copies it. Writes (server/src/db/fog.ts writeFog): a stroke, a fill or a restore in one transaction, with the token sight before and after and the numbering pass of D-144 unchanged; a write that leaves the mask equal is answered unchanged, sends nothing and records no undo step. Commands fog.paint {scene_id, stroke} and fog.fill {scene_id, fogged}, refused as scene_not_live off the live scene; undo and redo put the whole mask back (fog.restore, server-only). Events: fog.updated with the whole mask to both rooms, then token.removed for each token covered and token.added for each shown in ascending rank, as D-144 ordered them. REST in preparation: POST /api/scenes/:id/fog with {stroke} or {fill}, answering the mask; 409 scene_live on the live scene. Drawing: on the TV the mask is one path of rectangles (runs carried down the rows that repeat them) in #0A0806 with an 18 px soft shadow, its layer blurred 4 px by CSS so the cells' steps read as a round brush; in the DM view a dark tint under a blue hatch. The brush (F, or the rail's Fog brush): a drag paints or erases, a point every quarter radius, sent on release (a stroke past 1000 points is sent and continued); a right or middle drag pans; Enter paints at the view's centre, E switches, [ and ] step the radius, Escape drops the stroke under way, then leaves. A stroke sent stays drawn until the fog it made arrives, or is taken off when refused. The brush bar above the canvas holds Paint and Erase, a labelled range slider, Fog all and Clear all (each asked first; in preparation the dialog says there is no undo) and Done. The right panel's FOG group says how many tokens under the fog players cannot see, with Paint fog; a token's status says it is in the fog.
Why: D-154 and Q-101 as answered by the owner. Run-length rows keep a typical painted map to a few KB, a filled map to one run a row, and a point test to a binary search; a capsule per segment is the exact shape of a round brush moved along a line; one rule in shared code is still what the server filters by and the DM view counts by, so the hidden-information gate stays meaningful.
Alternatives: A bitmap per scene (rejected: its size follows the map, not the fog, and a 2000-square map would be megabytes); storing strokes and replaying them (rejected in D-154); sending only the cells a stroke changed (rejected: a whole mask keeps every view exact without ordering rules, and stays small); a canvas raster drawn at one pixel per cell and scaled (rejected: a large map's canvas would be too big); erasing with the same stroke leaving no slivers (not possible when the points differ; the DM erases with a wider brush).
Affected specs: `03` §1, `03` §7, `04` §2, `04` §3, `04` §4, `04` §8, `04` §13, `07` §5, `08` §11, `10` §3

## D-156 (2026-10-01) — The token popover opens on a click and closes on a drag or a pan (Q-102)
Type: spec-amendment
Decision: Q-102, answered A by the owner: the selected token's popover is open only after a click on the token, a choice of its row in the list, or its placement or duplication; it no longer follows the selection. A press on a token that the pointer leaves by 4 px or more on either axis (the larger of the two distances, as Konva's `dragDistance` measures it) is a drag: no drag starts below it, and the drag's start closes the popover, which stays closed after the drop while the token stays selected; a release within it is a click and opens the popover. Pressing another token closes the open popover before selecting it, so a popover never carries over to the token pressed. A pan, whether by Space and a drag or by dragging the empty map, closes it at its start, and it stays closed when the pan ends; Escape and a click on empty map still let the token go and the popover with it. `08` §11's popover bullet is amended to say so. The threshold is `DRAG_THRESHOLD_PX` and `movedPast` in `client/src/canvas/tokens.ts`; the open state is the scene panel's, beside the selection.
Why: The owner reported that the popover blocks the map while a token is dragged and specified the behaviour; 4 px is the owner's figure and the usual click slop for a mouse or a pen, small enough that a deliberate drag is never read as a click.
Alternatives: Hiding the popover during the drag and showing it again at the drop (rejected: the owner wants it closed after the drop); a time threshold instead of a distance (rejected: a slow click is still a click); judging the distance by Euclidean length (rejected: Konva starts a drag by the larger axis distance, and the two rules must agree so that a press never both drags and opens).
Affected specs: `08` §11

## D-157 (2026-10-01) — TBL-05: eighteen condition markers stored as objects, with SRD 5.1 rule text and bundled icons
Type: adr
Decision: Q-103, answered A by the owner: a token's markers are eighteen conditions. Pinned in the popover, in this order: Bloodied, Unconscious, Dead, Concentrating, Prone, Poisoned. Behind a More… button, a searchable alphabetical list: Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Incapacitated, Invisible, Paralyzed, Petrified, Restrained, Stunned. Active ones from the list show as selected chips too. `markers` becomes an array of objects, `{ id }`, or `{ id: 'exhaustion', level }` with level 1 to 6, each id at most once, kept in the order applied. `id` leaves room for a later `custom:<uuid>`, which is not built. The list is a data file, `shared/src/conditions.json` (id, label, pinned, icon, whether the text is the SRD's, rule text, implied conditions). It carries the SRD 5.1 text of the fifteen SRD conditions (CC BY 4.0) and a short original line for Bloodied, Dead and Concentrating. Implied conditions are named in the hover text and never applied. Icons are game-icons.net SVG paths (CC BY 3.0), bundled, credited with the SRD in an About & credits dialog in the DM view and in CREDITS.md. Drawing on both views: at most three badges, then +N, by priority Dead, Unconscious, Bloodied, then the rest in the order applied. Dead greys the token and strikes its label through. Bloodied keeps its ring and pulse. Concentrating keeps its dotted ring. Invisible draws the token semi-transparent on both views, players included, and is not the hide toggle. A hidden token's markers still never reach players. Data migration plan (`09` §2, `14` §8): migration 0007 rewrites every stored `token.markers` array of names into the same names as objects, in the same order. Lossless: the four old names are all among the eighteen. Rollback is the reverse map of each object's id; no data is dropped. The commands are unchanged: `token.setMarkers` carries the whole array and undo restores the previous one.
Why: The owner asked for this set and approved overriding the register entry that allowed four (Q-099) and the Phase 2 placement of other conditions and SRD content (`01` §4, §8). Objects carry Exhaustion's level and leave room for custom markers without another shape change. The rule text is read, never applied, so the MVP still has no game rules in code.
Alternatives: Keeping string ids with a separate exhaustion field (rejected: the owner gave the object shape, and a second field multiplies the contract); hardcoding the list in components (rejected by the owner); drawing the icons by hand (rejected: the owner named game-icons.net, and hand-drawn icons at 14 px would be weaker); applying implied conditions (rejected by the owner).
Affected specs: `01` §4, §8, §9; `03` §1; `04` §2, §4; `08` §11; `10` §3; `11`; `12`; `13` §10; AGENTS.md scope red line
Owner approval: granted 2026-10-01

## D-158 (2026-10-01) — TBL-05: how the eighteen markers are listed, validated, drawn and credited
Type: implementation
Decision: Implements D-157. List: `shared/src/conditions.json` holds, per condition, id, label, pinned, icon (game-icons.net source, author and the SVG path on a 512-unit square), srd, rule (one line per paragraph or bullet) and implies; `levels: 6` on Exhaustion. The SRD text of the fifteen SRD conditions was taken from the 2014 SRD 5.1 conditions in the 5e-bits/5e-database data set (MIT), not retyped. Labels and rule text are data, not catalogue strings, as the owner asked; the sentences around them ("Also means: … (not applied).", "{label} (level {level})") are the catalogue's. Icons: Bloodied lorc/drop, Unconscious delapouite/night-sleep, Dead sbed/death-skull, Concentrating lorc/concentration-orb, Prone sbed/falling, Poisoned lorc/poison-bottle, Blinded delapouite/blindfold, Charmed lorc/charm, Deafened skoll/hearing-disabled, Exhaustion lorc/oppression, Frightened lorc/screaming, Grappled lorc/grab, Incapacitated skoll/breaking-chain, Invisible delapouite/invisible, Paralyzed sbed/electric, Petrified lorc/stone-block, Restrained lorc/fishing-net, Stunned delapouite/knocked-out-stars; each checked legible at 14 px. Contract (`shared/src/conditions.ts`): MarkerSchema is `{ id }` of a non-Exhaustion id, or `{ id: 'exhaustion', level }` with an integer level 1–6, strict; MarkersSchema an array of at most 18, unique items; `normaliseMarkers` keeps each id's first marker, in the order sent, and drops anything else, so the server stores each condition once in the order applied; `sameMarkers` compares order and level, so the same set again tells nobody. Popover: six pinned chips, then a pressed chip for each other condition carried, then More…, a search combobox over the twelve others in alphabetical order (arrows, Enter, Escape; keys typed there never reach the map); Exhaustion turned on starts at 1 and its chip gains a − level + stepper, − at 1 turning it off; each chip's and option's title is its rule text with the implied conditions named. Canvas: badges 14 px across on the DM's screen, in a row along the token's top edge a pixel apart, so none overlaps another or the label however small the token is drawn; Dead, Unconscious, Bloodied first, then the order applied, three at most and a +N badge last; Exhaustion's badge carries its level on a small disc; the hidden badge moves to the token's right edge, clear of the row; Invisible draws the token's body at 45% opacity on both views; Bloodied's ring and pulse and Concentrating's dotted ring stay. About & credits: an info button in the header opens a dialog with the AGPL notice, the icons' authors read from the list and the SRD 5.1 attribution, with no link outside the LAN; CREDITS.md says the same.
Why: One data file is the single source the owner asked for, and both server and client read it from `shared`. Normalising rather than refusing a duplicate keeps the old fixed-order tolerance of TBL-02. A row of badges, unlike the earlier arc on the token's left, stays readable on the TV, where a 30-square map draws a token about 36 px wide and three badges would otherwise overlap one another and the label.
Alternatives: An arc or a column of badges on the left (rejected: they overlapped on small tokens in the screenshots); condition labels in the message catalogue (rejected: the owner asked for them in the data file); a native select for More… (rejected: no search, and no icons in its options); refusing a duplicate id with validation_failed (kept for exact duplicates through uniqueItems; a same id with another level is normalised).
Affected specs: `01` §9, `03` §1, `08` §11

## D-159 (2026-10-01) — TBL-05 review fixes: a condition named twice refused, migration 0007 safe to run again, the removed Exhaustion named at its level
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-158, which stays in force except as stated. Review of TBL-05 (prompt 2, security and isolation, 2026-10-01): no critical or high finding, one medium, three low. (M1) Two Exhaustion markers at different levels passed the schema, whose uniqueItems compares whole objects, and one was silently dropped; the REST PATCH and `token.setMarkers` now refuse any set that names a condition twice with validation_failed (`repeatsCondition` in shared), so D-158's 'a same id with another level is normalised' no longer holds; tests in `server/src/ws/markers.test.ts`, `server/src/http/tokens.test.ts` and `shared/src/conditions.test.ts`. (L2) Migration 0007 rewrites only arrays that still hold names, so running it again over converted rows changes nothing; `server/src/db/schema.test.ts` runs it twice. (L3) Removing Exhaustion announces the level it had, not level 1. (L4) No-op detection confirmed sound; no change. Also confirmed with no finding: the players' projection unchanged and strict, every REST and live path validated, nothing outside the LAN, no markup from the data, and no trademark in UI text (`CREDITS.md`'s SRD attribution URL is the licence's own wording, in a repository document).
Why: A body the server answers ok but stores differently breaks the contract that each condition appears once; a migration that empties data on a second run is a hazard on restore.
Alternatives: Restructuring MarkersSchema to make ids unique in JSON Schema (rejected: no portable keyword compares one property across items); normalising the duplicate silently (rejected by the review).
Affected specs: `03` §1

## D-160 (2026-10-01) — TBL-06: an initiative tracker, one encounter per scene stored in SQLite, with a DM panel and a TV strip
Type: adr
Decision: Q-104, Q-105 and Q-106, each answered A by the owner. A scene holds at most one encounter, a row of a new `encounter` table (`03` §1): `scene_id` (unique, deleted with the scene), `active`, `round` from 1, `current_index`, `enemies_seen` and `entries`, a JSON array in turn order. An entry is `{ id, kind: 'pc', token_id, initiative }` or the one `{ id, kind: 'dm', initiative }`, the initiative an integer or null; `kind` leaves room for a later `monster` entry, which is not built. The Enemies entry's members are computed from the scene's tokens whenever needed: monster and npc tokens players can see (`04` §4) that do not carry Dead. Eight live commands, each undoable and naming the live scene (`04` §2, §14): `encounter.start`, `encounter.end`, `encounter.reorder`, `encounter.setInitiative`, `encounter.next`, `encounter.previous`, `encounter.addEntry`, `encounter.removeEntry`; one event, `encounter.updated`, carrying the whole row to the DM and, to players, only a projection: the round, the entries of player characters they can see and the Enemies entry, which has the turn and which is next; no initiative number, member, count or `enemies_seen`, so a player view learns nothing it could not see on the map (`04` §4). Both snapshots carry the encounter. Deleting a token removes its entry in the same transaction, and undoing the delete puts the entry back. No dice anywhere. Data migration plan (`09` §2, `14` §8): migration 0008 creates the `encounter` table and its index; additive, nothing existing is read or rewritten. Rollback is dropping the table, losing only encounters.
Why: The owner asked for the tracker in detail and approved overriding its Phase 2 placement (`01` §4). Stored per scene, combat survives a restart and a switch to another scene and back, as the owner's model of one encounter per scene implies; a table of its own keeps the scene row unchanged. Computing the members rather than storing them is what the owner asked, and keeps the enemies' list right whatever command reveals, hides, fogs or kills a token. A projection built on the server keeps the hidden-information rule: every field players receive follows from what they already see.
Alternatives: Keeping the encounter in memory like the camera and the ruler (rejected by the owner, Q-105); a JSON column on `scene` (rejected: it would widen every scene read and the duplication path); one entry per monster (rejected by the owner, left for later through `kind`); always skipping an empty Enemies entry, or always stopping on it (rejected by the owner, Q-106); sending players the members and filtering them in the client (rejected: filtering happens on the server, `04` §4).
Affected specs: `01` §4, §9; `03` §1, §2, §7; `04` §2, §3, §4, §8, §14; `08` §12; `10` §3; `11`; `12`; `13` §10; AGENTS.md scope and play red lines
Owner approval: granted 2026-10-01

## D-161 (2026-10-01) — TBL-06: how the encounter is stored, decided, projected for players and shown
Type: implementation
Decision: Implements D-160. Rules: `shared/src/encounter.ts` holds the contract (EncounterSchema, PlayerEncounterSchema, initiative an integer from -99 to 999 or null, at most 200 entries) and every rule, pure: `membersOf` (monster and npc tokens `seenByPlayers`, without Dead), `takesTurn` (a player character's entry while players see its token, whatever its markers; the Enemies entry while it has members or once `enemies_seen`), `stepTurn` (+1 past the last entry counts a round, -1 before the first takes one back and does nothing on round 1), `sortByInitiative` (stable: highest first, unnumbered below in their order, so a dragged tie stays), `followTurn` (the turn stays with its entry through any reorder), `startTokens` (player characters players see, in stacking order, the DM's list order), `changeOf` (what each command does, or why it is refused: `bad_request` for starting a running encounter, any other command with none running, adding a token that is not a visible player character or already entered, removing the Enemies entry; `order_mismatch` for a reorder that does not name every entry once; `not_found` for an unknown entry or token) and `playerEncounter` (the players' projection). The server applies `changeOf` inside one transaction with the scene's tokens and fog read in it (`server/src/db/encounters.ts`); the client's test server applies the same function. Storage: migration 0008, one `encounter` row per scene (unique index on `scene_id`, ON DELETE CASCADE), `entries` a JSON array checked by the contract on read; End combat keeps the row with `active` 0, no entries, round 1. Deleting a token drops its entry in `deleteToken`'s transaction, in preparation and live alike; the live `token.delete` answers `token.removed` then `encounter.changed`, and its inverse carries the encounter before, which the undo restores with the token. Every encounter command's inverse is `encounter.restore`, the whole encounter before (none for a start on a scene that had none), not on the wire. `enemies_seen` is set by the command itself and, after any other live command that changed something, by `noteEnemies`, which tells the DM room and is no step of the history. Events: the DM room hears `encounter.updated` with the stored encounter from the projection of each `encounter.changed`; players hear `encounter.updated` with `playerEncounter` only when it differs from what it was before the command (`tellEncounter` in `server/src/ws/live.ts`, after the command's own events), so a token's reveal, fog or Dead that changes who is next reaches them and a hidden token's change does not; REST changes reach them in the snapshot diff of `refresh`. Both snapshots carry it. DM view: a third right-panel tab, Initiative, named with the round while combat runs (`Initiative · R3`); rows with a grip, the token's thumbnail, the name, Now and Next tags, the conditions as icons, a number field committed on Enter or blur (empty clears it, anything else not a whole number in bounds goes back), Move up and Move down, and Remove; native drag and drop as the scene list; the Enemies row opening on its turn to its members, each selecting and centring its token; the prompt on the Enemies turn with no members, whose End combat ends at once, since the prompt is the question, and whose Continue sends Next; an offer per visible player character without an entry, dismissed per browser; End combat from the header asked in a dialog. Enter and Shift+Enter pass the turn from anywhere in the DM view but a field, a button, a link, a tab, a menu item or an open dialog. The map rings the turn's player character with a wide ember ring and glow and each member on the Enemies turn with a thin faint ring, drawn whatever tool is on; the DM's camera centres on the player character whose turn comes up. TV: `InitiativeStrip`, fixed at the top centre, about 9% of the screen's height at 1080 lines, clamp-sized by the viewport's height; a portrait from the token's display image (what players may already fetch), the name, the Enemies card with a crossed-blades icon, NOW and NEXT tags, the round at the left; it fades in on its own, 400 ms like the scene fade, and out over 400 ms when combat ends; none with reduced motion. The client's checks of an arriving encounter are written out, as `isFogMask` is, so TypeBox's format checks do not bring the URL they name into the bundle (`check-external-urls`).
Why: One pure rule set used by the server and the client's test server keeps the DM view's tests honest against the server's behaviour. Comparing the players' projection around every command, rather than deriving it from the encounter's own events, is what makes the strip follow a reveal or a death without an encounter command, and send nothing when only a hidden token changed: the hidden-information gate's recordings stay identical with and without hidden tokens. The prompt's own End combat does not ask again: the prompt is the confirmation the owner described.
Alternatives: Deriving members on the client from players' tokens (rejected: players must not learn which tokens are enemies, `04` §4); a players' event per encounter command (rejected: a hidden token's change would then be countable by the version counter); storing `current_entry_id` instead of `current_index` (rejected: the owner gave `current_index`; the turn follows its entry by id when the order changes); a confirmation after the prompt's End combat (rejected: a second question for the same answer).
Affected specs: `03` §1, `04` §4, `04` §14, `08` §12

## D-162 (2026-10-01) — TBL-06: review fixes (the turn passed on removal, no Next named for an unseen turn, focus and strip markup)
Type: implementation
Decision: Refines D-161 after the security and isolation review (prompt 2), which found no critical or high issue. Medium, fixed: removing or deleting the entry whose turn it is passes the turn as Next would, to the next entry that takes it and counting a round past the last, never to an Enemies entry that never had a member or a player character players cannot see (`withoutEntry` takes the turn view). Medium, fixed: while the turn is a player character players cannot see, the players' projection names no Next either, so numbering, moving or removing that unseen entry changes nothing they receive; tested in `server/src/ws/encounter.test.ts` and `shared/src/encounter.test.ts`. Low, fixed: after Move up or Move down focus stays on the row moved, on whichever of the two buttons still works; a number not stored (offline, refused) goes back to the stored one, and the blur after Enter does not send it twice; the strip's round sits outside its list, so the list holds only its cards. Low, kept and recorded here: `enemies_seen` stays set when the reveal that set it is undone, since it says the encounter has had an enemy, not that one is up, and the end-combat prompt's Continue passes it; two DM browsers pressing Next at once pass two turns, last write wins as for every live command (`04` §2), and Previous takes one back.
Why: The owner's rule passes over an empty Enemies entry that never had a member (Q-106), whatever moved the turn there; and what players receive must follow from what they see (`04` §4), which an unseen turn's Next did not.
Alternatives: Keeping the turn's index after a removal (rejected: it could land on an entry that must be passed over); an expected-turn guard on Next and Previous (rejected for now: no other live command carries one, and Previous undoes a double press); undoing `enemies_seen` with the reveal (rejected: the flag records the encounter's history, not a token's state).
Affected specs: `04` §4, `04` §14, `08` §12

## D-163 (2026-10-01) — TBL-06: test data directories start from a copy of a database migrated once per test process
Type: implementation
Decision: `createTestData` (`server/src/http/testing/app.ts`) migrates one template database per test process with the real migrations and copies it into each test's data directory, then runs `migrateDataDirectory` on the copy, which finds nothing to apply; as `src/db/schema.test.ts` already does for its own fixture. Locally the server suite drops from 165 s to 111 s with the same 773 tests.
Why: On PR #31 `test · windows` failed three times on setup hooks and tests timing out at 60 s in files TBL-06 does not touch (ruler, camera, live, tokens, assets), the job taking 12 and 16.5 minutes against 8 on `main`. Each test data directory was migrated afresh, one committed transaction per migration, about 0.3 s a directory here and far more on the Windows runner; migration 0008 and TBL-06's new live tests added to every one of hundreds of such setups. Removing the cost at its cause is what the CI flake rule asks, not a longer timeout.
Alternatives: Raising the hook and test timeouts (rejected: hides the cost); fewer Vitest workers on Windows (rejected: slower everywhere for the same work); sharing one harness across the encounter tests alone (rejected: removes a fraction of the cost and weakens isolation).
Affected specs: `10` §2

## D-164 (2026-10-02) — PKG: a Windows x64 package with its own Node runtime, a portable zip and an installer, built by CI on a version tag
Type: adr
Decision: Draft, on the recommendations of Q-107, Q-108 and Q-109, none answered yet. A new Phase 6 in `13` with three packages. PKG-01 Portable build: the server bundled by esbuild into one module with `better-sqlite3` and `sharp` left external, so the `@emberglass/shared` workspace link is not shipped; a staged folder `Emberglass/` holding the Node 24 LTS `node.exe` renamed `emberglass.exe` with its icon and description set, `app/server.mjs`, `app/migrations/`, `app/client/dist/` and `app/node_modules/` with only the production native modules and their win32-x64 binaries, installed by `npm ci --omit=dev` on a Windows runner and never cross-built; `LICENSE`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` (Node, libvips under LGPL-3.0, every bundled npm licence) and a README naming the exact source tag (AGPL-3.0 §6); a launcher that, when `/api/health` already answers on the port, only opens `http://localhost:3000/dm` in the default browser, and otherwise starts the server and then opens it, so that PIN setup from loopback (`07` §1) works on the first run; the console window stays open and shows the TV address and QR code (`08` §9), and closing it stops the server. `paths.ts` resolves the migrations and the client from the bundle's directory. A real version replaces `0.0.0` in every `package.json` and is shown in About & credits. The zip and its SHA-256 are built by a `release` workflow on a `v*` tag on `windows-latest` and attached to a GitHub Release, which is not a package registry. PKG-02 Installer: Inno Setup over the PKG-01 folder; per-machine under Program Files with one UAC prompt (Q-108 A); Start Menu and optional desktop shortcuts; an inbound firewall rule for `emberglass.exe` on the private profile only, removed on uninstall; upgrade in place over an older version, the start-up migration and its dated backup doing the rest (`09` §2); uninstall never deletes `%APPDATA%\Emberglass` (`09` §5); unsigned, with the SmartScreen steps and the checksums in the README and release notes (Q-109 A). PKG-03 Package gates: the Windows CI job installs the built installer silently into a clean folder on the runner, starts the packaged launcher with an empty temporary data directory, and runs the five journeys of `10` §5, the hidden-information suite and the offline run against it (`10` §3, `10` §6); uninstalls and checks the data directory is still there; and a manual run in Windows Sandbox with no Node installed is recorded in `docs/acceptance/`. Nothing in the server's behaviour, the contract, the schema or the data directory changes: same process, same port, same `EMBERGLASS_PORT` and `EMBERGLASS_DATA_DIR`. No auto-update and no update check (`02` §6). Installation from source (`09` §1) stays supported and documented. Linux and macOS packages are not built. Rollback: delete the release assets and the workflow; nothing else depends on them.
Why: The owner wants a DM who is not a developer to install Emberglass without Node, Git or a terminal (Q-107). The architecture already fits: one Node process, no database server, state in one per-user directory, migrations with a backup at start, so the package only has to carry the runtime. Building on a Windows runner is the only way to get the right native binaries of `better-sqlite3` and `sharp`. A browser rather than a desktop window keeps the DM view and the TV exactly what the acceptance suite already tests, and `02` §8 still holds: a Tauri or Electron wrapper later can reuse the PKG-01 folder.
Alternatives: Electron (rejected for now: about 150 MB more and a second shell to maintain, for a window the browser already gives); Tauri (rejected: the server is Node, so Node still ships as a sidecar and Rust is added); a Node single executable application (rejected: native `.node` addons cannot load from inside it and would be extracted to disk at start, fragile with `sharp`); a Windows service (rejected: the data directory is per user and the DM starts the server when they play); MSI through WiX (rejected for now: more ceremony than Inno Setup for the same result; Inno Setup is free and scriptable from CI); per-user install without a firewall rule (Q-108 B); signing (Q-109 B, deferred).
Affected specs: `01` §5, §8; `02` §8; `09` §1, §3, §4; `10` §4; `11`; `12`; `13` new §11 Phase 6; AGENTS.md architecture and commands
Owner approval: pending
